> A hand-curated, license-aware map of the best free cybersecurity tools — from pentesting and bug bounty to OSINT, threat intel, DFIR, DevSecOps and AI/LLM security. Free, open-source and free-tier entries all earn their place. 🌟
> **2026 edition** — 1435 tools, 14 realms, every entry link-verified 2026-11-06. 📅

🧭 **1435 curated tools** · 🏰 **14 themed realms** · 🗓️ **2026 edition** · 🆓 **free, open-source & free-tier** · 🔬 **license-verified** · 🔄 **actively maintained** · 🧊 **dormant classics → Legacy Vault** · 💯 **no paywalls, no SaaS wrappers**

**Legend** — ⚖️ `(GPL-…)` / `(AGPL-…)` = copyleft license · 🎁 `(free tier)` / `(freemium)` = free tier of a
commercial service · 🔒 `(free, proprietary)` = freeware, not open source · 🏠 `(maintainer's project)` =
the curator's own tool, included transparently.
- [PEASS-ng](https://github.com/peass-ng/PEASS-ng) - Windows and Linux privilege-escalation suites (winPEAS, linPEAS): thousands of checks over services, paths, credentials and scheduled tasks. Used by pentesters sweeping for escalation paths; the de-facto standard priv-escalation audit on every engagement. (GPL-2.0-or-later)
- [Evil-WinRM](https://github.com/Hackplayers/evil-winrm) - The ultimate WinRM shell: PS history, tab completion, pass-the-hash logins, LAPS download and file exchange over WinRM/SMB. Used by pentesters who hold credentials and want the fastest route to an interactive shell; the WinRM shell every AD course teaches. (LGPL-3.0)
- [Ligolo-ng](https://github.com/nicocha30/ligolo-ng) - Reverse socks5 tunneling for pivots: one TUN interface maps the agent's networks into your local routing table, no proxychains juggling. Used by red-teamers pivoting through internal ranges; the tunnel that made pivoting feel local. (GPL-3.0)
- [Rubeus](https://github.com/GhostPack/Rubeus) - Kerberos interaction toolkit: AS-REP roasting, kerberoasting, pass-the-key, delegation abuse and diamond-ticket crafting. Used by AD attackers working the Kerberos layer; the GhostPack Kerberos swiss-army knife. (BSD-3-Clause)
- [CUPP](https://github.com/Mebus/cupp) - Common User Passwords Profiler: generates targeted wordlists from a victim's biography — names, dates, hobbies, languages. Used by pentesters crafting personalized wordlists; the original target-specific wordlist generator. (GPL-3.0)
- [GoMapEnum](https://github.com/nodauf/GoMapEnum) - User enumeration and password spraying across SMB, Kerberos, NTLM and LDAP from a single Go binary. Used by red-teamers enumerating Windows users before a spray; the Go-native Windows enum-and-spray chain. (GPL-3.0)
- [Legba](https://github.com/evilsocket/legba) - Multiprotocol brute-forcer — SSH, FTP, SMTP, HTTP, MySQL, MSSQL, Redis and more — with one consistent CLI. Used by pentesters who want a single cracker for every protocol; evilsocket's answer to scattered brute-forcers. (GPL-3.0)
- [DonPAPI](https://github.com/login-securite/DonPAPI) - Remote DPAPI credential collection: Chrome cookies, Wi-Fi keys and custom DPAPI blobs gathered from many hosts without touching disk. Used by attackers chaining credentials across Windows estates; the remote DPAPI collector that opens every browser. (GPL-3.0)
- [Certify](https://github.com/GhostPack/Certify) - Active Directory Certificate Services abuse toolkit: finds vulnerable certificate templates and chains ESC1-ESC8 from misconfigured CAs to domain compromise. Used by AD attackers auditing certificate services; the GhostPack tool that made AD CS a standard attack class. (BSD-3-Clause)
- [airgeddon](https://github.com/v1s1t0r1sh3r3/airgeddon) - All-in-one wireless security suite: handshakes, evil-twin attacks, captive portals, WPS and pmkid workflows behind one interactive menu. Used by Wi-Fi auditors running the full wireless chain; the menu-driven Wi-Fi lab. (GPL-3.0)
- [SMBMap](https://github.com/ShawnDEvans/smbmap) - SMB enumeration and share mapping: lists drives, permissions and writable shares, then downloads across a whole subnet. Used by pentesters enumerating Windows shares; the share-mapping staple since 2015. (GPL-3.0)
- [Medusa](https://github.com/jmk-foofus/medusa) - Parallel login brute-forcer for many services with modular credential lists and host batching. Used by pentesters running credential attacks at scale; the speed-first login cracker of the THC-Hydra era. (GPL-2.0)
- [Penelope](https://github.com/brightio/penelope) - Automated shell handler: catches reverse shells, upgrades them to fully interactive TTYs and logs every session. Used by pentesters tired of stty-raw gymnastics; the shell upgrader that saves the first ten minutes of every engagement. (GPL-3.0)
- [Pacu](https://github.com/RhinoSecurityLabs/pacu) - AWS exploitation framework: enumerates an account's services and chains misconfigurations into privilege-escalation paths. Used by cloud attackers chaining AWS misconfigs; the RhinoSecurity toolkit that defined open AWS offense. (BSD-3-Clause)
- [nomore403](https://github.com/devploit/nomore403) - 403/401 bypass toolkit: path-normalization tricks, header fuzzing and method overrides that turn forbidden pages into accessible ones. Used by bug hunters probing access-control edges; the 403-bypass playbook codified. (MIT)
- [cvcemap](https://github.com/projectdiscovery/vulnx) - ProjectDiscovery's CVE-to-version mapping (repo: vulnx): fingerprint a component and get the live CVE list for the exact version in one command. Used by recon pipelines mapping component exposure; the projectdiscovery take on CVE mapping. (MIT)
- [tlsx](https://github.com/projectdiscovery/tlsx) - TLS-focused recon: cipher and certificate enumeration, SAN extraction and TLS-version probing with the projectdiscovery CLI ergonomics. Used by recon pipelines snapshotting TLS posture; the fast TLS recon companion. (MIT)
- [cdncheck](https://github.com/projectdiscovery/cdncheck) - Detects whether a host sits behind a CDN or known cloud provider before you scan it, from ProjectDiscovery's curated provider list. Used by recon pipelines avoiding wasted scans on protected edges; the suite's CDN gatekeeper. (MIT)
- [findomain](https://github.com/Findomain/Findomain) - Subdomain discovery through certificate transparency and multiple data sources with a database backend. Used by bug hunters building subdomain inventories; one of the fastest passive enumerators of its generation. (GPL-3.0)
- [knockpy](https://github.com/guelfoweb/knockpy) - Subdomain enumeration by wordlist and CT logs with wildcard detection built in. Used by pentesters running dictionary subdomain passes; the classic knock turned into a maintained Python tool. (GPL-3.0)
- [subcat](https://github.com/duty1g/subcat) - Subdomain discovery through search-engine and passive sources, built for pipeline use. Used by recon chains wanting passive subdomain sets; the lightweight passive-enum addition. (MIT)
- [graphw00f](https://github.com/dolevf/graphw00f) - GraphQL fingerprinter: detects the engine behind an endpoint — Apollo, Hasura, Yoga and the rest — from behavioral signatures. Used by pentesters preparing engine-specific GraphQL payloads; the fingerprinter that tells you which GraphQL you are attacking. (BSD-3-Clause)
- [gowitness](https://github.com/sensepost/gowitness) - Website screenshot recon: headless Chrome captures whole asset inventories and serves them in a browsable report. Used by bug hunters eyeballing thousands of hosts at once; the recon screenshot standard. (GPL-3.0)
- [FAVIHUNTER](https://github.com/eremit4/favihunter) - Favicon-based asset hunting: mmh3 favicon hashes match your targets' properties against public scan datasets. Used by attack-surface mappers looking for forgotten properties; the favicon-hash bridge for asset discovery. (MIT)
- [gitjacker](https://github.com/liamg/gitjacker) - Leaks .git directories from misconfigured webservers and reconstructs the repository history. Used by bug hunters checking for exposed source; the git-dump attack automated. (Unlicense)
- [InfoHound](https://github.com/Fundacio-i2CAT/InfoHound) - OSINT search-engine framework: crawls people, emails, domains and leaks into structured intelligence reports. Used by investigators building target dossiers; the i2CAT open intelligence engine. (AGPL-3.0)
- [Investigo](https://github.com/tdh8316/Investigo) - Username and email finder across social networks and forums with a clean CLI. Used by OSINT researchers tracing identities; a long-standing social presence mapper. (MIT)
- [EmailHarvester](https://github.com/maldevel/EmailHarvester) - Email enumeration via search engines and public sources. Used by OSINT collectors gathering corporate footprints; the maldevel classic that keeps earning its place. (GPL-3.0)
- [Instaloader](https://github.com/instaloader/instaloader) - Instagram archiving: photos, stories, captions and metadata downloaded rate-limit-aware. Used by investigators archiving social evidence; the Instagram archiver that respects both public data and platform limits. (MIT)
- [gallery-dl](https://github.com/mikf/gallery-dl) - Command-line downloader for image galleries and video collections from hundreds of sites, for authorized archiving and OSINT work. Used by archivers preserving online media; the gallery downloader that covers almost every platform. (GPL-2.0)
- [TestDisk](https://github.com/cgsecurity/testdisk) - Partition-table and file recovery: undeletes, fixes boot sectors and rescues lost partitions across most filesystems. Used by DFIR and ops teams rescuing broken disks; the cgsecurity recovery classic. (GPL-2.0)
- [androguard](https://github.com/androguard/androguard) - Reverse-engineering framework for Android: DEX/ODEX parsing, decompilation, static analysis and APK diffing in Python. Used by mobile analysts tearing APKs apart; the Android RE foundation. (Apache-2.0)
- [hurl](https://github.com/Orange-OpenSource/hurl) - HTTP requests in plain text: run and test endpoints with a human-readable format, assertions and CI-ready reports. Used by API testers scripting endpoint checks; the plain-text HTTP runner. (Apache-2.0)
- [chisel](https://github.com/jpillora/chisel) - TCP tunnel over HTTP: a single client/server binary pairs port-forwards through restrictive egress. Used by red-teamers tunneling through web-exit-only networks; the jpillora tunnel standard. (MIT)
- [GoReplay](https://github.com/probelabs/goreplay) - Traffic replay: capture production HTTP and replay it at scale against staging — shadow testing with real load instead of synthetic. Used by testers replaying real traffic; the open-core replay standard. (LGPL-3.0, open-core)
- [Slither](https://github.com/crytic/slither) - Solidity static analysis: 90+ detectors for reentrancy, variable shadowing and dangerous patterns in smart contracts. Used by auditors reviewing Solidity; the smart-contract SAST standard. (AGPL-3.0)
- [AFL++](https://github.com/AFLplusplus/AFLplusplus) - The fuzzer: coverage-guided mutation with modern instrumentation for everything from parsers to kernels. Used by researchers finding memory corruption; the community fuzzer that keeps out-performing the original AFL. (AGPL-3.0)
- [pip-audit](https://github.com/pypa/pip-audit) - Scans Python environments for known vulnerabilities against the PyPA advisory database. Used by Python shops securing their dependency trees; the official PyPA audit tool. (Apache-2.0)
- [xeol](https://github.com/xeol-io/xeol) - End-of-life dependency detection: finds packages past their support lifetime in SBOMs and repositories. Used by DevSecOps teams tracking EOL software; the lifecycle-aware scanner. (Apache-2.0)
- [CodeQL](https://github.com/github/codeql) - Semantic code analysis from GitHub: query code as a database and hunt vulnerability classes across languages with variant analysis. Used by security researchers hunting bug classes at scale; free for open-source research. (MIT)
- [cloudfox](https://github.com/BishopFox/cloudfox) - Cloud attack-surface auditor: maps IAM, services and privileges across AWS, Azure and GCP for engagement prep. Used by cloud pentesters building their attack map; the BishopFox cloud 'who am I' reconnaissance tool. (MIT)
- [GCP Scanner](https://github.com/google/gcp_scanner) - Google Cloud security scanner: enumerates projects, services, IAM and exposure across a GCP organization. Used by cloud auditors assessing GCP estates; the official google/gcp_scanner. (Apache-2.0)
- [Tracee](https://github.com/aquasecurity/tracee) - Container runtime security tracing with eBPF: follows syscalls and events to detect suspicious behavior in real time. Used by runtime-security teams watching containers live; Aquasecurity's eBPF sensor. (Apache-2.0)
- [Hayabusa](https://github.com/Yamato-Security/hayabusa) - Windows event-log analysis and threat hunting: parses EVTX at speed, detects with built-in Sigma rules and builds timelines. Used by DFIR hunters triaging Windows estates; the Yamato-Security log analyzer. (AGPL-3.0)
- [Netlas](https://netlas.io/) - Internet-registry search across ASNs, DNS, certificates and network data with a generous free tier for researchers. Used by OSINT and attack-surface work at zero budget; the registry search with the free-research tier. (free tier)

## ⚔️ The Armory — Offensive Security, Pentesting & Bug Bounty Tools
Intro: The professional offensive toolkit for authorized engagements, red-team exercises and training labs — the weapons every pentester reaches for first.

- [Metasploit Framework](https://github.com/rapid7/metasploit-framework) - The world's most-used pentest framework; thousands of validated modules for exploitation, post-engagement evidence gathering and reporting in authorized tests. Used by professional penetration testers and red teams in authorized engagements; the open-source counterpart to commercial exploitation platforms like Core Impact and Cobalt Strike.
- [Nmap](https://nmap.org) - The original network mapper: port, service and OS discovery plus the NSE scripting engine for everything from TLS audits to SMB checks. Used by virtually every network security auditor on earth; the open standard that commercial scanners built their discovery phases on.
- [Burp Suite Community Edition](https://portswigger.net/burp/communitydownload) - The industry-standard intercepting proxy for manual web testing; the free tier is where nearly every bug hunter starts. Used by web penetration testers as their daily intercepting proxy; the free tier of the industry-standard Burp Suite Professional. (free, proprietary)
- [Nuclei](https://github.com/projectdiscovery/nuclei) - Template-driven vulnerability scanner; the huge community template corpus gives it detection breadth no single scanner matches. Used by bug bounty hunters and enterprise security teams alike for template-driven scanning at scale; a free staple that rivals paid web-scanning engines for public attack-surface checks.
- [afrog](https://github.com/zan8in/afrog) - Fast cross-platform vulnerability scanner driven by a 1,700+ community-maintained YAML PoC corpus covering CVE, CNVD, unauthorized-access, default-password and fingerprinting checks. Used by bug bounty hunters and pentesters running corpus-driven scans; the open-source scanner with one of the largest community PoC collections. (MIT)
- [OWASP ZAP](https://github.com/zaproxy/zaproxy) - The open-source intercepting proxy and scanner — a full free Burp alternative with CI/CD integration and a big add-on ecosystem. Used by web pentesters, QA teams and bug bounty beginners.
- [OpenTRAP](https://github.com/opentrap/opentrap) - Free open-source Burp Suite alternative: an intercepting proxy with Repeater, Intruder, a Collaborator-style OAST server and the built-in OWASP ZAP scanner in one desktop suite. Used by web pentesters who want a full proxy workbench without a license; the open alternative built on the ZAP engine.. (AGPL-3.0)
- [Akto](https://github.com/akto-api-security/akto) - Self-hosted API-security platform that builds a continuous API inventory from mirrored runtime traffic and runs business-logic tests (BOLA, BFLA, OWASP API Top 10) in CI. Used by API security teams testing business-logic flaws generic DAST misses; the open-source alternative to API-scanning SaaS. (MIT)
- [sqlmap](https://github.com/sqlmapproject/sqlmap) - The definitive SQL injection detection and exploitation tool for authorized web application testing. Used by penetration testers everywhere for SQL injection proof; the definitive free SQLi automation tool with no real commercial equal. (GPL-2.0)
- [unKover](https://github.com/BRuteLogic/unKover) - The 403 access-bypass tester: a 13-technique battery (path rewrites, header tricks, HTTP method and version shifts) that turns forbidden endpoints into authorized access. Used by web pentesters proving 403s are misconfigurations during engagements; the focused free alternative to commercial authz-testing modules. (MIT per README)
- [jwt-hack](https://github.com/hahwul/jwt-hack) - JWT attack toolkit: cracking, payload generation, alg-confusion and claim-abuse checks for JSON Web Tokens in one CLI. Used by bug bounty hunters and pentesters auditing token-based authentication; the dedicated free toolkit for the JWT bug class.. (MIT)

- [Impacket](https://github.com/fortra/impacket) - The standard Python library for low-level Windows protocol work (SMB, LDAP, Kerberos); powers wmiexec/secretsdump-class tooling and AD assessments. Used by red teamers and AD security researchers; the Python underpinning of countless offensive and defensive toolkits.
- [MITRE Caldera](https://github.com/apache/caldera) - Scalable adversary-emulation platform built on ATT&CK; run purple-team exercises that prove your detections actually fire. Used by red teams and purple teams for automated adversary emulation; the open-source alternative to commercial breach-attack-simulation platforms like AttackIQ and SafeBreach.
- [Realm](https://github.com/spellshift/realm) - Open-source adversary-emulation framework with cross-platform implants and Terraform-driven C2 infrastructure for authorized red teams. Used by red teams running repeatable, scoped adversary emulation; the open-source alternative to commercial adversary-simulation platforms. (GPL-3.0)
- [BloodHound](https://github.com/SpecterOps/BloodHound) - Maps the shortest attack paths through Active Directory; defenders run it to find and fix their way to Domain Admin. Used by Active Directory pentesters to map hidden attack paths; the standard tool defenders also run to harden AD.
- [MobSF](https://github.com/MobSF/Mobile-Security-Framework-MobSF) - All-in-one mobile app security: static + dynamic analysis for Android/iOS in a single pipeline. Used by mobile app security testers for static and dynamic analysis; the open-source alternative to commercial mobile app testing suites. (GPL-3.0)
- [WPScan](https://github.com/wpscanteam/wpscan) - Black-box WordPress scanner: plugin/theme enumeration and version-based vuln lookups. Used by WordPress security auditors and managed-service providers; the standard black-box WordPress scanner, free alternative to paid WP security suites. (free for non-commercial use)
- [Bettercap](https://github.com/bettercap/bettercap) - The Swiss-army knife for authorized network assessments: ARP/DNS spoofing, MITM proxies, WiFi and BLE modules. Used by network penetration testers for authorized MITM analysis; the modern successor to Ettercap in most toolkits. (GPL-3.0)
- [Nikto](https://github.com/sullo/nikto) - Venerable web-server scanner; noisy but thorough on server misconfigurations and default files. Used by web server auditors for two decades; the venerable free complement to commercial web scanners' misconfiguration checks. (GPL-3.0; DB files have custom terms)
- [h5i](https://github.com/h5i-dev/h5i) - Agent-native web-security workspace: deterministic recon, secrets scanning, DOM post-message auditing and CI regression checks — fully usable without any LLM key. Used by security engineers wanting agent-native, deterministic web checks; a newer open alternative to dashboard-driven commercial scanners. (Apache-2.0)
- [goshs](https://github.com/goshs-labs/goshs) - Hardened HTTP file-transfer server built for engagements: upload/download, authentication, access control and TLS — the modern replacement for `python -m http.server`. Used by pentesters on engagements as a hardened file-transfer server; the security-hardened alternative to spinning up ad-hoc Python servers.
- [Dalfox](https://github.com/hahwul/dalfox) - Fast XSS scanner with DOM-aware analysis and a strong payload set; the modern XSS go-to. Used by bug bounty hunters for fast, DOM-aware XSS scanning; a favorite free alternative to Burp's scanner for XSS-only sweeps.
- [pphack](https://github.com/edoardottt/pphack) - Advanced client-side prototype-pollution scanner for JavaScript-heavy web apps. Used by bug bounty hunters auditing front-end pollution sinks; the dedicated free scanner for this bug class. (MIT)
- [NetExec](https://github.com/Pennyw0rth/NetExec) - CrackMapExec's actively maintained successor; audit credential hygiene and service misconfigurations across whole subnets. Used by internal network auditors and AD pentesters for credential-hygiene sweeps.
- [Responder](https://github.com/lgandx/Responder) - LLMNR/NBT-NS/MDNS poisoner; the fastest way to demonstrate credential-leak exposure on an authorized internal network. Used by internal penetration testers to demonstrate credential exposure on enterprise networks; the canonical LLMNR/NBT-NS poisoner. (GPL-3.0)
- [commix](https://github.com/commixproject/commix) - Automated OS command injection detection and exploitation for web tests. Used by web pentesters for command-injection proof; a focused free tool that complements general-purpose commercial scanners. (GPL-3.0)
- [Ghauri](https://github.com/r0oth3x49/ghauri) - A strong sqlmap alternative for error/union/blind SQLi with clever parameter tampering; slower release cadence but unique value. Used by bug bounty hunters for tricky blind, error-based and time-based SQLi.
- [2MNY Website Vulnerability Scanner](https://github.com/RanaKhan77/Website-Vulnerability-Scanner) - Open-source web vulnerability scanner with 202 deterministic modules: OWASP Top 10, XSS/SQLi/SSRF, subdomain takeover, WordPress and AI/LLM surface checks, CVSS-scored evidence and HTML/PDF reports. Self-hosted via Docker. Used by security teams that want evidence-backed, deterministic scanning they can self-host; the open-source alternative to paid scanner SaaS for website attack surfaces. (maintainer's project)
- [masscan](https://github.com/robertdavidgraham/masscan) - The fastest TCP port scanner on earth; scan the whole internet in minutes — on networks you're authorized to test. Used by researchers and recon teams for internet-scale port discovery. (AGPL-3.0)
- [RustScan](https://github.com/bee-san/RustScan) - Modern Rust port scanner that finds open ports in seconds and pipes them straight into Nmap. Used by bug bounty hunters and sysadmins for fast port discovery with Nmap piping; a modern free alternative to legacy scanners. (GPL-3.0)
- [crossfyre-toolchain](https://github.com/clickswave/crossfyre-toolchain) - Recon-to-scan toolchain of five Rust engines — subdomain discovery, port scanning, content fuzzing, fingerprinting and vulnerability checks — plus a self-hosted OAST server that confirms each finding before it reports it. Used by bug bounty hunters running fast terminal-first pipelines; the Rust-native alternative to stitched-together shell toolkits.. (Apache-2.0)
- [Tsunami](https://github.com/google/tsunami-security-scanner) - Google's security scanner for large fleets, focused on reliably exploitable high-severity flaws with few false positives. Used by security teams scanning large fleets of internet-facing services; Google's open-source answer to commercial enterprise web scanners.
- [VulnRadar](https://github.com/VulnRadar/vulnradar.dev) - Self-hostable web vulnerability scanner with 855+ deterministic checks, stable finding IDs for scan-to-scan diffing, severity-rated reports and actionable fix guidance. Used by teams wanting instant, shareable scan reports without a SaaS subscription; the self-hosted alternative to commercial web scanners.. (GPL-3.0)
- [Prism X](https://github.com/yqcs/prismx) - Automated enterprise network security risk detection: one command sweeps hosts and services for exploitable findings like the commercial AWVS/fscan class. Used by internal teams risk-scoring their own networks; the free enterprise-web risk scanner. (Apache-2.0)
- [WAVSS](https://github.com/Ammar-1993/wavss) - Audit-first web vulnerability scanner for common flaws like SQLi and XSS, with domain-ownership verification, DoS-avoidant pacing and PDF reports with remediation guidance. Used by auditors scanning client sites safely under strict rules of engagement; the audit-focused alternative to exploit-heavy scanners. (no license yet — verify before reuse)
- [SoliTools](https://github.com/soliofski/SoliTools) - High-accuracy web security scanner in Go built on the rule that a finding must be worth a human's time — precision beats recall at every decision point. Used by bug bounty hunters who want quiet, high-precision scans; the precision-first free scanner.. (MIT)
- [Hakscan](https://github.com/slayzbsss/Hakscan) - Fast, self-contained vulnerability scanner shipped as a single Go binary — covers web apps, APKs and binaries, extensible with JSON and nuclei YAML templates. Used by bug bounty hunters wanting one-binary scanning across target types; the portable free scanner for quick triage.. (MIT)
- [wafw00f](https://github.com/EnableSecurity/wafw00f) - Fingerprints the web application firewall sitting in front of a site so you know exactly what you're testing against. Used by pentesters planning engagements to fingerprint WAFs first; the standard free WAF detector.
- [wapiti](https://github.com/wapiti-scanner/wapiti) - Open-source black-box web vulnerability scanner that crawls and audits without needing a browser. Used by web pentesters and CI pipelines for black-box web scanning; a solid free alternative to paid web vulnerability scanners. (GPL-2.0)
- [WhatWeb](https://github.com/urbanadventurer/WhatWeb) - Identifies what a website is built with — frameworks, CMSs, servers and versions — in one command. Used by recon teams to fingerprint what a site runs on; the standard free tech-identifier behind many commercial recon feeds. (GPL-2.0)
- [GitTools](https://github.com/internetwache/GitTools) - Recovers an exposed `.git` directory into full source and commit history — audit your servers before attackers do. Used by bug bounty hunters to recover exposed .git directories into full source; the go-to free tool for this bug class.

- [kiterunner](https://github.com/assetnote/kiterunner) - API route brute-forcer with method-aware wordlists derived from Swagger specs; dormant upstream, still the reference. Used by API security testers brute-forcing routes with method-aware wordlists; the open-source alternative to commercial API discovery scans. (AGPL-3.0)
- [osmedeus](https://github.com/j3ssie/osmedeus) - Workflow engine that chains recon and scanning into scheduled, repeatable attack-surface pipelines. Used by recon teams automating full attack-surface workflows; the free alternative to commercial ASM platforms for scheduled recon.
- [PWN](https://github.com/0dayInc/pwn) - Ruby offensive-security automation framework that orchestrates Burp, Nessus, OpenVAS, ZAP and other engines into scripted authorized engagements. Used by pentesters chaining commercial and open engines from one Ruby CLI; the glue layer for multi-engine engagements. (MIT)
- [ettercap](https://github.com/Ettercap/ettercap) - The veteran MITM analysis suite for ARP poisoning, sniffing and protocol dissection — bettercap's classic sibling. Used by network pentesters and students learning MITM analysis; the veteran suite that inspired later tools like Bettercap. (GPL-2.0)
- [OpenVAS Scanner](https://github.com/greenbone/openvas-scanner) - Greenbone's open-source vulnerability scanning engine for network and service flaws. Used by security teams running unauthenticated network vulnerability scans; the classic open-source alternative to Nessus, Qualys and Rapid7 InsightVM. (GPL-2.0)
- [aircrack-ng](https://github.com/aircrack-ng/aircrack-ng) - The complete WiFi security audit suite: capture, inject and audit — the wireless standard for two decades. Used by WiFi security auditors worldwide; the complete free suite replacing any commercial WiFi assessment tool. (GPL-2.0)
- [THC-Hydra](https://github.com/vanhauser-thc/thc-hydra) - The classic parallelized login cracker for 50+ protocols; password audits only on systems you own or are paid to test. Used by pentesters for authorized login audits across 50+ protocols; the classic parallelized cracker with no commercial equal. (AGPL-3.0)
- [brutespray](https://github.com/x90skysn3k/brutespray) - Multi-protocol credential brute-forcer that parses Nmap and Nessus output and automatically sprays discovered services, on networks you are authorized to test. Used by pentesters validating default and weak credentials during engagements; the classic Python companion to Hydra. (MIT)
- [objection](https://github.com/sensepost/objection) - Frida-powered runtime exploration toolkit for mobile apps — inspect and manipulate iOS/Android at runtime. Used by mobile pentesters for runtime exploration without jailbreaks; the go-to Frida front-end for mobile engagements. (GPL-3.0)
- [RsaCtfTool](https://github.com/Ganapati/RsaCtfTool) - RSA security-testing tool: attacks weak keys and unprotected disclosures purely offline, for auditors and CTF practice. Used by CTF players and crypto researchers attacking weak RSA keys; a niche free tool with no commercial counterpart. (GPL-3.0)
- [Pywerview](https://github.com/the-useless-one/pywerview) - A Python port of PowerView's AD recon verbs; same questions, no Windows host required. Used by AD pentesters who live on Linux but need PowerView-style Windows enumeration; the Python port of the classic PowerView. (GPL-3.0)
- [Red-Teaming-Toolkit](https://github.com/infosecn1nja/Red-Teaming-Toolkit) - Curated index of adversary-emulation and red-team tradecraft resources. Used by red teamers planning their tooling stack. (curated list)
- [Nightingale](https://github.com/RAJANAGORI/Nightingale) - Dockerized pentest environment that comes preconfigured with the essential VAPT toolset, so a full offensive lab is one pull away. Used by pentesters and training labs that want a reproducible toolbox without hand-installing tools; the Docker alternative to hand-built pentest VMs. (GPL-3.0)
- [f8x](https://github.com/ffffffff0x/f8x) - Red/blue team environment automation: a few commands deploy a full security-tool stack on a fresh Linux host. Used by red and blue teams standing up engagement or lab boxes fast; a community standard for security-toolbox provisioning. (Apache-2.0)
- [jiraffe](https://github.com/0x48piraj/jiraffe) - Jira recon and security auditing: version fingerprinting, gadget/permission checks and CVE mapping for authorized instances. Used by bug bounty hunters targeting Atlassian deployments; a focused free exploitation kit for the Jira bug class. (MIT)

- [authz0](https://github.com/hahwul/authz0) - CLI broken-access-control tester that replays the same URL set under multiple roles' credentials from a YAML template and flags unauthorized responses. Used by pentesters proving BOLA/authz gaps in minutes instead of hours; role-pair replay made repeatable. (MIT)
- [idoraider](https://github.com/jojin1709/idoraider) - Template-driven BOLA/IDOR scanner in Go with dual-token replay: replay the same request under two identities and flag object-level authorization breaks, with zero external dependencies. Used by bug bounty hunters hunting IDOR at scale; the focused free scanner for the BOLA bug class.. (MIT)
- [InQL](https://github.com/doyensec/inql) - The reference GraphQL security testing toolkit as a Burp Suite extension: schema introspection analysis, query generation, vulnerability scanning and engine fingerprinting. Used by pentesters auditing GraphQL APIs where classic proxies stop at the surface; doyensec-maintained since 2018. (Apache-2.0)
- [OXO](https://github.com/Ostorlab/oxo) - Pip-installable security-scanning orchestrator that composes Docker-image agents (nmap, nuclei, ZAP, subfinder…) into typed asset scan graphs with a CLI + GraphQL API. Used by security engineers chaining multi-tool pipelines without glue code; scans IPs, domains, links, files and mobile apps. (Apache-2.0)
- [Artemis](https://github.com/CERT-Polska/Artemis) - Modular web-vulnerability scanning platform that powers CERT Poland's nation-scale scanning (50.6k domains since 2023) and auto-generates human-readable, FP-filtered advisory reports per organization. Used by CSIRTs running continuous constituency scanning with e-mailable advisories; the public blueprint for national-scale detection. (BSD-3-Clause)
- [graudit](https://github.com/wireghoul/graudit) - Grep-based rough source-code auditor: one shell script + 26 regex signature databases (PHP, SQL, XSS, secrets, java, python…) for first-pass static review. Used by auditors triaging unfamiliar codebases before deep review; 17 years maintained, v4.1 (2026). (GPL-3.0)
- [rcekit](https://github.com/kabiri-labs/rcekit) - Zero-dependency RCE confirmation toolkit with false-positive-resistant sink tiering. Used by pentesters confirming command-injection findings safely; the confirmation tier for injection testing. (MIT)
- [Sniper](https://github.com/sm1ee/Sniper) - Fast Rust-native intercepting proxy with passive scanning and AI agent support. Used by web testers wanting a lightweight daily proxy; the Rust alternative for intercepting work. (MIT)
- [Nullock](https://github.com/Bikebrainz/Nullock) - Zero-telemetry HTTP/2 and WebSocket-native Burp alternative with a plugin ecosystem. Used by privacy-conscious web testers; the telemetry-free Burp alternative. (MIT)
- [perseid](https://github.com/andyintheshell/perseid) - Memory-bounded Rust Burp alternative with no artificial rate limits. Used by long-running web test sessions; the resource-frugal intercepting proxy. (Apache-2.0)
- [wp-hunter](https://github.com/Aryoksss/wp-hunter) - Bilingual WordPress plugin triage toolkit with static authorization analysis, archive integrity checks and version monitoring. Used by WordPress security researchers auditing plugins; the plugin-audit toolkit for WP research. (MIT)
- [pentool](https://github.com/DrXOps/pentool) - Console-based Burp alternative with crawler, scanner and CI/CD mode. Used by terminal-first pentesters; the TUI web-testing workbench. (AGPL-3.0, copyleft)
- [5G Houl](https://github.com/asset-group/5ghoul-5g-nr-attacks) - Fuzzing tool that discovers vulnerabilities in 5G NR implementations, misleading UEs into connecting to malicious base stations. Used by telecom security researchers; a notable 5G NR fuzzer. (GPL-2.0)
- [5Greplay](https://github.com/Montimage/5Greplay) - NGAP fuzzing tool used for testing 5G core network AMF implementations across Open5GS, free5GC, OAI and ella-core. Used by telecom security researchers; a focused 5G core fuzzer. (Apache-2.0)
- [AdvPhishing](https://github.com/Ignitetch/AdvPhishing) - Advanced phishing tool with login page cloning and tunneling support. Used by penetration testers and security researchers; an offensive social-engineering framework.
- [APKLeaks](https://github.com/dwisiswant0/apkleaks) - Scans APK files for URIs, endpoints, and hardcoded secrets, quickly extracting API keys and internal URLs from compiled Android apps. Used by mobile pentesters and bug bounty hunters; a fast secrets-hunting utility. (Apache-2.0)
- [ArchStrike](https://archstrike.org/) - Arch Linux repository providing security tools. Used by Arch users and pentesters; a convenient package source for offensive tooling.
- [Armitage](https://github.com/rsmudge/armitage) - GUI front-end for Metasploit that visualizes attacks and targets. Used by penetration testers and red teams; a well-known offensive collaboration tool. (BSD-3-Clause)
- [ARS3NAL](https://github.com/inflictx/Arsenal) - Offline-first self-hosted pentest and bug-bounty arsenal with searchable payloads, command generator, GTFOBins and wordlists. Used by pentesters and bug-bounty hunters; a convenient all-in-one reference. (GPL-3.0)
- [asnfuzzgen](https://github.com/FICS/asnfuzzgen) - ASN.1-based fuzzing tool for the Radio Resource Control protocol in 4G and 5G networks, generating syntactically correct payloads. Used by telecom security researchers; a specialized RRC fuzzer. (MIT)
- [Astra](https://github.com/flipkart-incubator/astra) - Automated security testing tool for REST APIs from Flipkart, checking endpoints for common API vulnerabilities. Used by API developers and pentesters; a solid open-source API scanner. (Apache-2.0)
- [awesome-cve-poc](https://github.com/qazbnm456/awesome-cve-poc) - Curated list of CVE proof-of-concept exploits maintained on GitHub. Used by pentesters and vulnerability researchers; a broad PoC discovery index.
- [aws_pwn](https://github.com/dagrz/aws_pwn) - Collection of AWS penetration testing scripts and utilities for assessing cloud account security. Used by cloud pentesters and red teamers; a practical AWS attack toolkit.
- [BackBox](https://www.backbox.org/) - Ubuntu-based Linux distribution for penetration testing. Used by security testers; a lightweight option for offensive assessments.
- [BeEF (Browser Exploitation Framework)](https://github.com/beefproject/beef) - Browser exploitation framework that hooks browsers for client-side attacks. Used by penetration testers and red teams; a classic offensive security platform.
- [Binary Exploitation Writeups](https://github.com/susheel-cybercode/binary-exploitation-writeups) - CTF-focused writeups covering buffer overflows, ret2libc, ROP, and shellcode. Used by security students and CTF players; a practical binary-exploitation learning collection.
- [BlackArch Linux](https://blackarch.org/) - Arch-based Linux distribution bundling over 2800 hacking tools. Used by pentesters and researchers; a large offensive tool collection.
- [BlackEye](https://github.com/An0nUD4Y/blackeye) - Phishing toolkit that clones login pages for credential harvesting. Used by red teams and social-engineering testers; a popular but ethically sensitive offensive framework.
- [BlueToolkit](https://github.com/tehseensagar/BlueToolkit-exploit) - Extensible Bluetooth Classic vulnerability testing framework cataloging known automotive Bluetooth exploits. Used by automotive security testers; a focused testing framework. (MIT)
- [blutter](https://github.com/worawit/blutter) - Reverse engineering tool that extracts Dart code and function signatures from Flutter snapshot files. Used by mobile reverse engineers; a focused free Flutter analysis utility. (MIT)
- [BugBoard](https://github.com/yogsec/BugBoard) - Bug hunter dashboard for vulnerability scanning, automation, and managing bug bounty workflows. Used by bug bounty hunters; a workflow hub for organizing recon and findings. (MIT)
- [Bugtraq](https://bugtraq-team.com/) - Linux distribution for penetration testing and malware analysis. Used by security testers and analysts; a combined offensive and analysis environment.
- [Bully](https://github.com/aanarchyy/bully) - WPS attack tool that brute-forces wireless PINs. Used by penetration testers; a focused free utility for WPS security testing. (GPL-3.0)
- [Burp Suite](https://portswigger.net/burp) - Web application and API security testing platform with a free community edition. Used by pentesters and bug-bounty hunters; the industry-standard toolkit for probing web targets. (free tier)
- [bXSS](https://github.com/LewisArdern/bXSS) - Simple blind XSS application adapted from cure53 research for testing out-of-band payload execution. Used by web pentesters; a lightweight blind XSS harness. (MIT)
- [BYOB (Build Your Own Botnet)](https://github.com/malwaredllc/byob) - Post-exploitation framework and C2 platform that generates cross-platform encrypted binaries with multi-stage delivery chains and reverse shells. Used by red teamers and researchers; a full-featured botnet-style command and control toolkit. (GPL-3.0)
- [BYOVD Research](https://github.com/BlackSnufkin/BYOVD) - Bring Your Own Vulnerable Driver research repository with detection and reverse engineering methodology covering recent CVEs. Used by red teamers and defenders; a practical reference for BYOVD attack and detection. (GPL-3.0)
- [CamPhish](https://github.com/techchipnet/CamPhish) - Webcam phishing attack tool that captures images via a crafted link. Used by penetration testers and security educators; a popular social-engineering demonstration utility. (GPL-3.0)
- [CANToolz](https://github.com/eik00d/CANToolz) - Modular CAN bus analysis framework chaining modules for ECU discovery, man-in-the-middle testing, fuzzing, and brute-forcing. Used by automotive researchers; a flexible CAN toolkit. (GPL-3.0)
- [Caring Caribou](https://github.com/CaringCaribou/caringcaribou) - Zero-knowledge CAN bus exploration tool that discovers services, dumps traffic, and fuzzes messages. Used by automotive pentesters; a popular discovery utility. (GPL-3.0)
- [CDK](https://github.com/cdk-team/CDK) - Zero-dependency container penetration toolkit for stable exploitation in slimmed containers without OS dependencies. Used by container pentesters; a compact free exploitation toolkit. (Apache-2.0)
- [cefdebug](https://github.com/taviso/cefdebug) - Minimal code to connect to a Chrome Embedded Framework debugger for exploitation research. Used by security researchers; a focused CEF debugging utility. (Apache-2.0)
- [CFR](https://www.benf.org/other/cfr/) - Java decompiler that converts compiled bytecode back into readable source. Used by reverse engineers and security researchers; a capable standalone decompiler.
- [Charlotte](https://github.com/9emin1/charlotte) - C++ shellcode launcher designed to bypass security monitoring without triggering alerts. Used by red teamers; a compact loader for evasion testing.
- [Cmsmap](https://github.com/Dionach/CMSmap) - CMS detection and exploitation tool that fingerprints content management systems and probes for known issues. Used by web pentesters; a practical CMS-focused scanner. (GPL-3.0)
- [Coldfire](https://github.com/redcode-labs/Coldfire) - Golang malware development library providing functions for process manipulation, network operations, privilege checking, and evasion. Used by red teamers and malware researchers; a convenient Go toolkit for offensive implants. (MIT)
- [Covenant](https://github.com/cobbr/Covenant) - C#-based post-exploitation platform for red team operations. Used by red teams and penetration testers; a modern offensive C2 framework. (GPL-3.0)
- [CrackStation](https://crackstation.net/) - Free online service for cracking password hashes using dictionary attacks. Used by security testers and researchers; a convenient free hash lookup service. (free, proprietary)
- [CredSniper](https://github.com/ustayready/CredSniper) - Phishing framework with two-factor authentication bypass support for credential capture. Used by red teams and security researchers; an advanced offensive social-engineering tool. (Apache-2.0)
- [CSS-Keylogging](https://github.com/maxchehab/CSS-Keylogging) - Chrome extension and Express server demonstrating keylogging via CSS injection techniques. Used by security researchers and pentesters; an educational CSS exfiltration proof of concept.
- [ctftool](https://github.com/taviso/ctftool) - Interactive CTF exploration tool for Windows environments. Used by red teamers and CTF players; an offensive exploration and exploitation utility. (Apache-2.0)
- [Cyclops is a novel browser that can detect vulnerability automatically](https://github.com/v8blink/Chromium-based-XSS-Taint-Tracking/) - Chromium-based browser with XSS taint tracking to detect vulnerabilities automatically. Used by web security researchers; a novel offensive analysis tool. (GPL-3.0)
- [DarkArmour](https://github.com/bats3c/darkarmour) - Windows antivirus evasion tool that stores and executes an encrypted Windows binary entirely from memory without writing to disk. Used by red teamers; a compact in-memory execution utility. (MIT)
- [DarkWidow](https://github.com/reveng007/DarkWidow) - Advanced malware payload framework using indirect dynamic syscalls, SSN sorting via modified TartarusGate, and APC-based remote process injection. Used by red teamers and malware researchers; a sophisticated evasion-focused offensive toolkit. (MIT)
- [Dirb](https://gitlab.com/kalilinux/packages/dirb) - Web content scanner that brute-forces directories and files to discover hidden resources. Used by pentesters and bug bounty hunters; a classic Kali tool for content discovery. (GPL-2.0)
- [DNS Rebind Toolkit](https://github.com/brannondorsey/dns-rebind-toolkit) - Frontend JavaScript framework for developing DNS rebinding exploits against vulnerable hosts and services on a local network. Used by pentesters; an exploit development toolkit. (MIT)
- [Donut](https://github.com/TheWover/donut) - Position-independent shellcode generator and in-memory payload injector that converts executables, .NET assemblies, and scripts into encrypted shellcode. Used by red teamers and malware researchers; a widely adopted payload conversion utility. (BSD-3-Clause)
- [Dradis](https://dradisframework.com/) - Collaboration and reporting platform for penetration testers to organize findings and share results. Used by pentest teams; a practical tool for consolidating engagement output.
- [Drozer](https://github.com/ReversecLabs/drozer) - Security assessment framework for Android focused on exploiting inter-process communication surfaces. Used by mobile pentesters; a leading tool for Android IPC testing. (NOASSERTION)
- [dtd-finder](https://github.com/GoSecure/dtd-finder) - Lists local DTDs and generates XXE payloads using them for XML external entity testing. Used by web pentesters; a focused XXE payload helper.
- [DVCS-Pillage](https://github.com/evilpacket/DVCS-Pillage) - Tool to pillage web-accessible Git, Mercurial and Bazaar repositories for source code recovery. Used by pentesters and bug-bounty hunters; a handy VCS exposure exploitation utility.
- [dvcs-ripper](https://github.com/kost/dvcs-ripper) - Rips web-accessible distributed version control repositories such as SVN, Git and Mercurial. Used by pentesters and bug-bounty hunters; a reliable source-code exposure extractor. (GPL-2.0)
- [EAPHammer](https://github.com/s0lst1c3/eaphammer) - Targeted evil twin attacks against WPA2-Enterprise networks, supporting hostile portal and credential capture. Used by red teamers and wireless testers; a focused enterprise Wi-Fi attack tool. (GPL-3.0)
- [Empire](https://github.com/BC-SECURITY/Empire) - Post-exploitation framework for PowerShell agents with modular capabilities. Used by red teams and penetration testers; a well-known offensive C2 platform. (BSD-3-Clause)
- [Evilginx2](https://github.com/kgretzky/evilginx2) - Phishing toolkit using reverse proxy to capture credentials and session tokens. Used by red teams; a powerful free adversary-in-the-middle tool. (BSD-3-Clause)
- [Fedora Security Lab](https://labs.fedoraproject.org/security/) - Fedora spin for security auditing and testing. Used by security professionals; a ready-made Fedora environment for audits.
- [Fern WiFi Cracker](https://github.com/matosdiego/fern-wifi-cracker) - Python/Qt-based GUI for wireless security auditing and attack, capable of cracking WEP, WPA and WPS keys. Used by wireless testers and learners; a beginner-friendly GUI auditor.
- [Flashrom](https://github.com/flashrom/flashrom) - Utility for detecting, reading, writing, verifying, and erasing flash chips via SPI programmers. Used by firmware analysts; the standard open-source flash tool. (GPL-2.0)
- [FlowDroid](https://github.com/secure-software-engineering/FlowDroid) - Precise context, flow, field, and object-sensitive taint analysis framework for Android apps, detecting data leakage and injection vulnerabilities. Used by mobile security researchers; a rigorous static analysis engine. (LGPL-2.1)
- [Fluxion](https://github.com/FluxionNetwork/fluxion) - Social-engineering focused Wi-Fi attack tool that creates rogue access points and captive portals to harvest credentials. Used by penetration testers; a popular rogue AP framework. (GPL-3.0)
- [Foojank](https://github.com/foohq/foojank) - Open-source C2 framework built on NATS providing customizable transport over TCP and WebSockets. Used by red and purple teams; a modern open-source command-and-control option. (Apache-2.0)
- [fuzz.txt](https://github.com/Bo0oM/fuzz.txt) - Wordlist of potentially dangerous files for web fuzzing and content discovery. Used by pentesters and bug-bounty hunters; a handy payload list for finding exposed sensitive paths. (WTFPL)
- [Fuzzbunch](https://github.com/fuzzbunch/fuzzbunch) - NSA exploit framework leaked as part of the Shadow Brokers dump. Used by researchers and red teams; a historically significant offensive exploitation toolkit.
- [FuzzDB](https://github.com/fuzzdb-project/fuzzdb) - Dictionary of attack patterns and primitives for black-box fault injection and resource discovery. Used by pentesters; a classic fuzzing payload collection.
- [FuzzyDoo](https://github.com/gabrielepongelli/FuzzyDoo) - Open-source framework for finding flaws in the 5G landscape, enabling dynamic test message generation for encrypted communications. Used by telecom security researchers; a specialized 5G fuzzing framework. (MIT)
- [GadgetToJScript](https://github.com/med0x2e/GadgetToJScript) - Generates .NET serialized gadgets that trigger assembly load and execution when deserialized from JS/VBS/VBA scripts, enabling fileless execution. Used by red teamers; a known tradecraft utility for script-based payload delivery. (GPL-3.0)
- [Ghostwriter](https://github.com/GhostManager/Ghostwriter) - Reporting and engagement management platform for offensive security teams. Used by red teams and pentesters; a structured way to track engagements and deliver findings. (BSD-3-Clause)
- [GnackTrack](https://sourceforge.net/projects/gnacktrack/) - Linux live distribution for penetration testing. Used by security testers; a bootable offensive testing environment.
- [Gophish](https://github.com/gophish/gophish) - Open-source phishing toolkit for awareness training and authorized testing campaigns. Used by security teams; a popular free phishing simulation platform.
- [Grapefruit](https://github.com/ChiChou/grapefruit) - Open-source runtime mobile instrumentation toolkit powered by Frida with a web interface for inspecting, hooking, and modifying iOS and Android apps. Used by mobile security researchers; a practical dynamic analysis workbench. (MIT)
- [HackRF One](https://greatscottgadgets.com/hackrf/) - Open-source software defined radio platform for wireless experimentation, capturing and transmitting signals across a wide frequency range. Used by RF researchers and wireless testers; a popular affordable SDR.
- [hcxdumptool](https://github.com/ZerBea/hcxdumptool) - Small tool for capturing packets from WLAN devices, specifically designed for PMKID and handshake collection for Hashcat. Used by wireless auditors; a modern capture utility. (MIT)
- [hcxtools](https://github.com/ZerBea/hcxtools) - Converts packet captures into hash files for cracking with Hashcat or John the Ripper. Used by wireless auditors; essential for modern WPA/WPA2 auditing workflows. (MIT)
- [hermes-dec](https://github.com/P1sec/hermes-dec) - Decompiler for Hermes bytecode used in React Native apps, converting bytecode back to readable JavaScript. Used by mobile reverse engineers; a niche free analysis tool. (AGPL-3.0)
- [how2heap](https://github.com/shellphish/how2heap) - Educational repository of heap exploitation techniques with example code. Used by CTF players and security students; a well-known learning resource for heap bugs. (MIT)
- [HTTPLeaks](https://github.com/cure53/HTTPLeaks) - Reference listing all possible ways a website can leak HTTP requests to external destinations. Used by web security researchers; an educational leak-vector catalogue. (BSD-2-Clause)
- [Iaitō](https://github.com/hteso/iaito) - Qt and C++ GUI for the radare2 reverse engineering framework. Used by malware analysts and reverse engineers; a graphical disassembly and analysis frontend. (GPL-3.0)
- [Inceptor](https://github.com/klezvirus/inceptor) - Template-driven framework for AV and EDR evasion, providing modular templates for payload generation and obfuscation. Used by red teamers; a flexible evasion template engine. (NOASSERTION)
- [IndustrialXPL-Forge (IXF)](https://github.com/mrhenrike/IndustrialXPL-Forge) - Large Python-first OT/ICS/SCADA security assessment and exploitation framework with hundreds of modules spanning dozens of vendors. Used by industrial penetration testers; one of the broadest open exploitation collections for OT. (BSD-3-Clause)
- [js-vuln-db](https://github.com/tunz/js-vuln-db) - Collection of JavaScript engine CVEs with accompanying proof-of-concept code. Used by browser exploit developers; a focused JS engine PoC database.
- [JShell](https://github.com/s0md3v/JShell) - Tool that obtains a JavaScript shell through XSS, enabling interactive exploitation in the victim browser. Used by web pentesters and bug-bounty hunters; a clever XSS post-exploitation helper.
- [JTAGenum](https://github.com/cyphunk/JTAGenum) - Arduino-compatible JTAG pin scanning tool that enumerates instruction registers and discovers undocumented instructions. Used by hardware hackers; a handy open-source enumerator. (GPL-3.0)
- [JTAGulator](https://github.com/grandideastudio/jtagulator) - Open-source hardware tool that brute-forces pins to discover JTAG and UART assignments on unknown boards. Used by hardware hackers; a staple of PCB reverse engineering.
- [jwt-auditor](https://github.com/mohelobeid/jwt-auditor) - Offline CLI that decodes and audits JWTs for alg:none, weak HMAC secrets, and RS256-to-HS256 confusion. Used by pentesters and developers; a focused token-audit utility. (AGPL-3.0)
- [Kali Linux](https://www.kali.org/) - Advanced penetration testing and security auditing Linux distribution. Used by pentesters and researchers; a widely adopted offensive security platform.
- [Katana Framework](https://github.com/PowerScript/KatanaFramework) - Post-exploitation and reporting utility framework for penetration testing. Used by pentesters; a tool for managing exploitation activity and output.
- [Killer](https://github.com/0xHossam/Killer) - Simple tool designed to bypass AV/EDR security tools using various evasion techniques. Used by red teamers; a lightweight evasion utility for quick testing.
- [KillerBee](https://github.com/riverloopsec/killerbee) - Zigbee security testing framework for capturing traffic, testing replay attacks, and checking key exchange security. Used by wireless pentesters; a classic Zigbee toolkit. (GPL-3.0)
- [KubeXHunt](https://pypi.org/project/kubexhunt/) - Kubernetes post-compromise security assessment tool mapping attack paths from pod to cloud account. Used by cloud attackers and defenders; a free offensive assessment utility.
- [libc-database](https://github.com/niklasb/libc-database) - Builds a database of libc offsets to identify libc versions from leaked addresses. Used by exploit developers and CTF players; a practical binary-exploitation helper. (MIT)
- [MagicTree](http://www.magictree.org/) - Pentesting productivity tool for data aggregation and reporting. Used by penetration testers; a lightweight way to organize findings into reports.
- [MDK4](https://github.com/aircrack-ng/mdk4) - Wi-Fi testing tool designed to exploit common protocol weaknesses, including deauthentication and beacon flooding. Used by wireless auditors; a staple for protocol stress testing. (GPL-3.0)
- [Merlin](https://github.com/Ne0nd0g/merlin) - Post-exploitation command-and-control server using HTTP/2 for covert channels. Used by red teams and penetration testers; an offensive C2 framework. (GPL-3.0)
- [Mimikatz](https://github.com/gentilkiwi/mimikatz) - Well-known Windows credential extraction tool pulling plaintext passwords, NTLM hashes, and Kerberos tickets from LSASS. Used by red teamers and penetration testers; the canonical credential-dumping utility.
- [MNSF](https://github.com/noz-co-id/MNSF) - Mobile network security framework providing open-source security testing for 2G through 5G networks using SDR and telecom stacks. Used by telecom security researchers; a modular testing framework.
- [Mythic](https://github.com/its-a-feature/Mythic) - Collaborative cross-platform C2 framework with plug-and-play architecture for payload types and communication profiles. Used by red teams and purple teamers; a flexible open-source command-and-control platform. (NOASSERTION)
- [nano](https://github.com/s0md3v/nano) - Family of code-golfed PHP shells designed to be tiny and evasive. Used by red teamers; a minimal webshell collection for constrained payloads.
- [Nazar](https://pypi.org/project/nazar/) - Open-source autonomous security and quality scanner for React Native, Flutter, and native mobile projects with 197+ checks. Used by mobile developers; a broad free auditing tool.
- [NetHunter CARsenal](https://www.kali.org/docs/nethunter/nethunter-carsenal/) - Kali NetHunter automotive security toolset bundling CAN configuration, VIN decoding, can-utils, and Caring Caribou. Used by mobile pentesters; a convenient Android toolkit. (GPL-3.0)
- [Nmap Vulners](https://github.com/vulnersCom/nmap-vulners) - Nmap NSE script that queries the Vulners database to detect CVEs for discovered services. Used by pentesters and network auditors; a handy way to enrich Nmap scans with vulnerability data. (NOASSERTION)
- [NodeZero](https://sourceforge.net/projects/nodezero/) - Ubuntu-based penetration testing operating system. Used by security testers; a distribution bundling offensive tools.
- [numasec](https://github.com/FrancescoStabile/numasec) - AI-driven penetration-testing platform coordinating 10 agents and 38 vulnerability scanners across the OWASP Top 10. Used by pentesters and security teams; an ambitious autonomous testing framework. (AGPL-3.0)
- [Obfusk8](https://github.com/x86byte/Obfusk8) - Lightweight C++17 header-only obfuscation library for Windows binaries, providing compile-time obfuscation capabilities. Used by red team developers; an easy-to-integrate obfuscation building block. (MIT)
- [one_gadget](https://github.com/david942j/one_gadget) - Finds magic gadgets in libc that spawn a shell with minimal constraints. Used by CTF players and exploit developers; a handy binary-exploitation utility. (MIT)
- [ONUS](https://github.com/maverickaayush/ONUS) - Web vulnerability assessment platform orchestrating multiple scanners with deterministic CVSS v3.1 scoring and AI-assisted reporting. Used by security teams and pentesters; a consolidated assessment workflow. (MIT)

- [Protofire](https://github.com/ridpath/protofire) - Modular OT/ICS protocol fuzzer with plugin support for Modbus/TCP, DNP3, S7comm, IEC 60870-5-104, and OPC UA, plus PCAP replay and anomaly detection. Used by ICS vulnerability researchers; a versatile fuzzing harness for industrial protocols. (MIT)

- [ROPgadget](https://github.com/JonathanSalwan/ROPgadget) - Searches binaries for ROP gadgets across ELF, PE, and Mach-O formats. Used by exploit developers and CTF players; a standard binary-exploitation utility.

- [ScareCrow](https://github.com/Tylous/ScareCrow) - Payload toolkit using suspended processes and direct syscalls for EDR evasion, generating loaders that execute payloads without touching disk. Used by red teamers; a popular loader generator for evasion testing.

- [Shellter](https://www.shellterproject.com) - Dynamic shellcode injector for Windows executables to bypass antivirus. Used by penetration testers and red teams; a popular offensive payload tool.
- [Shhhloader](https://github.com/icyguider/Shhhloader) - Shellcode loader that compiles a C++ stub performing multiple AV/EDR bypass techniques, with a Python builder for Linux-based cross-compilation. Used by red teamers; a convenient loader-generation workflow. (GPL-3.0)
- [SigPloit](https://github.com/SigPloiter/SigPloit) - Telecom signaling exploitation framework covering SS7, GTP, Diameter and SIP protocols for testing mobile operator interconnects. Used by telecom security researchers; a multi-protocol signaling testbed. (MIT)
- [Singularity of Origin](https://github.com/nccgroup/singularity) - DNS rebinding attack framework that rebinds the attack server's DNS name to the target machine's IP and serves payloads. Used by security researchers; a ready-made rebinding platform. (MIT)

- [SWITCHSNIPER](https://github.com/detectum-cybersecurity/SWITCHSNIPER) - Comprehensive signaling security testing framework for telecom switches and core network systems covering SS7, GTP, Diameter and SIP. Used by telecom security testers; a broad core-network testing suite. (NOASSERTION)
- [SysWhispers](https://github.com/jthuraisamy/SysWhispers) - Syscall generator for evasion, enabling direct system calls to bypass user-mode hooks placed by EDR solutions. Used by red teamers and malware researchers; a foundational direct-syscall tool. (Apache-2.0)
- [TLS-Scanner](https://github.com/tls-attacker/TLS-Scanner) - Open-source tool for analyzing TLS configurations in 5G core networks, checking for padding oracle, Bleichenbacher and POODLE vulnerabilities. Used by telecom security testers; a specialized TLS scanner. (Apache-2.0)
- [tplmap](https://github.com/epinna/tplmap) - Detection and exploitation tool for code and server-side template injection vulnerabilities. Used by pentesters and bug-bounty hunters; a leading SSTI exploitation framework. (GPL-3.0)
- [trape](https://github.com/jofpin/trape) - Open-source session-based person tracking and reconnaissance framework. Used by penetration testers and OSINT investigators; a powerful tracking and exploitation toolkit. (MIT)

- [UpClaw](https://github.com/okdkebm/UpClaw) - AI-driven web pentest CLI in a single zero-dependency Python file with 29 built-in checks, 16 tool adapters, and evidence reports. Used by pentesters; a portable offensive testing tool. (Apache-2.0)
- [uxss-db](https://github.com/Metnew/uxss-db) - Collection of UXSS CVEs with proof-of-concept code. Used by browser security researchers; a focused universal XSS PoC database. (MIT)
- [Vega](https://github.com/subgraph/Vega) - GUI-based web vulnerability scanner for crawling sites and probing for common injection and misconfiguration flaws. Used by web pentesters and security testers; a graphical scanner for interactive assessments. (GPL-2.0)
- [Vigolium](https://github.com/vigolium/vigolium) - High-fidelity vulnerability scanner fusing agentic AI with native speed, modularity and precision. Used by security researchers and pentesters; a modern scanning engine from a known author.
- [VulnHub Binary Exploitation Repos](https://github.com/TheMalwareGuardian/Binary-Exploitation) - Curated collection of vulnerable binaries, CVE walkthroughs, and exploitation notes. Used by security students and CTF players; a hands-on binary-exploitation learning repo. (GPL-3.0)
- [Vulnify](https://vulnify.app/) - Web application security scanner for identifying vulnerabilities, security misconfigurations, and OWASP Top 10 risks with actionable remediation. Used by web security testers; a commercial scanner offering a free tier. (free tier)
- [Wakaru](https://github.com/pionxzh/wakaru) - JavaScript decompiler that splits production bundles from webpack, esbuild, Browserify, and Metro into modules and de-minifies code. Used by analysts; a bundle reconstruction tool. (Apache-2.0)
- [webshell](https://github.com/tennc/webshell) - Open-source collection of webshells for various languages and platforms. Used by red teamers and pentesters; a reference repository of web-based implants. (MIT)
- [Webshell-Sniper](https://github.com/WangYihang/Webshell-Sniper) - Terminal tool to manage a compromised website via webshell interaction. Used by penetration testers; a post-exploitation webshell management utility. (GPL-3.0)
- [Weevely](https://github.com/epinna/weevely3) - Weaponized web shell providing a stealthy backdoor and post-exploitation modules over HTTP. Used by pentesters and red teamers; a modular webshell framework. (GPL-3.0)

- [XSRFProbe](https://github.com/0xInfection/XSRFProbe) - CSRF audit and exploitation toolkit that scans applications for cross-site request forgery weaknesses. Used by web pentesters and bug-bounty hunters; a dedicated CSRF testing suite. (GPL-3.0)
- [xssor2](https://github.com/evilcos/xssor2) - XSS exploitation toolkit that lets testers hack with JavaScript payloads in browser contexts. Used by web pentesters and bug-bounty hunters; a classic XSS research utility. (BSD-2-Clause)
- [XSStrike](https://github.com/s0md3v/XSStrike) - Cross-site scripting detection and exploitation tool with advanced payload generation and fuzzing. Used by web pentesters; a capable XSS-focused scanner. (GPL-3.0)

- [Rubeus](https://github.com/GhostPack/Rubeus) - Kerberos toolkit: roast, pass-the-key, diamond tickets. (BSD-3-Clause)
