## 🛡️ The Bastion — Hardening & Defensive Infrastructure Tools
Intro: Lock it down before they get in — auditing, hardening and host/network defense tooling.

- [CrowdSec](https://github.com/crowdsecurity/crowdsec) - Collaborative behavior-detection engine with a global IP-reputation network; the modern Fail2ban, 60x faster and IPv6-ready. Used by server administrators and hosting providers; the open-source, crowdsourced alternative to commercial WAF and bot-management services.
- [Fail2ban](https://github.com/fail2ban/fail2ban) - The classic log-watching IPS that bans brute-forcing IPs; still the default answer on every Linux server. Used by sysadmins on millions of servers to stop brute force; the classic free guard no commercial tool needed to replace. (GPL-2.0)
- [Lynis](https://github.com/CISOfy/lynis) - Agentless system auditing and hardening with CIS/PCI-DSS-aligned findings; runs anywhere in minutes. Used by system auditors running CIS-style hardening checks; the open-source alternative to commercial compliance scanners for Linux/Unix hosts. (GPL-3.0)
- [OpenSnitch](https://github.com/evilsocket/opensnitch) - Interactive application firewall (Little Snitch for Linux): allow/deny every outbound connection per process. Used by Linux power users and admins wanting per-application firewall control; the open equivalent of Little Snitch on macOS. (GPL-3.0)
- [testssl.sh](https://github.com/drwetter/testssl.sh) - Command-line TLS/SSL configuration auditor: protocols, ciphers, chains, vulnerabilities, grading — no agent required. Used by TLS auditors and pentesters everywhere; the free command-line alternative to Qualys SSL Labs for server-side TLS grading. (GPL-2.0)
- [ComplianceAsCode content](https://github.com/ComplianceAsCode/content) - The open SCAP/Baseline rule corpus for RHEL, Ubuntu and friends; the content that powers OpenSCAP-style audits. Used by compliance and platform teams applying SCAP hardening profiles; the open-source ruleset commercial compliance products build on.
- [ssh-audit](https://github.com/jtesta/ssh-audit) - SSH server configuration auditor: algorithms, keys and best-practice flags in one run. Used by server hardening teams to audit SSH configurations and algorithms; the standard free SSH auditor. (GPL-3.0)
- [OSSEC](https://github.com/ossec/ossec-hids) - The original open-source HIDS: log analysis, file integrity and rootkit detection (Wazuh's ancestor). Used by SOC teams on a budget for host intrusion detection; the veteran open HIDS that predates commercial EDR. (GPL-2.0)
- [Coraza](https://github.com/corazawaf/coraza) - ModSecurity-compatible WAF engine in Go; the actively developed successor for OWASP CRS deployments. Used by platform teams embedding a WAF engine inside Go services.
- [OWASP Core Rule Set](https://github.com/coreruleset/coreruleset) - The world's most-deployed WAF ruleset; battle-tested protection for web servers, tuned for ModSecurity and Coraza. Used by WAF administrators on nginx, Apache and Coraza; the free ruleset protecting more sites than most commercial WAF feeds.
- [dev-sec hardening collection](https://github.com/dev-sec/ansible-collection-hardening) - Battle-tested Ansible roles for OS, SSH, nginx and MySQL baselines; hardening as code. Used by DevOps teams enforcing server baselines as code; the open-source alternative to commercial configuration-compliance agents.
- [OPNsense](https://github.com/opnsense/core) - Open-source firewall/router distro with IDS, VPN and reporting — a pfSense successor built on HardenedBSD. Used by network administrators as a full open-source firewall/router; the business-ready free alternative to commercial UTM appliances. (BSD-2)
- [BunkerWeb](https://github.com/bunkerity/bunkerweb) - Next-gen open WAF built on nginx: HTTPS, bot mitigation and network protections on by default. Used by web admins wanting a next-gen open-source WAF in front of their sites; the self-hosted alternative to Cloudflare's managed WAF. (AGPL-3.0)
- [open-appsec](https://github.com/openappsec/openappsec) - ML-driven prevention engine that stops OWASP Top 10 and zero-day web attacks without signatures. Used by security teams deploying machine-learning WAF protection; an open-source alternative to commercial ML-based WAF products.
- [OpenRASP](https://github.com/baidu/openrasp) - Runtime application self-protection: instrument the app itself so attacks get blocked from the inside. Used by AppSec teams adding runtime self-protection to apps; the open-source alternative to commercial RASP vendors.

- [OpenVPN](https://github.com/OpenVPN/openvpn) - The industry-standard open-source VPN daemon for site-to-site and remote-access tunnels. Used by IT teams everywhere; the open-source alternative to commercial VPN gateways. (GPL-2.0 core, component exceptions)
- [stunnel](https://github.com/mtrojnar/stunnel) - Universal TLS wrapper that adds encryption to any TCP service in minutes. Used by admins adding TLS to legacy services; the free universal TLS wrapper. (GPL-2.0)
- [OpenSSH](https://github.com/openssh/openssh-portable) - The world's default secure remote-access suite: encrypted shells, tunnels and key management. Used on virtually every Linux server on earth; the open standard for secure remote access that commercial SSH servers complement, not replace. (BSD-style)
- [ClamAV](https://github.com/Cisco-Talos/clamav) - The open-source antivirus engine that powers mail gateways and file-screening pipelines everywhere. Used on countless mail gateways and Linux file screens; the AV engine many commercial mail filters embed. (GPL-2.0)
- [AIDE](https://github.com/aide/aide) - Advanced Intrusion Detection Environment: file-integrity monitoring that alerts the moment system files change. Used by hardening teams for file-integrity monitoring; the free open alternative to commercial FIM modules. (GPL-2.0)
- [Tor](https://www.torproject.org/) - The anonymity network: routed egress through thousands of volunteer relays; the standard for privacy-preserving research access. Used by privacy-focused researchers, journalists and investigators; the free anonymity network with no commercial equivalent. (BSD-3-Clause)
- [Netfilter/iptables](https://netfilter.org/) - The Linux kernel's native packet-filtering framework; the foundation every host firewall build starts from. Used by every Linux firewall admin; the kernel-level packet filter that commercial Linux firewalls wrap a UI around. (GPL-2.0)
- [SELinux](https://github.com/SELinuxProject/selinux) - Mandatory access control for Linux: policy-based confinement that survives a compromised application. Used by hardened enterprise Linux deployments; the mandatory access control standard in RHEL-family distributions. (GPL-2.0)
- [OpenBSD PF](https://www.openbsd.org/faq/pf/) - The packet filter famous for correctness and readable rules; the syntax OpenBSD, pfSense and OPNsense share. Used by OpenBSD and firewall admins; the famously audited packet filter behind many security appliances. (ISC)
- [checksec.sh](https://github.com/slimm609/checksec.sh) - One-shot binary hardening audit: NX, canaries, PIE, RELRO and fortify flags in a single run. Used by exploit developers and binary auditors checking hardening mitigations; the standard free one-liner for this job. (BSD-3-Clause)
- [Vuls](https://github.com/future-architect/vuls) - Agentless vulnerability scanner for servers and containers, with CVE reporting across Linux and FreeBSD fleets. Used by server admins for agentless vulnerability scanning; the open-source alternative to Nessus for host patch audits. (GPL-3.0)
- [SpamAssassin](https://spamassassin.apache.org/) - The veteran open-source spam filter; bayesian scoring and rule engine at the mail gateway. Used by mail administrators on countless gateways; the veteran open-source anti-spam engine many commercial filters license. (Apache-2.0)
- [Fleet](https://github.com/fleetdm/fleet) - Osquery fleet management: device visibility and policy enforcement at scale, open core with a free tier. Used by IT and security teams managing osquery fleets at scale; the open-source alternative to commercial device management and EDR consoles. (MIT, open core)
- [shellfirm](https://github.com/kaplanelad/shellfirm) - A pre-execution guard for your shell: challenges dangerous commands (rm -rf, kubectl delete) before they run. Used by engineers protecting themselves from dangerous commands; a lightweight open guardrail before your shell executes rm -rf. (Apache-2.0)
- [Qubes OS](https://www.qubes-os.org/) - The security-by-compartmentalization desktop OS; " reasonably secure" by architecture, not by antivirus. Used by journalists, researchers and high-risk users; the compartmentalized security desktop with no commercial counterpart. (GPL-2.0)

- [Neo23x0 auditd config](https://github.com/Neo23x0/auditd) - Florian Roth's hardened auditd rule set: the Linux logging baseline used across incident-response teams. Used by blue teams hardening Linux audit collection; the widely-forked open auditd baseline behind many SOC deployments. (Apache-2.0)
- [unmask](https://github.com/unmask-sh/unmask) - Self-hosted privacy-first bot management gateway with verified-crawler bypass. Used by teams filtering bot traffic without third-party SaaS; the self-hosted bot-defense gateway. (Apache-2.0)
- [OpenNHP](https://github.com/OpenNHP/opennhp) - Go implementation of the Cloud Security Alliance's Network-infrastructure Hiding Protocol: conceals ports, IPs and domains behind a cryptographically signed knock gate — services stay invisible until the client proves trust. Used by zero-trust teams making infrastructure undiscoverable to mass and AI-driven scanning; the reference open implementation of CSA NHP. (Apache-2.0)
- [Artillery](https://github.com/trustedsec/artillery) - Blue team tool designed to protect Linux and Windows systems through multiple defensive methods. Used by defenders and system administrators; a hardening and detection utility from TrustedSec.
- [CISO Assistant](https://github.com/intuitem/ciso-assistant-community) - Open-source GRC platform simplifying governance, risk, and compliance management while supporting over 70 frameworks worldwide. Used by security and compliance teams; a modern framework-agnostic GRC. (MIT)
- [Credential Guard](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/security/identity-protection/credential-guard/) - Windows security feature isolating LSASS secrets via virtualization-based security, making credential extraction significantly harder. Used by Windows admins; a built-in hardening control. (free, proprietary)
- [Csper](https://csper.io) - Set of tools for building, evaluating and monitoring Content-Security-Policy to prevent and detect cross-site scripting. Used by web security engineers; a practical CSP management suite. (free tier)
- [DOMPurify](https://github.com/cure53/DOMPurify) - Fast, tolerant DOM-only sanitizer for HTML, MathML and SVG that prevents XSS. Used by web developers worldwide; the de facto browser sanitizer library. (Apache-2.0)
- [Eramba](https://www.eramba.org/) - Open-source GRC software providing governance, risk management, and compliance tools with a community-driven edition. Used by compliance teams; a broad GRC platform. (GPL-3.0)
- [FCaptcha](https://github.com/WebDecoy/FCaptcha) - Self-hosted CAPTCHA with behavioral analysis, vision-AI agent detection, headless-browser fingerprinting and proof-of-work. Used by web developers; a modern bot-defense component. (MIT)
- [Fort Firewall](https://github.com/tnodir/fort) - Windows-only open-source firewall built on the Windows Filtering Platform with app groups and wildcard path matching. Used by Windows users; a lightweight host firewall alternative. (GPL-3.0)
- [GovReady-Q](https://github.com/GovReady/govready-q) - Self-service open-source GRC tool automating security assessments and compliance documentation for frameworks like SOC 2 and ISO. Used by compliance teams; a documentation automation platform. (Apache-2.0)
- [GrapheneOS](https://grapheneos.org/) - Privacy and security-hardened Android OS for Pixel devices with no Google services by default and a hardened memory allocator. Used by mobile security enthusiasts; a hardened mobile platform. (MIT)
- [Hardenix](https://github.com/murat-akpinar/hardenix) - OpenSCAP-powered Linux hardening CLI combining compliance scanning with Lynis second-opinion audits and OVAL-based CVE detection. Used by Linux admins; a practical hardening workflow. (GPL-3.0)
- [IPFire](https://www.ipfire.org/) - Hardened Linux firewall splitting networks into color-coded trust zones (LAN, DMZ, wireless, internet). Used by network admins; a security-first perimeter firewall. (GPL-2.0)
- [js-xss](https://github.com/leizongmin/js-xss) - JavaScript library that sanitizes untrusted HTML against a whitelist to prevent XSS. Used by web developers; a widely adopted client-side sanitizer.
- [LAPS (Local Administrator Password Solution)](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-server/identity/laps/laps-overview) - Microsoft solution managing local administrator passwords on domain-joined computers, enforcing uniqueness and limiting lateral movement. Used by Windows admins; a built-in hardening tool. (free, proprietary)
- [Microsoft Defender for Endpoint](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/security/business/endpoint-security/microsoft-defender-endpoint) - Enterprise endpoint detection and response platform bundled with Microsoft 365 subscriptions. Used by enterprise security teams; a mature EDR option for Microsoft-centric organizations. (free tier)
- [MONARC](https://www.monarc.lu/) - Open-source risk analysis tool mapping assets, threats, and vulnerabilities for security assessments; used by risk managers and auditors. (GPL-3.0)
- [OpenRMF](https://www.openrmf.io/) - Open-source Risk Management Framework tool focused on managing NIST RMF compliance and assessments with automated compliance checklists. Used by federal and enterprise assessors; a NIST-aligned GRC tool. (Apache-2.0)
- [OpenSCAP](https://www.open-scap.org/) - Reference implementation of the SCAP standard, NIST-certified and built into RHEL and derivatives. Used by compliance teams; the standard baseline for automated configuration scanning. (LGPL-2.1)
- [Owlyshield](https://github.com/HydraDragonAntivirus/Owlyshield) - Open-source EDR framework in Rust for Linux and Windows servers that analyzes process file behavior. Used by defenders and researchers; a lightweight open endpoint detection option. (EUPL-1.2)
- [Pavois](https://github.com/stephrobert/pavois) - Linux compliance and hardening tool over CINC/InSpec producing A-E grades with multi-standard mappings including CIS and ANSSI BP-028. Used by compliance engineers; a graded hardening assessor. (Apache-2.0)
- [pfSense](https://www.pfsense.org/) - Widely deployed open-source FreeBSD-based firewall with the largest community and extensive third-party documentation. Used by network admins; a mature perimeter security platform. (Apache-2.0)
- [Pompelmi](https://github.com/pompelmi/pompelmi) - In-process file-upload security middleware for Node.js that scans untrusted uploads for malware, MIME spoofing and risks. Used by Node developers; a practical upload-hardening library. (ISC)
- [Portmaster](https://safing.io/portmaster/) - PC-based firewall suite hooking into the network stack via WFP on Windows and nfqueue/eBPF on Linux. Used by privacy-conscious users; a granular per-app network firewall. (GPL-3.0)
- [Samhain](https://launchpad.net/ubuntu/stonking/+source/samhain) - Open-source multiplatform HIDS providing file integrity checking, log monitoring, rootkit detection, and port monitoring. Used by sysadmins; a long-standing host intrusion detection tool.
- [Sentinel Antivirus](https://github.com/mukti-sys/sentinel-antivirus) - Lightweight open-source antivirus engine for Windows with YARA scanning, PE machine-learning classification, and a kernel minifilter driver. Used by defenders and tinkerers; a compact free endpoint scanner. (MIT)
- [SigFW](https://github.com/P1sec/SigFW) - Open-source signaling firewall for SS7 and Diameter filtering with anti-spoofing and anti-sniffing capabilities. Used by telecom operators and security engineers; a defensive signaling filter. (AGPL-3.0)
- [SimpleRisk](https://www.simplerisk.com/) - Lightweight open-source GRC platform focused on risk management for SMEs needing straightforward risk tracking. Used by small security teams; a simple governance and risk tool. (MPL-2.0)
- [sqlchop](https://sqlchop.chaitin.cn/) - SQL injection detection engine offered as a web service by Chaitin. Used by defenders and developers; a hosted SQLi detection check. (free, proprietary)
- [Subgraph OS](https://subgraph.com/) - Secure Linux distribution with a hardened kernel for privacy and resistance. Used by security-conscious users; a hardened desktop option.
- [Ubuntu Security Guide (USG)](https://documentation.ubuntu.com/security/compliance/usg/) - Canonical's built-in compliance tooling for Ubuntu providing CIS Benchmark profiles at Level 1 and Level 2 for workstations and servers. Used by Ubuntu admins; free automated hardening guidance. (free, proprietary)
- [UUSEC WAF](https://github.com/Safe3/uusec-waf/) - Open-source web application firewall and API security gateway from UUCORP. Used by defenders and sysadmins; a free WAF option for protecting web services. (BSD-2-Clause)
- [VyOS](https://vyos.io/) - Open-source network operating system providing routing alongside firewall features with a Juniper-like CLI. Used by network engineers; a capable open routing and firewall platform. (GPL-2.0)
- [Wazuh SCA (Security Configuration Assessment)](https://wazuh.com/) - Built-in compliance assessment module within the Wazuh SIEM performing policy monitoring and configuration checks against CIS Benchmarks. Used by SOC teams; free configuration assessment inside an open platform. (GPL-2.0)
- [WebDecoy](https://github.com/WebDecoy/wordpress-plugin) - Zero-configuration WordPress bot-detection plugin combining WebDriver detection, headless fingerprinting, behavioral analysis and CAPTCHA. Used by WordPress site owners; a simple bot-defense add-on. (GPL-2.0)
- [xsschop](https://xsschop.chaitin.cn/) - XSS detection engine offered as a web service by Chaitin. Used by defenders and developers; a hosted cross-site scripting detection check. (free, proprietary)

## 🔭 The Watchtower — Threat Intelligence Tools & Feeds
Intro: See what's coming — intel platforms, knowledge bases, ransomware trackers and exposure watchtowers.

- [MISP](https://github.com/MISP/MISP) - The de-facto open threat-sharing platform: events, attributes, galaxies and thousands of community feeds. Used by CERTs and threat-intel teams worldwide; the leading open-source alternative to commercial threat-intelligence platforms like Recorded Future and ThreatQ. (AGPL-3.0)
- [OpenCTI](https://github.com/OpenCTI-Platform/OpenCTI) - Threat-intel knowledge graph built on STIX2; analyst-first investigation dashboards instead of raw feeds. Used by CTI teams building knowledge graphs of threat data; the open-source alternative to commercial intel platforms with graph UIs.
- [MITRE ATT&CK](https://attack.mitre.org) - The adversary tactics & techniques knowledge base that the entire industry maps detections and emulations against. Used by red teams, defenders and vendors as the shared language of adversary behavior; the free framework the whole industry maps to.
- [IntelOwl](https://github.com/intelowlproject/IntelOwl) - Bulk-analyze observables across 100+ online services from one API and web UI. Used by SOC analysts enriching observables in bulk; the open-source alternative to commercial one-click threat-inquiry APIs. (AGPL-3.0)
- [SpiderFoot](https://github.com/smicallef/spiderfoot) - Open-source OSINT automation with 200+ data sources; attack-surface and exposure monitoring out of the box. Used by OSINT investigators automating recon; the free self-hosted version of the SpiderFoot HX service.
- [Uncover](https://github.com/projectdiscovery/uncover) - Query exposed services across Shodan, Censys and friends in one sweep — the instant exposure check. Used by recon teams querying exposure across search engines at once; the free aggregator for Shodan-style lookups.
- [Yeti](https://github.com/yeti-platform/yeti) - Organize your threat intel: observables, indicators and TTPs in one searchable, API-first platform. Used by CTI analysts organizing indicators and TTPs; the open-source threat-intel tracker for teams without commercial TIP budgets.
- [RansomLook](https://github.com/RansomLook/RansomLook) - Open ransomware-group leak-site tracker with historical victim data (see also the live stats at ransomware.live). Used by CTI analysts tracking ransomware leak sites; the free watchtower the commercial feeds also scrape. (GPL-3.0)
- [Malpedia](https://malpedia.caad.fkie.fraunhofer.de) - Curated malware encyclopedia: families, actors, papers and sample references for researchers. Used by malware researchers as the reference index of families and papers; the free, vendor-neutral encyclopedia.
- [firehol/blocklist-ipsets](https://github.com/firehol/blocklist-ipsets) - Aggregated, daily-updated IP blocklists (attacks, spam, abuse) ready for iptables/fail2ban; check each list's own license before redistribution. Used by firewall admins feeding blocklists; the community-maintained alternative to commercial IP-reputation feeds.
- [IntelMQ](https://github.com/certtools/intelmq) - The feed plumber's dream: collect, normalize and route threat-intel feeds from hundreds of sources into your stack. Used by national CERTs automating feed ingestion; the open-source alternative to commercial intel-pipeline tooling. (AGPL-3.0)
- [NetworkMiner](https://www.netresec.com/?page=NetworkMiner) - Passive network-forensics analyzer: extracts files, credentials and sessions from recorded traffic without touching the live network. Used by DFIR analysts for passive network forensics and artifact extraction; the free alternative to commercial network-forensics suites. (free, proprietary)
- [ZDI advisories](https://www.zerodayinitiative.com/advisories/published/) - Published zero-day advisories from the Zero Day Initiative; the disclosure record for enterprise-software flaws. Used by patch-priority teams tracking pre-disclosure zero-days; the free advisory stream competing with vendor feeds. (free service)
- [Emerging Threats Open](http://doc.emergingthreats.net/bin/view/Main/EmergingFAQ) - The open Suricata/Snort rule feed from Proofpoint; thousands of community-maintained detection rules. Used by Suricata and Snort defenders everywhere; the free ruleset that the commercial ET Pro subscription extends. (free feed)
- [http-detection-agent](https://github.com/ai-blueteam/http-detection-agent) - Traffic anomaly detection for the blue team: labels HTTP datasets without shipping them off-box. Used by recon pipelines fingerprinting web servers at scale; a focused open utility for asset inventories. (MIT)
- [vx-underground](https://vx-underground.org) - The malware corpus and INT report archive; free, massive and respected across the research community. Used by malware researchers studying live collections; the free sample and paper archive with no commercial gate. (free service)
- [ICHI — Internet Cyber Health Index](https://ichi.global) - Free, source-transparent daily barometer of internet cyber health, aggregating threat intelligence, security news, and telemetry from 20+ named public sources. Used by SOC analysts and security researchers for daily situational awareness of global cyber conditions; the free daily-refresh alternative to scattered vendor intelligence portals. (free service)

- [cve-search](https://github.com/cve-search/cve-search) - Self-hosted CVE/CPE database in MongoDB with web UI, API and CLI. Used by orgs (incl. CIRCL's former public CVE service) to run local, rate-limit-free vulnerability lookups; strict/lax CPE matching, 14 yrs of maintenance. (AGPL-3.0)
- [OpenCVE](https://github.com/opencve/opencve) - Self-hosted vulnerability intelligence platform: it indexes CVEs and notifies on the products and keywords you follow. Used by security teams tracking new CVEs for their asset inventory; the self-hosted alternative to commercial CVE feeds. (Apache-2.0)
- [CVE2CAPEC](https://github.com/Galeax/CVE2CAPEC) - Maps CVEs through CWE to CAPEC, MITRE ATT&CK, D3FEND and ATLAS techniques with a daily-updated database; showcased at BlackHat Europe 2025 Arsenal. Used by threat-intel analysts turning CVE lists into technique-level attack context; the free CVE-to-technique enrichment engine.. (GPL-3.0)
- [CrawlerScope](https://github.com/ipanalytics/CrawlerScope) - Operator-published crawler and AI-bot CIDR dataset for bot attribution. Used by defenders verifying whether traffic is a legitimate crawler; the public dataset for bot-network attribution. (CC0)
- [CERT-FR (ANSSI)](https://www.cert.ssi.gouv.fr/) - French national cybersecurity agency's advisories and alerts on vulnerabilities and threats; used by defenders tracking European intelligence. (free, proprietary)
- [Check Point Research](https://research.checkpoint.com/) - Check Point Research blog covering APT campaigns, vulnerability discoveries, and attack trends; read by threat intelligence analysts. (free, proprietary)
- [CISA Advisories](https://www.cisa.gov/cybersecurity-advisories) - CISA's official alerts, advisories, and Known Exploited Vulnerabilities catalog; used by defenders worldwide to prioritize patching. (free, proprietary)
- [Cisco Talos Intelligence](https://blog.talosintelligence.com/) - Cisco Talos threat research blog covering vulnerabilities, malware campaigns, and threat actor activity; used by defenders for intelligence. (free, proprietary)
- [CrowdStrike Blog](https://www.crowdstrike.com/blog/) - CrowdStrike's vendor blog with investigative research on threats, vulnerabilities, and APT groups; used by defenders for threat intelligence. (free, proprietary)
- [EQGRP](https://github.com/x0rz/EQGRP) - Decrypted content of the EQGRP auction file attributed to offensive tooling. Used by researchers and defenders; a historical artifact for threat analysis.
- [Fortinet Threat Research](https://www.fortinet.com/blog/threat-research) - FortiGuard Labs research blog covering emerging threats, malware analysis, and vulnerability intelligence; read by security operations teams. (free, proprietary)
- [Google Online Security Blog](https://security.googleblog.com/) - Google's security team blog covering vulnerabilities, security features, and threat research; read by practitioners and researchers. (free, proprietary)
- [Google Project Zero](https://googleprojectzero.blogspot.com/) - Google Project Zero's blog publishing deep technical analyses of zero-day vulnerabilities and exploitation techniques; read by elite researchers. (free, proprietary)
- [IBM X-Force Exchange](https://exchange.xforce.ibmcloud.com/) - Free community threat intelligence research platform offering IOC search and malware analysis data. Used by CTI analysts and researchers; a long-standing free exchange for threat data. (free tier)
- [Malwarebytes Labs](https://www.malwarebytes.com/blog) - Malwarebytes Labs blog covering malware research, threat analysis, and security news; read by practitioners tracking emerging threats. (free, proprietary)
- [Mandiant Blog](https://www.mandiant.com/resources/blog) - Mandiant's research blog providing frontline threat intelligence and incident response insights; used by defenders tracking major breaches. (free, proprietary)
- [MITRE ATT&CK Navigator](https://mitre-attack.github.io/attack-navigator/) - Coverage mapping tool visualizing detection and hunting coverage against the ATT&CK matrix, with an MCP server for AI assistance. Used by defenders; the standard ATT&CK coverage mapper.
- [NSFOCUS](https://nti.nsfocus.com/) - NSFOCUS threat intelligence portal providing IOC and threat data. Used by SOC analysts and defenders; a vendor threat intel resource. (free tier)
- [SANS Internet Storm Center](https://isc.sans.edu/) - Daily diary of security incidents and handler insights from SANS. Used by defenders and threat analysts; a trusted community resource for real-time threat awareness.
- [Securelist (Kaspersky)](https://securelist.com/) - Kaspersky's research blog with deep malware analysis, APT tracking, and threat intelligence; used by researchers and defenders. (free, proprietary)
- [SentinelLabs](https://www.sentinelone.com/labs/) - SentinelOne's research division publishing threat intelligence, malware analysis, and offensive security research; used by defenders and researchers. (free, proprietary)
- [ssltest](https://www.ssllabs.com/ssltest/) - Online service performing deep analysis of SSL web server configuration. Used by sysadmins and security testers; the standard TLS configuration grader. (free, proprietary)
- [The Record by Recorded Future](https://therecord.media/) - Intelligence-driven reporting on attackers, vulnerabilities, and global security incidents backed by Recorded Future. Used by defenders and analysts; a reliable news source for threat awareness.
- [threat-intel (GitHub)](https://github.com/phutran1210dev/threat-intel) - Real-time threat intelligence aggregation and visualization platform with IOC tracking, actor profiling and Elasticsearch/Kibana dashboards. Used by SOC analysts and CTI teams; an open-source aggregation stack. (MIT)
- [ThreatFox](https://threatfox.abuse.ch/) - Free community IOC platform by abuse.ch for searching domains, IPs and file hashes against known malware infrastructure. Used by threat analysts and SOC teams; a constantly updated IOC lookup. (free, proprietary)
- [ThreatHunting-Keywords](https://github.com/mthcht/ThreatHunting-Keywords) - Massive database of keywords, artifacts, and patterns organized by offensive tool, ATT&CK technique, and log type with ready queries. Used by threat hunters; a practical detection keyword reference.
- [Trust Scan](https://github.com/undeadlist/trust-scan) - URL security scanner combining threat intelligence feeds like URLhaus, PhishTank and Spamhaus with 40+ scam and phishing pattern checks. Used by defenders and analysts; a practical phishing triage helper. (MIT)
- [VulnWatch](https://vulnwatch.tech/) - Cybersecurity platform dedicated to tracking vulnerabilities, monitoring emerging security threats, and providing insights. Used by security analysts and defenders; a free vulnerability and threat tracking service. (free, proprietary)
- [WhiteIntel](https://whiteintel.io/) - Dark web monitoring service tracking exposed data and credentials across underground sources. Used by defenders and organizations; a free-tier early warning for leaked credentials. (free tier)
- [Zero Day Initiative (ZDI)](https://www.zerodayinitiative.com/) - Trend Micro's bug bounty program publishing vulnerability advisories and zero-day disclosures; used by defenders tracking unpatched flaws. (free, proprietary)

## 🗺️ The Cartographers — Recon, OSINT & Attack-Surface Mapping Tools
Intro: Map the terrain before you move — subdomain, content and OSINT discovery for bug bounty and asset management.

- [Amass](https://github.com/owasp-amass/amass) - Deep attack-surface mapping: DNS enumeration, scraping, archives, APIs and permutations in one OWASP project. Used by recon teams and bug bounty hunters for deep subdomain enumeration; the OWASP project that anchors most serious attack-surface pipelines.
- [subfinder](https://github.com/projectdiscovery/subfinder) - Fast, passive subdomain discovery from dozens of sources; the standard first command of any engagement. Used by bug bounty hunters for fast passive subdomain discovery; the ProjectDiscovery staple of recon automation.
- [katana](https://github.com/projectdiscovery/katana) - Next-generation crawler with headless-browser support for JavaScript-heavy applications. Used by bug hunters and pentesters crawling modern JS-heavy apps; the ProjectDiscovery successor to legacy crawlers in recon pipelines.
- [theHarvester](https://github.com/laramies/theHarvester) - Emails, subdomains, hosts and names from public sources — the OSINT starter kit. Used by pentesters in the recon phase harvesting emails, names and subdomains; the veteran free OSINT gatherer. (GPL-2.0)
- [httpx](https://github.com/projectdiscovery/httpx) - The HTTP prober: filter thousands of hosts by status, title, tech, CDN and TLS in a single pass. Used by bug bounty hunters triaging alive hosts from mass subdomain lists; the ProjectDiscovery prober in nearly every recon pipeline.
- [bbot](https://github.com/blacklanternsecurity/bbot) - Recursive, modular OSINT/scanning framework — spider the whole attack surface with one command. Used by attack-surface teams running modular, recursive recon; the open-source framework rivaling commercial ASM discovery. (AGPL-3.0)
- [ARL-Next](https://github.com/owl234/ARL-Next) - Attack-surface reconnaissance platform combining subdomain enumeration, port scanning, fingerprinting and a nuclei PoC pipeline with task scheduling. Used by attack-surface teams running scheduled recon over large asset lists; the actively maintained continuation of the ARL recon platform. (GPL-3.0)
- [targetfinder](https://github.com/NaroChatyan/targetfinder) - Self-hosted bug bounty scope tracker that watches new programs, targets, fee and reward changes across HackerOne, Bugcrowd, Intigriti, YesWeHack, HackenProof and Sherlock — CLI, HTTP API and MCP server, no API keys. Used by bug bounty hunters catching scope changes the moment they happen; the free alternative to paid program-monitoring services.. (MIT)
- [feroxbuster](https://github.com/epi052/feroxbuster) - Fast recursive content discovery in Rust with great heuristics and recursion handling. Used by web pentesters for fast recursive content discovery; the Rust-based favorite alongside ffuf and gobuster.
- [reKover](https://github.com/BRuteLogic/reKover) - Stealth hybrid URL mapper that merges wayback-machine history with wordlist probing to recover hidden and forgotten paths. Used by bug bounty hunters recovering forgotten endpoints and legacy paths; the zero-dependency bash alternative to heavy content-discovery suites. (MIT per README)

- [ffuf](https://github.com/ffuf/ffuf) - The fast web fuzzer everyone uses for content, parameter and vhost discovery. Used by web pentesters as the de facto standard free web fuzzer.
- [GHunt](https://github.com/mxrch/GHunt) - Investigate a Google account's public footprint (photos, reviews, services) from an email — for authorized OSINT; respect ToS. Used by OSINT investigators profiling Google accounts from public artifacts; the open alternative to commercial people-intel tools. (AGPL-3.0)
- [sherlock](https://github.com/sherlock-project/sherlock) - Hunt one username across 400+ social networks; people-OSINT in seconds. Used by OSINT investigators checking username presence across hundreds of platforms; the standard free account-discovery tool.
- [naabu](https://github.com/projectdiscovery/naabu) - Fast, SYN-based port scanner built to be pipelined, not watched. Used by bug bounty hunters for SYN port scanning; the ProjectDiscovery port scanner in recon pipelines.
- [dnsx](https://github.com/projectdiscovery/dnsx) - Multi-purpose DNS toolkit: reverse lookups, wildcard detection, response filtering. Used by recon teams for DNS querying and reverse lookups at scale; the ProjectDiscovery DNS multitool.
- [massdns](https://github.com/blechschmidt/massdns) - High-performance DNS stub resolver for bulk subdomain brute-forcing at scale. Used by recon pipelines resolving millions of subdomains; the free high-performance resolver no commercial tool beats on price. (GPL-3.0)
- [subsonar](https://github.com/softstaticx/subsonar) - Ultra-fast asynchronous subdomain and web-interface scanner with anonymous DNS resolution and 0-100 confidence scores that explain every verdict. Used by recon teams enumerating live web surfaces quickly; the confidence-scored free subdomain scanner.. (MIT)
- [gobuster](https://github.com/OJ/gobuster) - The classic directory/DNS/vhost buster in Go; the tool feroxbuster and ffuf grew from. Used by pentesters for directory, DNS and vhost brute-forcing; the veteran free alternative to commercial content scanners.
- [gau](https://github.com/lc/gau) - Fetch URLs indexed by AlienVault OTX, the Wayback Machine and Common Crawl. Used by bug bounty hunters pulling all known URLs from public archives; the free fetcher that seeds most recon pipelines.
- [waymore](https://github.com/xnl-h4ck3r/waymore) - Pull way more from the Wayback Machine: full responses, not just URLs. Used by bug hunters mining the Wayback Machine deeply for hidden endpoints and parameters; the successor to gau for archive URL intel.
- [Maltego CE](https://www.maltego.com) - The standard OSINT graphing tool for entity-relationship investigation; free community edition. Used by OSINT analysts and investigators for link analysis; the free community edition of the commercial Maltego. (free tier)

- [CloakQuest3r](https://github.com/spyboy-productions/CloakQuest3r) - Unmask the real origin IP behind Cloudflare and similar CDNs using historical DNS, SSL certificates and subdomain correlation. Used by security teams verifying true origin exposure before hardening allowlists; the open-source alternative to commercial CDN-unmasking services. (MIT)

- [ParamSpider](https://github.com/devanshbatham/ParamSpider) - Mines archived URLs and parameters from the Wayback Machine to seed injection testing. Used by bug hunters mining hidden parameters from archives; the free parameter-mining step before injection testing.
- [recon-ng](https://github.com/lanmaster53/recon-ng) - The other recon framework: fully modular, database-backed web recon with a Metasploit-style console. Used by pentesters running structured recon workflows; the Metasploit-style framework for the recon phase. (GPL-3.0)
- [reNgine](https://github.com/yogeshojha/rengine) - Automated web reconnaissance framework with highly configurable scan engines, recon-data correlation, continuous monitoring and a clean web UI. Used by bug bounty hunters running repeatable, scheduled recon; the self-hosted alternative to commercial ASM platforms for web recon.. (GPL-3.0)
- [FinalRecon](https://github.com/thewhiteh4t/FinalRecon) - All-in-one web recon: headers, whois, SSL, DNS, subdomains, ports, directories and traceroute in a single modular run. Used by pentesters running a fast first-pass recon sweep before deep-dive tooling; the modular Python alternative to juggling ten single-purpose scripts. (MIT)
- [BloodRecon](https://github.com/MrHacker-X/BloodRecon) - Automated recon pipeline that chains subdomain discovery, JavaScript endpoint extraction and a nuclei handoff with Telegram notifications. Used by bug bounty hunters automating the recon-to-scan handoff; the one-command recon pipeline for bounty workflows. (MIT)
- [dnsrecon](https://github.com/darkoperator/dnsrecon) - DNS enumeration workhorse: records, zone transfers, reverse lookups and subdomain brute-forcing. Used by pentesters enumerating DNS records and zone transfers; the veteran free DNS enumeration tool. (GPL-2.0)
- [recon](https://github.com/blisspixel/recon) - Passive domain-intelligence framework with per-phase structured output for tech-stack and email-security audits. Used by auditors assembling passive domain profiles without touching the target; the structured-output recon framework for assessment prep. (Apache-2.0)
- [VHostScan](https://github.com/codingo/VHostScan) - Enumerates virtual hosts on a shared IP — discover the neighbors your target lives with. Used by pentesters discovering virtual hosts on shared infrastructure; a focused free tool for shared-hosting engagements. (GPL-3.0)
- [wfuzz](https://github.com/xmendez/wfuzz) - The web fuzzer that replaces any part of a request with a payload — parameters, headers, paths, verbs. Used by web pentesters fuzzing any part of an HTTP request; the classic free fuzzer predating ffuf. (GPL-2.0)
- [AtDork](https://github.com/amnottdevv/atdork) - Multi-engine Google-dork scraper with categorized GHDB output for authorized reconnaissance. Used by bug bounty hunters automating Google dorking for exposed targets; the free dork engine for recon. (MIT)
- [DorkCraft](https://github.com/juandresrodca/DorkCraft) - Rule-based dork generator: translates intent into search queries deterministically, zero AI, zero outbound calls. Used by recon practitioners bulk-running dork queries; the open dork-scanning alternative to paid search tooling. (MIT)
- [DorkEye](https://github.com/xPloits3c/DorkEye) - OSINT dork pipeline: email, subdomain and people discovery through structured query automation. Used by bug hunters running Google dorks via SearX; the free web-based dork launcher. (MIT)
- [Dorker](https://github.com/HexSilence/Dorker) - Cross-platform multi-engine Google-dork CLI with per-engine query translation and scoped validation. Used by OSINT hunters running one dork across multiple engines; the dork translator for cross-engine reconnaissance. (Apache-2.0)
- [Dork](https://github.com/Naqaab50/Dork) - Curated Google-dork payload library for cloud, AI, SaaS and IoT reconnaissance. Used by recon teams pulling tested dorks per target class; the categorized dork corpus for scoped discovery. (MIT)
- [DorkIntel](https://github.com/unfantasmaenelsistema/DorkIntel) - Client-side library of 162 categorized Google dorks for intelligence dashboards. Used by OSINT tool builders embedding dork queries in dashboards; the embeddable dork dataset for client-side intel tooling. (MIT)

- [EyeWitness](https://github.com/RedSiege/EyeWitness) - Screenshots, headers and key info from web servers during authorized recon passes. Used by pentesters screenshotting and triaging web servers in bulk; the standard free recon triage tool. (GPL-3.0)
- [WiGLE](https://wigle.net) - The wireless network mapping archive; queries by BSSID/SSID across millions of observed networks. Used by wireless OSINT researchers mapping networks worldwide; the free wardriving database with no commercial equal. (free service)
- [DNSdumpster](https://dnsdumpster.com) - Visual DNS reconnaissance: subdomains, MX, and infrastructure maps from public records. Used by beginners and quick-recon tasks for visual DNS mapping; the free web tool that kickstarts attack-surface discovery. (free service)
- [CeWL](https://digi.ninja/projects/cewl.php) - Custom wordlist generation by spidering target sites — the content-aware dictionary for authorized assessments. Used by pentesters building custom wordlists from target sites for password audits; the standard free custom-wordlist spider. (GPL-3.0)
- [Awesome Censys Queries](https://github.com/thehappydinoa/awesome-censys-queries) - A curated corpus of Censys search queries for internet-wide recon. Used by CTI analysts running certified Censys hunting; the community cookbook for asset-search queries. (CC-BY-4.0, curated list)
- [Recog](https://github.com/rapid7/recog) - The fingerprint database behind Metasploit and Nexpose: service banners mapped to OS and product families. Used by fingerprinting tool maintainers for service identification; the open database behind Metasploit and Rapid7 tooling. (BSD-3-Clause)
- [nerva](https://github.com/praetorian-inc/nerva) - High-performance service fingerprinting CLI identifying 170+ protocols on TCP, UDP and SCTP with safe misconfiguration checks. Used by recon teams fingerprinting services at scale without credentials; Praetorian's open-source protocol identification engine. (Apache-2.0)
- [JARM](https://github.com/salesforce/jarm) - Active TLS server fingerprint: identifies servers behind CDNs from their handshake behavior. Used by CTI analysts identifying TLS server infrastructure; the free passive fingerprint actively used in threat research. (BSD-3-Clause)
- [Merklemap](https://merklemap.com) - Subdomain discovery through certificate-transparency logs; billions of entries, searchable. Used by recon teams searching Certificate Transparency for subdomains; the free CT search joining crt.sh-style tooling. (free service)
- [subjack](https://github.com/haccer/subjack) - Subdomain-takeover scanner that flags dangling CNAMEs, stale NS delegations, orphaned A records and SPF/MX/SRV vectors across large concurrent domain lists. Used by bug bounty hunters and asset teams hunting hijackable subdomains before attackers do; the veteran takeover checker that still anchors this bug class. (MIT)
- [dirsearch](https://github.com/maurosoria/dirsearch) - The 2013-vintage web path brute-forcer with an optional Rust native backend, wordlist transformations and resumable sessions. Used by web pentesters enumerating hidden paths and endpoints for over a decade; the classic Python scanner — the lineage anchor alongside ffuf and feroxbuster. (GPL-2.0)
- [hakrawler](https://github.com/hakluke/hakrawler) - Lightweight Go crawler that eats URLs from stdin and emits discovered endpoints and JavaScript file locations up to a configurable depth. Used by bug bounty hunters seeding recon pipelines from quick crawls; hakluke's pipeline-friendly staple, packaged in Kali. (GPL-3.0)
- [reconFTW](https://github.com/six2dez/reconftw) - Fully automated recon framework orchestrating ~100 external tools (subfinder, nuclei, nmap, gitleaks…) into one configurable pipeline with OSINT, cloud-enum and Azure-tenant stages. Used by recon teams running scheduled attack-surface sweeps without hand-rolling bash; the Docker-deployed recon automation standard. (MIT)
- [lit-bb-hack-tools](https://github.com/edoardottt/lit-bb-hack-tools) - Monorepo of 26 small stdin/stdout CLI utilities that reshape and pivot recon output — subdomain-takeover checks, redirect chains, JS and parameter mining. Used by bug bounty hunters gluing tool outputs into pipelines; the unix-philosophy companion to monolithic recon suites. (GPL-3.0)
- [Assetfinder](https://github.com/tomnomnom/assetfinder) - Subdomain discovery tool that pulls results from multiple public sources. Used by recon specialists and pentesters; a fast open-source way to enumerate an organization's hosts. (MIT)
- [BuiltWith](https://builtwith.com/) - Website technology lookup and analysis service. Used by recon specialists and researchers; a free-tier way to fingerprint the stack behind a site. (free tier)
- [buster](https://github.com/sham00n/buster) - Open-source tool that checks whether an email address is registered on popular online services. Used by OSINT investigators and bug-bounty hunters; a quick account-enumeration aid. (GPL-3.0)
- [CC.LA](https://cc.la) - Free WHOIS and RDAP lookup with DNS records, name server history, IP WHOIS, and SSL certificate search. Used by recon specialists and admins; a broad free domain intelligence hub. (free, proprietary)
- [Check-Host](https://check-host.net/) - Network monitoring and diagnostic tool checking host availability, ping, HTTP, TCP, and DNS from multiple locations. Used by admins and researchers; a free way to probe reachability worldwide. (free, proprietary)
- [dirhunt](https://github.com/Nekmo/dirhunt) - Web crawler optimized for searching and analyzing a site's directory structure. Used by pentesters; an efficient content discovery tool. (MIT)
- [Discord ID Lookup](https://discord.id/) - Free lookup tool resolving Discord account details by ID. Used by OSINT investigators and researchers; a quick social-media identifier lookup aid. (free, proprietary)
- [DiscordLookup](https://discordlookup.org/) - Free lookup tool resolving Discord account details by ID. Used by OSINT investigators and researchers; a quick social-media identifier lookup aid. (free, proprietary)
- [domain_analyzer](https://github.com/eldraco/domain_analyzer) - Analyzes the security of a domain by gathering all possible information. Used by pentesters; a broad domain reconnaissance script.
- [Email Dossier](https://centralops.net/co/emaildossier.aspx) - Email domain and MX diagnostics for mail infrastructure. Used by admins and investigators; a mail configuration lookup. (free, proprietary)
- [Facebook Photo Search Engine](https://cse.google.com/cse/publicurl?cx=013991603413798772546:jyvyp2ppxma) - Free Google CSE for searching Facebook profiles and photos. Used by OSINT researchers and investigators; a quick social-media profile discovery tool. (free, proprietary)
- [Fierce](https://github.com/mschwager/fierce) - DNS reconnaissance and attack tool for mapping domains and nearby hosts. Used by pentesters and network testers; an open-source way to enumerate DNS infrastructure. (GPL-3.0)
- [Finding The Real Origin IPs Hiding Behind CloudFlare or TOR](https://www.secjuice.com/finding-real-ips-of-origin-servers-behind-cloudflare-or-tor/) - Article on finding real origin IPs hiding behind Cloudflare or TOR. Used by recon specialists; a practical guide to origin discovery.
- [FOFA](https://fofa.so/?locale=en) - Cyberspace search engine for discovering internet-facing assets and services. Used by recon specialists and researchers; a broad attack-surface search platform. (free tier)
- [GhostTrack](https://github.com/HunxByts/GhostTrack) - Open-source phone and IP lookup tool that queries free APIs. Used by OSINT investigators and bug-bounty hunters; a quick tracking and reconnaissance utility. (GPL-3.0)
- [GitMiner](https://github.com/UnkL4b/GitMiner) - Advanced mining tool for searching content and secrets across GitHub repositories. Used by OSINT analysts and bug-bounty hunters; a powerful GitHub recon engine. (GPL-3.0)
- [Google to search profiles on Dribbble](https://recruitin.net/dribbble.php) - Free Google CSE that returns real Dribbble profiles. Used by OSINT researchers and recruiters; a quick social-media profile discovery tool. (free, proprietary)
- [grayhatwarfare](https://buckets.grayhatwarfare.com/) - Search engine indexing publicly exposed cloud storage buckets for reconnaissance. Used by security researchers and bug-bounty hunters; a useful exposure-discovery resource. (free tier)
- [GSDF](https://github.com/We5ter/GSDF) - Domain searcher named GoogleSSLdomainFinder for enumerating related domains. Used by recon specialists; a Google-based domain discovery tool. (Apache-2.0)
- [HTTP Security Report](https://httpsecurityreport.com/) - Generates an instant report of how a website measures against HTTP security best practices. Used by web developers and admins; a fast header and configuration review. (free, proprietary)
- [ignorant](https://pypi.org/project/ignorant/) - Python package that checks whether a phone number is registered on real platforms. Used by OSINT investigators and bug-bounty hunters; a fast phone-enumeration tool. (free, proprietary)
- [ImmuniWeb - Website Security Test](https://www.immuniweb.com/websec/) - Tests a website for vulnerabilities, AI bot protection, HTTP security and privacy headers, DNSSEC, CSP, and compliance. Used by defenders and auditors; a broad external web check. (free tier)
- [ImmuniWeb CyberScore](https://www.immuniweb.com/cyberscore/) - Provides a free cybersecurity, privacy, and AI security rating for a company, partner, or supplier. Used by risk and procurement teams; a quick external posture score. (free tier)
- [ImmuniWeb SSL Security Test](https://www.immuniweb.com/ssl/) - Free online tool checking a website or email server's SSL/TLS configuration against security standards. Used by admins and auditors; a focused TLS compliance test. (free tier)
- [IP-Lookup.org](https://www.ip-lookup.org/) - IP lookup tool resolving geolocation, ISP, ASN, and hostname details for any address. Used by analysts and admins; a quick free way to map network origins. (free, proprietary)
- [IPLocation.net](https://www.iplocation.net/) - IP geolocation lookup service showing location, ISP, and related details for addresses. Used by analysts and admins; a free quick-reference for mapping network origins. (free, proprietary)
- [IVRE](https://github.com/ivre/ivre) - Self-hosted network recon framework for building your own Shodan or Censys alternative using Nmap, Masscan, and Zeek. Used by network researchers and attack-surface analysts; a powerful self-hosted reconnaissance platform. (GPL-3.0)
- [JoomlaScan](https://github.com/drego85/JoomlaScan) - Free scanner that enumerates components installed on Joomla CMS sites to reveal the attack surface. Used by pentesters and bug-bounty hunters; a focused CMS recon tool. (GPL-3.0)
- [KeyDrift](https://keydrift.dev) - Scans deployed HTML and JavaScript for exposed secrets while recognizing public browser credentials that are not leaks. Used by web developers and security teams; reduces false-positive secret alerts. (free tier)
- [Kismet](https://kismetwireless.net/) - Wireless network detector, sniffer, and intrusion detection system. Used by wireless analysts and wardrivers; a long-standing free passive Wi-Fi discovery platform.
- [LinkedIn Search Engine](https://cse.google.com/cse?cx=daaf18e804f81bed0) - Free Google CSE returning real LinkedIn profiles. Used by OSINT researchers and recruiters; a quick professional-profile discovery tool. (free, proprietary)
- [LinkFinder](https://github.com/GerbenJavado/LinkFinder) - Python script that finds endpoints and links hidden inside JavaScript files. Used by bug-bounty hunters and recon specialists; a popular JS endpoint discovery tool. (MIT)
- [MailTester](https://mailtester.com/) - Free web service that verifies whether an email address exists via SMTP checks. Used by OSINT researchers and marketers; a simple email-existence validation tool. (free, proprietary)
- [Marshall Extensions](https://github.com/bad-antics/marshall-extensions) - OSINT and security extensions for the Marshall privacy browser. Used by OSINT practitioners; a plugin bundle for reconnaissance.
- [Netcraft](https://www.netcraft.com/) - Website profiling and phishing detection service. Used by analysts and defenders; a free-tier way to fingerprint site infrastructure and spot suspicious hosts. (free tier)
- [OpenBuckets](https://openbuckets.io/) - Search engine for misconfigured public cloud storage buckets across providers. Used by security researchers; a fast exposure discovery service. (free, proprietary)
- [Pentest Tools - Network Vulnerability Scanner](https://pentest-tools.com/network-vulnerability-scanning/network-security-scanner-online) - Online scanner identifying vulnerabilities, misconfigurations, outdated services, and exposed ports in network infrastructure. Used by security teams; a hosted external network discovery tool. (free tier)
- [plcscan](https://github.com/meeas/plcscan) - Tool for enumerating Siemens S7 PLCs via TCP/IP or LLC networks, serving as a foundational discovery utility for ICS networks. Used by OT security assessors; a simple, reliable first step for mapping industrial assets. (GPL-3.0)
- [Raccoon](https://github.com/evyatarmeged/Raccoon) - High performance offensive security tool for reconnaissance and vulnerability scanning. Used by pentesters; a fast recon and scanning framework. (MIT)
- [ReconDog](https://github.com/s0md3v/ReconDog) - Reconnaissance Swiss Army knife gathering domain and network information. Used by pentesters; a multi-purpose recon toolkit. (Apache-2.0)
- [Reddit User Analyser](https://reddit-user-analyser.netlify.app/) - Free web tool analyzing a Reddit username's profile and activity. Used by OSINT investigators and researchers; a quick social-media profile analysis aid. (free, proprietary)
- [Reddit_Persona](https://github.com/n2itn/reddit_persona) - Open-source tool building a persona from a Reddit user's data. Used by OSINT researchers and investigators; a social-media profiling aid. (MIT)

- [redditsfinder](https://pypi.org/project/redditsfinder/) - Open-source Python package aggregating a Reddit username's real history. Used by OSINT investigators and researchers; a fast social-media data collection tool. (MIT)
- [Reveddit.com](https://www.reveddit.com/) - Free service showing removed content for a Reddit user. Used by OSINT researchers and investigators; a useful social-media history recovery tool. (free, proprietary)
- [Reverse Whois](https://osint.sh/reversewhois/) - Reverse WHOIS lookup mapping domains to registrants. Used by recon analysts and investigators; a domain ownership discovery tool. (free, proprietary)
- [S3Scanner](https://github.com/sa7mon/S3Scanner) - Cloud storage scanner for identifying exposed S3 buckets containing firmware or configuration data. Used by cloud security testers; a focused exposure scanner. (MIT)
- [SaaSFort](https://saasfort.com/scan) - Runs a free 60-second external NIS2 and security posture scan with an A-F grade and no signup. Used by compliance-minded teams; a quick external exposure check. (free tier)
- [Scorifya](https://www.scorifya.com) - Gives a 0-100 security score for any website covering TLS, headers, cookies, DNS, and email signals. Used by defenders and site owners; a quick external configuration rating. (free, proprietary)

- [Shodan / Censys](https://pyxida.aueb.gr/server/api/core/bitstreams/e1c13860-502a-4ef6-b5a3-ded20368be05/content) - Internet-connected ICS device OSINT search engines for discovering publicly exposed industrial control assets and conducting external reconnaissance. Used by OT security analysts; a fast way to map exposed industrial infrastructure. (free tier)
- [slurp](https://github.com/hehnope/slurp) - Tool to evaluate the security of Amazon S3 buckets. Used by cloud security testers; a focused bucket misconfiguration scanner.
- [snallygaster](https://github.com/hannob/snallygaster) - Scans HTTP servers for accidentally exposed secret files and leftover artifacts. Used by pentesters and bug-bounty hunters; a fast, focused exposure scanner. (0BSD)
- [SpiderSuite](https://github.com/spidersuite/SpiderSuite) - Cross-platform web security crawler with standard, headless, interactive, brute-force and archive modes for attack-surface mapping. Used by pentesters and bug-bounty hunters; a versatile crawling workbench.

- [WHOIS](https://www.whois.com/) - Domain registration and ownership lookup service. Used by investigators and admins; a free reference for checking who owns a domain. (free, proprietary)
- [cdncheck](https://github.com/projectdiscovery/cdncheck) - CDN / cloud provider detection before scanning. (MIT)
- [cvcemap](https://github.com/projectdiscovery/vulnx) - CVE-to-version mapping (repo: vulnx). (MIT)
- [FAVIHUNTER](https://github.com/eremit4/favihunter) - favicon-hash asset discovery against scan datasets. (MIT)
- [findomain](https://github.com/Findomain/Findomain) - CT-based subdomain discovery with database backend. (GPL-3.0)
- [gitjacker](https://github.com/liamg/gitjacker) - .git directory leak dump + repo reconstruction. (Unlicense)
- [gowitness](https://github.com/sensepost/gowitness) - website screenshot recon with browsable reports. (GPL-3.0)
- [graphw00f](https://github.com/dolevf/graphw00f) - GraphQL engine fingerprinter. (BSD-3-Clause)
- [knockpy](https://github.com/guelfoweb/knockpy) - subdomain enum by wordlist + CT with wildcard detection. (GPL-3.0)
- [subcat](https://github.com/duty1g/subcat) - passive subdomain discovery for pipelines. (MIT)
- [tlsx](https://github.com/projectdiscovery/tlsx) - TLS recon: ciphers, certs, SANs, versions. (MIT)

## 🕵️ The Shadow Bureau — OSINT & Public-Intelligence Tools
Intro: Public data, lawful purpose — OSINT frameworks, people-search, breach lookups, site monitoring and archive history for investigators, defenders and researchers. These tools only surface what is already public; use them for authorized investigations, defense and education only — never to stalk, harass or dox anyone.

### 🧭 Frameworks & Crawlers

- [sn0int](https://github.com/kpcyrd/sn0int) - Semi-automatic OSINT framework with scriptable modules, its own package ecosystem and a database that remembers everything you've collected. Used by OSINT investigators scripting structured intel gathering; the semi-automatic OSINT framework for serious cases. (GPL-3.0)
- [Photon](https://github.com/s0md3v/Photon) - Fast OSINT crawler that rips a target apart for emails, social profiles, intel files and metadata, with exportable reports. Used by OSINT researchers crawling targets for emails, socials and intel; the fast open crawler for investigations. (GPL-3.0)
- [chiasmodon](https://github.com/chiasmod0n/chiasmodon) - Domain-centric OSINT: pivot from one domain to emails, subdomains, ASN and related-asset intelligence across multiple sources. Used by investigators pivoting from domains, emails and IPs across OSINT sources; the open alternative to commercial attribution tooling.

- [Seekr](https://github.com/seekr-osint/seekr) - Self-hosted keyless OSINT toolkit with a desktop-style web UI, person database and account cards. Used by investigators gathering and managing identifier lookups (email via Discord/Spotify/PGP keyservers, username discovery, GitHub→email) with zero API keys; plugin system, Docker/Nix. (GPL-3.0)

### 👤 People & Account Discovery

- [maigret](https://github.com/soxoj/maigret) - Builds a full dossier from a single username across thousands of sites; powerful people-OSINT — authorized investigations only. Used by OSINT investigators generating full username reports across platforms; the Sherlock successor with report output.
- [WhatsMyName](https://whatsmyname.app) - Web app plus open database for checking one username across hundreds of platforms at once; keep lookups lawful and authorized. Used by OSINT investigators checking usernames across hundreds of sites; the open web project behind many commercial aggregators.
- [Osintgram](https://github.com/Datalux/Osintgram) - Interactive Instagram OSINT (followers, public emails/phones, geotags, posting patterns); requires an Instagram account — authorized research only. Used by investigators with authorized access profiling Instagram accounts; the open Instagram OSINT toolkit. (GPL-3.0)
- [socialscan](https://github.com/iojw/socialscan) - Accurate email and username existence checks across major platforms by querying signup servers directly instead of scraping profile pages; quiet upstream but still accurate. Used by investigators verifying account existence with fewer false positives; the accuracy-first checker for email and username lookups. (MPL-2.0)
- [gosearch](https://github.com/ibnaleem/gosearch) - Fast concurrent username enumeration across 190+ sites with category-filtered account discovery. Used by OSINT investigators mapping a person's account footprint quickly; the concurrent alternative to sherlock-class username hunts. (GPL-3.0)
- [socid-extractor](https://github.com/soxoj/socid-extractor) - The extraction engine behind Maigret: turn any profile URL into structured identifiers — internal user IDs, linked accounts and hidden page metadata. Used by OSINT investigators pivoting from one profile to linked identities; the maigret author's extractor for deep profile intelligence. (MIT)
- [GitFive](https://github.com/mxrch/GitFive) - GitHub account OSINT: reconstruct a user's footprint from commits, organizations, emails and repo activity to map identities and aliases. Used by investigators tracking GitHub identities across repositories; the deep GitHub profiler for authorized cases. (MPL-2.0)
- [Marple](https://github.com/soxoj/marple) - Collect links to profiles by username through search engines, then analyze the results to surface related accounts across platforms. Used by OSINT investigators expanding one username into a full cross-platform profile set; the search-engine profile aggregator from the maigret author. (MIT)

### 📧 Email & Phone Intel

- [MailAccess](https://github.com/KatrielMoses/MailAccess) - Email-exposure toolkit that flags breach appearances and stealer-log presence for an address — authorized investigations only. Used by investigators during authorized account-recovery engagements to check email credential exposure; a focused checker for red-team and OSINT work.
- [check-if-email-exists](https://github.com/reacherhq/check-if-email-exists) - Verify an email address exists without sending any message: SMTP-level mailbox checks with disposable and catch-all detection, self-hostable or as a service. Used by investigators confirming a mailbox is real before deeper checks; the free verifier behind Reacher. (MIT)
- [telegram-phone-number-checker](https://github.com/bellingcat/telegram-phone-number-checker) - Check which phone numbers are connected to Telegram accounts and pull usernames and names; needs your own Telegram session — authorized research only. Used by investigators with authorized Telegram access linking numbers to accounts; Bellingcat's open checker for sanctioned research. (MIT)
- [whatsapp-osint](https://github.com/jasperan/whatsapp-osint) - Monitor WhatsApp online/offline presence and profile signals from a linked session for lawful, authorized investigations only. Used by investigators tracking activity patterns in sanctioned cases; the open WhatsApp presence monitor for lawful research. (MIT)

### 🩸 Breach & Leak Lookup

- [leaker](https://github.com/vflame6/leaker) - One terminal command, ten public breach databases — passive checks for whether an email or domain appears in known leaks. Used by OSINT researchers searching breach corpora for exposed records; the free alternative to paid breach-search subscriptions.
- [LeakLens](https://leaklens.org) - Non-commercial breach registry with a keyless phone, email, username and password exposure audit API. Used by anyone auditing their own (or authorized) identifier exposure without accounts or fees; the free keyless breach-lookup service. (free service, non-commercial)

- [Exposure-Check-OSINT](https://github.com/RanaKhan77/Exposure-Check-OSINT) - Self-hosted exposure checker for your own identifiers: 25 keyless sources spanning breaches, infostealer logs and the COMB 11B+ corpus, with HIBP k-anonymity and a 200-platform username grid. Used by privacy-conscious users auditing their own leak footprint; the self-hosted, no-API-keys alternative to paid identity-monitoring services. (Apache-2.0, maintainer's project)

### 🌐 Website Monitoring & Analysis

- [web-check](https://github.com/lissy93/web-check) - All-in-one website OSINT dashboard — DNS, headers, tech, ports, TLS, whois and more — self-hostable in one Docker pull. Used by bug hunters and defenders getting a full technical profile of any site in one page; the free all-in-one site analyzer.
- [changedetection.io](https://github.com/dgtlmoon/changedetection.io) - Self-hosted website change monitoring with selectors, visual diffs and dozens of notification targets. Used by investigators and ops teams monitoring pages for changes; the open-source alternative to commercial change-monitoring SaaS.

### 🕰️ Archive & History

### 🎯 Threat & Attack-Surface Intel

- [opensquat](https://github.com/atenreiro/opensquat) - Watches newly-registered domains for typosquats and brand lookalikes, with free daily feeds. Used by brand-protection teams watching for newly registered lookalike domains; the free alternative to commercial domain-monitoring services. (GPL-3.0)
- [cyberbro](https://github.com/stanfrbd/cyberbro) - Self-hosted IOC reputation station: paste URLs, IPs or hashes and get verdicts from dozens of engines on one page. Used by SOC analysts triaging indicators through multiple OSINT sources in one UI; the free aggregator for daily IOC lookups.
- [HoneyLabs](https://honeylabs.net) - Free per-IP honeypot-telemetry reports: see whether an address has been tripping sensors worldwide. Used by researchers studying deceptive-security techniques; the honeypot research project for defender experiments.
- [ip.thc.org](https://ip.thc.org) - Free, keyless JSON API for reverse DNS, subdomain and CNAME lookups on any IP — the quick IP pivot. Used by analysts quickly checking IP reputation and attributes; the free no-frills lookup service.
- [OSINT-Framework](https://osintframework.com/) - The classic OSINT tool index: categorized sources from public records to social analysis. Used by investigators navigating the OSINT tool landscape; the classic free index that commercial trainings reference. (free service)
- [intel-codex](https://github.com/gl0bal01/intel-codex) - Open-source field manual for OSINT, DFIR and cybersecurity investigations — operational SOPs, browsable online or clonable as an Obsidian vault. Used by OSINT investigators and DFIR responders following tested procedures; the free operational playbook library.. (MIT)
- [aware-online](https://www.aware-online.com/en/) - OSINT training and tooling from the Dutch awareness school; free investigative resources. Used by OSINT learners building investigation skills; the free training resource behind many analyst onboarding paths. (free service)
- [Nipe](https://github.com/GouveaHeitor/nipe) - A tool to make your traffic pass through the Tor network; redirect the machine's egress for privacy. Used by researchers anonymizing tool traffic through Tor exit nodes; the open anonymity router for sanctioned research. (MIT)
- [Whonix](https://www.whonix.org) - The anonymity-focused OS: a Workstation VM routed entirely through a Tor Gateway VM. Used by journalists and researchers needing compartmentalized anonymity; the free VM-based anonymity workstation. (GPL-3.0)
- [PhoneInfoga](https://github.com/sundowndev/PhoneInfoga) - Phone-number OSINT: carriers, reputation and footprint from public sources, no scraping of private data. Used by investigators profiling phone numbers from public sources; the standard free phone-OSINT scanner. (GPL-3.0)

- [TOsint](https://github.com/drego85/tosint) - Telegram OSINT: user, channel and bot intelligence from public messaging data. Used by investigators mining Telegram accounts and groups from public data; the focused Telegram OSINT client. (GPL-3.0)
- [tg-chat-dump](https://github.com/renkagod/tg-chat-dump) - Archive public Telegram chats to SQLite for structured analysis. Used by researchers exporting Telegram chat history for analysis; the open dump utility for sanctioned investigations. (MIT)
- [VoidAccess](https://github.com/KatrielMoses/voidaccess) - Self-hosted dark-web monitoring platform for threat-intelligence teams. Used by bug hunters dorking for exposed panels and documents; the free web-search automation for recon. (MIT)
- [IntelHub](https://github.com/tomsec8/IntelHub) - Browser extension that runs OSINT analysis locally; no data leaves the analyst's machine. Used by analysts running quick multi-source OSINT lookups from the browser; the free aggregator extension for triage. (MIT)
- [Taranis AI](https://github.com/taranis-ai/taranis-ai) - OSINT gathering and enrichment platform for analysts; self-hostable with an AI-assist layer. Used by CTI teams gathering and briefing intel with AI assistance; the open-source alternative to commercial intel-briefing tools. (EUPL-1.2)
- [Mitaka](https://github.com/ninoseki/mitaka) - IOC-pivot browser extension: search an IP/domain/hash across 30+ engines from the context menu. Used by analysts searching IOCs across 60+ services from the browser; the free browser extension for instant CTI lookups. (MIT)
- [ScamSearch](https://scamsearch.io) - Scammer profile lookup by phone, email or username; crowdsourced anti-fraud intel. Used by fraud investigators checking scammers against a public database; the free lookup for scammer attribution. (free service)
- [Flowsint](https://github.com/reconurge/flowsint) - Self-hosted graph-based OSINT investigation platform: interactive relationship graphs across domains, IPs, emails, phone numbers, people and crypto wallets with automated enrichment modules. - [Telegram Search. Search for posts](https://tgstat.com/search) - TGStat search lets users find Telegram posts and channels by keyword. Used by OSINT investigators and journalists; a free analytics search for public Telegram content. (free tier)
- [TelPoisk](https://telpoisk.com/) - Russian phone directory for looking up numbers and associated subscriber details. Used by OSINT investigators and researchers; a free lookup for Russian phone numbers. (free, proprietary)
- [Thats them](https://thatsthem.com/) - Free people search aggregating public records. Used by investigators; a consolidated person lookup. (free, proprietary)
- [ThatsThem Phone Lookup](https://thatsthem.com/reverse-phone-lookup) - Free reverse phone lookup returning names and addresses. Used by OSINT researchers and investigators; a people-search lookup for phone numbers. (free, proprietary)
- [TIKTOK Search Engine](https://cse.google.com/cse?cx=011444696387487602669:aqf7d9w73om) - Google Custom Search Engine that indexes TikTok pages for keyword queries. Used by OSINT investigators and researchers; a free search interface for public TikTok content. (free, proprietary)
- [TinEye](https://tineye.com/) - Reverse image search engine that finds where images appear online. Used by OSINT investigators and researchers; a free tier for tracing photo origins and reuse. (free tier)
- [tinfoleak](https://github.com/vaguileradiaz/tinfoleak) - Open-source tool for Twitter intelligence analysis. Used by OSINT investigators; a complete social media analysis utility. (CC-BY-SA-4.0)
- [toutatis](https://github.com/megadose/toutatis) - Open-source tool that gathers Instagram account info and probes email or phone via password reset. Used by OSINT investigators; a reconnaissance utility for linking accounts to contact details. (GPL-3.0)
- [Ufind.name](https://ufind.name/) - Free name search returning real profile links. Used by investigators; a name-to-profile discovery tool. (free, proprietary)
- [user-scanner](https://github.com/kaifcodec/user-scanner.git) - Open-source per-site email registration checker. Used by OSINT investigators; an email account enumeration tool. (free, proprietary)
- [USPhonebook](https://www.usphonebook.com/) - Free reverse phone lookup returning name and address details. Used by OSINT researchers and investigators; a people-search lookup for US phone numbers. (free, proprietary)
- [Validnumber.com](https://validnumber.com/) - Free phone lookup service returning carrier, line type, and owner details. Used by OSINT researchers and fraud analysts; a quick phone-number intelligence lookup. (free, proprietary)
- [WebMii](https://webmii.com/) - Free name-based search surfacing people and web visibility. Used by OSINT researchers; a quick person-to-web presence lookup. (free, proprietary)
- [WhatBreach](https://github.com/Ekultek/WhatBreach) - Open-source breach database search tool. Used by OSINT investigators; a scriptable leak lookup. (MIT)
- [White Pages](https://www.whitepages.com/) - Free basic phone and address people lookup. Used by investigators; a US directory search for contact details. (free tier)
- [Yandex Images](https://yandex.com/images/) - Yandex reverse image search with strong face and photo matching. Used by OSINT investigators and researchers; a powerful free image search for identifying people and places. (free, proprietary)
- [zaba search](https://www.zabasearch.com/) - Free people search drawing on public records. Used by OSINT investigators; a name-to-record lookup. (free, proprietary)
- [ZLOOKUP](https://www.zlookup.com/) - Free reverse phone lookup service returning names tied to numbers. Used by OSINT investigators and researchers; a simple people-search lookup for phone numbers. (free, proprietary)
- [EmailHarvester](https://github.com/maldevel/EmailHarvester) - email enumeration via search engines. (GPL-3.0)
- [gallery-dl](https://github.com/mikf/gallery-dl) - gallery/video downloader for hundreds of sites. (GPL-2.0)
- [InfoHound](https://github.com/Fundacio-i2CAT/InfoHound) - OSINT framework: people, emails, domains, leaks. (AGPL-3.0)
- [Instaloader](https://github.com/instaloader/instaloader) - Instagram archiving with metadata. (MIT)
- [Investigo](https://github.com/tdh8316/Investigo) - username/email finder across social networks. (MIT)

## 🔑 The Vault — Secrets, Credentials & Password Security Tools
Intro: Where secrets live and leak — find them before attackers do, and keep the new ones safe.

- [gitleaks](https://github.com/gitleaks/gitleaks) - Fast Git-history secret scanning from pre-commit hooks to CI; the default choice for repos. Used by DevSecOps teams scanning repos for secrets in CI; the open-source alternative to GitGuardian's paid platform.
- [trufflehog](https://github.com/trufflesecurity/trufflehog) - Deep secret scanning across Git, S3, Docker images and 800+ detector types with live credential verification. Used by security teams hunting leaked secrets across Git history and object stores; the open-source alternative to commercial secret-scanning services. (AGPL-3.0)
- [hashcat](https://github.com/hashcat/hashcat) - The world's fastest password-recovery tool; the standard for authorized password-strength audits. Used by password auditors and DFIR teams worldwide; the cracker commercial rigs also run.
- [John the Ripper](https://github.com/openwall/john) - The classic password auditor: hundreds of hash formats and the legendary jumbo community build. Used by password auditors for three decades; the classic open-source cracker no pentest kit ships without. (GPL-2.0)
- [haiti](https://github.com/noraj/haiti) - Hash-type identifier: given a digest, it narrows down the algorithm family so cracking work starts on the right track. Used by CTF players and pentesters triaging found hashes before running hashcat or John; the standard free helper for hash identification. (MIT)
- [KeePassXC](https://github.com/keepassxreboot/keepassxc) - Cross-platform, offline, no-cloud password manager — the security-community standard. Used by security-conscious professionals storing credentials offline; the community alternative to 1Password and LastPass. (GPL-3.0)
- [Bitwarden](https://github.com/bitwarden/server) - Open-source, sync-everywhere password management for teams and families. Used by teams and individuals wanting open-source password management; the self-hostable alternative to commercial password SaaS. (AGPL + commercial options)
- [HashiCorp Vault](https://github.com/hashicorp/vault) - The enterprise secrets platform: dynamic credentials, PKI, encryption-as-a-service. Used by platform teams managing secrets at scale; the open-source alternative to cloud secret stores and CyberArk for dynamic secrets. (source-available BSL)
- [SOPS](https://github.com/getsops/sops) - Encrypted secrets in Git: edit YAML/JSON values, commit ciphertext, rotate keys. Used by DevOps teams encrypting secrets inside config repos; the open standard for gitops-friendly secret management. (MPL-2.0)
- [age](https://github.com/FiloSottile/age) - Simple, modern, opinionated file-encryption tool for the post-GPG era. Used by developers wanting simple, modern file encryption; the spiritual successor to GPG for the gitops era.
- [detect-secrets](https://github.com/Yelp/detect-secrets) - Enterprise secret scanner designed for baseline diffs and incremental CI audits. Used by CI pipelines baseline-scanning for secrets; Yelp's open scanner that complements commercial enterprise offerings.
- [siloscan](https://github.com/RandomCodeSpace/siloscan) - Offline air-gapped repository secret and code-quality scanner with 228 rules and SARIF output; zero telemetry. Used by teams scanning sensitive repositories where nothing may leave the network; the air-gap-friendly secret scanner for restricted environments. (MIT)
- [LaZagne](https://github.com/AlessandroZ/LaZagne) - Audits which passwords your workstations remember — browsers, mail clients, OS stores — for authorized inventory and exposure checks only.
