Used by pentesters in authorized engagements demonstrating credential exposure on compromised hosts; the classic credential-harvesting tool. (LGPL-3.0)
- [MAT2](https://0xacab.org/jvoisin/mat2) - Metadata Anonymisation Toolkit v2: strips hidden metadata from documents, images and more. Used by journalists and privacy-conscious users stripping metadata from documents; the free alternative to commercial redaction suites. (GPL-3.0)
- [Retraced (BoxyHQ)](https://github.com/retracedhq/retraced) - Open-source audit-log API: compliant, tamper-evident activity records for your SaaS. Used by SaaS builders adding compliance-grade audit logging; the open-source alternative to commercial audit-log services. (Apache-2.0)
- [hardened_malloc](https://github.com/GrapheneOS/hardened_malloc) - The allocator that makes exploitation harder by design; from the GrapheneOS project. Used by hardened deployments and privacy distros for exploit-mitigating memory allocation; the open allocator adopted by GrapheneOS-class systems. (MIT)
- [Databunker](https://databunker.org/) - Encrypted personal-data vault: API-first storage for PII with built-in privacy compliance. Used by backend teams storing customer PII encrypted and auditable; the open-source alternative to commercial customer-data vaults. (GPL-3.0)
- [keyFinder](https://github.com/momenbasel/keyFinder) - Passively discovers leaked API keys and secrets in web pages during browsing. Used by forensic analysts locating crypto key material in memory images; the niche free utility for DFIR work. (MIT)

- [Kingfisher](https://github.com/mongodb/kingfisher) - MongoDB's open-source Rust secret scanner: detect leaked secrets across code/Git/history/containers/cloud, then live-validate credentials, map blast radius and revoke — the full post-detection lifecycle. Used by security teams killing the "leaked-but-rotated key" false-positive class; embeddable as CLI, Rust lib or Python SDK. (Apache-2.0)
- [Acra](https://github.com/cossacklabs/acra) - Client-side encryption engine for SQL databases with selective encryption, SQL injection prevention and intrusion detection. Used by developers and DBAs; a strong data-protection layer. (Apache-2.0)
- [Cryptomator](https://cryptomator.org/) - Client-side encryption for cloud storage, encrypting files before they reach Google Drive, Dropbox, or OneDrive. Used by privacy-minded cloud users; a transparent encryption layer. (GPL-3.0)
- [Dogtag Certificate System](https://www.dogtagpki.org/) - Scalable enterprise PKI solution managing the full digital certificate lifecycle. Used by enterprise PKI administrators; a robust free certificate management system.
- [GnuPG (GPG)](https://gnupg.org/) - Complete OpenPGP implementation for encryption and signing, now supporting post-quantum Kyber algorithms. Used by privacy-conscious users and developers; the essential free crypto toolkit.
- [GTKCrypto](https://github.com/paolostivanin/GTKCrypto) - GTK application for encrypting and signing files or text with AES, Twofish, Serpent, and Camellia. Used by Linux desktop users; a simple free file encryption GUI. (GPL-3.0)
- [OpenXPKI](https://www.openxpki.org/) - Enterprise-grade open-source PKI and trustcenter solution supporting CA rollover and multiple PKI realms. Used by PKI operators; a capable free certificate authority platform.
- [Step CLI](https://smallstep.com/cli/) - CLI tool for building, operating, and automating PKI systems with X.509 operations. Used by DevOps and security engineers; a handy free certificate automation utility.
- [Tuta Mail](https://tuta.com/) - Privacy-focused end-to-end encrypted email service for secure communication. Used by privacy advocates and journalists; a genuine free tier for encrypted mail. (free tier)
- [VeraCrypt](https://www.veracrypt.fr/) - Free open-source disk encryption software and successor to TrueCrypt, supporting full-disk encryption, hidden volumes, and plausible deniability. Used by security practitioners; a trusted encryption standard. (Apache-2.0)

## 🐳 The Foundry — DevSecOps, Cloud & Container Security Tools
Intro: Shift security left — SAST, SCA, IaC, SBOM and container scanning that fit the pipeline.

- [Trivy](https://github.com/aquasecurity/trivy) - The all-in-one scanner: containers, IaC, repos, SBOMs, licenses and secrets, with one binary. Used by DevSecOps teams scanning containers, IaC and repos in CI; the open-source alternative to Snyk and Aqua's commercial scanners.
- [Semgrep](https://github.com/semgrep/semgrep) - Fast, expressive static analysis with community rule packs for 30+ languages; write rules like you write code. Used by AppSec teams writing custom code-scanning rules fast; the open-source alternative to commercial SAST like Checkmarx and Veracode. (LGPL-2.1)
- [Falco](https://github.com/falcosecurity/falco) - CNCF runtime threat detection for containers, Kubernetes and cloud workloads. Used by cloud SOC teams detecting runtime threats in Kubernetes; the open-source alternative to commercial CNAPP runtime agents.
- [Prowler](https://github.com/prowler-cloud/prowler) - AWS/Azure/GCP security posture: hundreds of checks mapped to CIS benchmarks and compliance frameworks. Used by cloud security teams auditing AWS, GCP and Azure; the open-source alternative to commercial CSPM platforms.
- [Grype](https://github.com/anchore/grype) - Vulnerability scanner for container images and SBOMs with KEV/EPSS enrichment for prioritization. Used in CI pipelines scanning SBOMs and images for vulnerabilities; the open-source vulnerability scanner from Anchore.
- [Clair](https://github.com/quay/clair) - Static vulnerability analysis for container images and container registries, from the Quay project. Used by platform teams scanning images in CI/CD and registry pipelines; the veteran open-source container scanner alongside Grype and Trivy. (Apache-2.0)
- [Copacetic](https://github.com/project-copacetic/copacetic) - CLI that patches container images directly from vulnerability scan reports, without rebuilding the Dockerfile. Used by platform teams cutting fix latency on deployed images; a focused open-source image-patching tool. (Apache-2.0)
- [Syft](https://github.com/anchore/syft) - Generate clean SBOMs (SPDX/CycloneDX) from images and filesystems; feeds Grype and everything else. Used by supply-chain teams producing SBOMs in CI; the open-source SBOM generator that Grype and commercial tooling build on.
- [OSV-Scanner](https://github.com/google/osv-scanner) - Dependency vulnerability scanner backed by Google's OSV database; fast, transparent, no vendor lock. Used by developers scanning dependencies against the OSV database; Google's free alternative to commercial SCA tools.
- [retire.js](https://github.com/RetireJS/retire.js) - Scanner detecting JavaScript libraries with known vulnerabilities, in web pages and Node dependencies. Used by developers and pentesters flagging risky client-side libraries; the long-standing reference scanner for this niche. (Apache-2.0)
- [Checkov](https://github.com/bridgecrewio/checkov) - IaC scanning for Terraform, CloudFormation, Kubernetes and more, with auto-fix guidance. Used by DevSecOps scanning Terraform, CloudFormation and Kubernetes for misconfigurations; the open-source IaC scanner alternative to commercial policy platforms.
- [KICS](https://github.com/Checkmarx/kics) - 1,000+ security queries across Terraform, Ansible, Kubernetes, Dockerfile and other IaC. Used by platform teams scanning infrastructure-as-code across platforms; the open-source alternative to commercial IaC security products.
- [Kyverno](https://github.com/kyverno/kyverno) - CNCF policy engine for Kubernetes: validate, mutate and generate configurations as code. Used by Kubernetes platform teams enforcing policies natively in-cluster; the CNCF alternative to commercial admission-control products.
- [kube-bench](https://github.com/aquasecurity/kube-bench) - Run the CIS Kubernetes Benchmark checks against your nodes in minutes. Used by cluster admins running CIS Kubernetes benchmarks; the open-source compliance check for cluster hardening.
- [Dependency-Track](https://github.com/DependencyTrack/dependency-track) - SBOM-based continuous component analysis platform with portfolio risk dashboards. Used by supply-chain teams managing SBOMs and vulnerability exposure continuously; the OWASP alternative to commercial SBOM platforms.
- [ScoutSuite](https://github.com/nccgroup/ScoutSuite) - The classic multi-cloud security auditor with a friendly HTML report — quiet upstream, still the reference. Used by cloud auditors assessing multi-cloud security posture; the open-source alternative to commercial cloud-security audits. (GPL-3.0)
- [cosign](https://github.com/sigstore/cosign) - Sigstore's keyless container signing and verification; make provenance part of your pipeline. Used by supply-chain automation signing and verifying containers; the Sigstore tool making signed images the default, free.
- [Bandit](https://github.com/PyCQA/bandit) - The standard Python security linter; wire it into CI and never merge an `eval()` again. Used by Python teams catching security issues in CI; the standard open-source Python SAST tool.
- [Brakeman](https://github.com/presidentbeef/brakeman) - Static analysis purpose-built for Ruby on Rails; no config, no instrumentation, just findings. Used by Rails teams scanning source for security flaws; the standard open-source Rails SAST tool.
- [Horusec](https://github.com/ZupIT/horusec) - Open-source SAST orchestration that aggregates multiple language analyzers into one report. Used by dev teams running multi-language static analysis in CI; the self-hosted alternative to commercial SAST suites. (Apache-2.0)
- [secureCodeBox](https://github.com/secureCodeBox/secureCodeBox) - Kubernetes-native scanning orchestration: chains nmap, ZAP, Semgrep, SSLyze and more into automated pipelines. Used by DevSecOps teams running declarative scan flows; the open-source continuous-security-testing framework. (Apache-2.0)

- [StackRadar](https://github.com/lockdep/stackradar) - Kubernetes vulnerability scanner that builds an SBOM of every running image and ranks findings by CVSS, EPSS and CISA KEV. Used by DevSecOps teams prioritizing which container CVE actually matters in-cluster; the self-hosted alternative to commercial runtime CSPM. (AGPL-3.0)
- [cloudsplaining](https://github.com/salesforce/cloudsplaining) - Maps AWS IAM policies and flags the privilege-escalation paths you never meant to grant. Used by AWS security teams finding over-privileged IAM policies; the open-source alternative to commercial IAM analyzers.
- [Docker Bench for Security](https://github.com/docker/docker-bench-security) - Audits your Docker host and daemon configuration against the CIS Benchmark in a single script run. Used by admins auditing Docker hosts against the CIS benchmark; the reference open-source check script.
- [GDB](https://www.sourceware.org/gdb/) - The GNU debugger: breakpoints, watchpoints and reverse debugging across dozens of architectures. Used by reverse engineers and exploit developers on every platform; the GNU debugger standard for binary analysis. (GPL-3.0)
- [WinDbg](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-hardware/drivers/debugger/) - The Windows debugger for user, kernel and crash-dump analysis; now with a modern UI. Used by malware analysts and driver developers debugging Windows; Microsoft's debugger at the core of Windows-forensics work. (free, proprietary)
- [ChipWhisperer](https://github.com/newaetech/chipwhisperer) - Side-channel analysis and glitching for hardware security research. Used by hardware-security researchers performing side-channel and glitching attacks; the open-source hardware lab that commercial gear costs six figures to replicate. (GPL-3.0)
- [ProgPilot](https://github.com/designsecurity/progpilot) - Static taint analysis for PHP sources: finds input-to-sink flows at build time. Used by PHP AppSec teams doing taint analysis of sources and sinks; the open-source PHP static-analysis tool. (GPL-3.0)

- [cppcheck](https://github.com/danmar/cppcheck) - Static analysis for C/C++: detects bugs and undefined behavior, not just style. Used by C/C++ developers catching defects and undefined behavior; the free static analyzer alongside commercial suites. (GPL-3.0)
- [cwe_checker](https://github.com/fkie-cad/cwe_checker) - Finds common CWE patterns in binaries, direct from firmware images. Used by firmware auditors mapping binary patterns to CWEs; the open-source binary-analysis aid for embedded reviews. (LGPL-2.1+)
- [Substation](https://github.com/brexhq/substation) - An event and data pipeline toolkit for detection engineering at scale. Used by OT/ICS security researchers testing industrial protocols; the open-source toolkit for power-grid-class engagements. (MIT)

- [Kubescape](https://github.com/kubescape/kubescape) - CNCF-incubating Kubernetes security platform: scan manifests, Helm charts, Kustomize dirs, live clusters and images against NSA-CISA, MITRE ATT&CK and CIS frameworks from one CLI. Used by platform teams hardening Kubernetes before and after deploy; CNCF-backed with an in-cluster operator option. (Apache-2.0)
- [command-center](https://github.com/openjs-foundation/command-center) - OpenJS security and compliance monitor tracking SECURITY.md hygiene and OSSF Scorecard posture across projects. Used by open-source maintainers monitoring project security posture; the OpenJS supply-chain health monitor. (MIT)
- [Calico](https://www.tigera.io/project-calico/) - Open-source networking and network policy engine for containers operating at layer three. Used by Kubernetes operators; a mature free network security solution.
- [Chainloop](https://chainloop.dev/) - Open-source evidence store for supply chain attestations, SBOMs, VEX, SARIF, and QA reports with SLSA Level 3 support. Used by DevSecOps teams; a capable free attestation platform.
- [Chainmail](https://chainmail.saisravancherukuri.com) - Unified open-source workbench bringing eight supply chain security capabilities together in one place. Used by DevSecOps teams; a free consolidated supply chain toolkit.
- [Chef InSpec](https://inspec.io/) - Open-source testing framework for infrastructure compliance with an auditd resource for testing audit rules against system configuration. Used by DevSecOps engineers; compliance-as-code for infrastructure. (Apache-2.0)
- [CloudSploit](https://github.com/aquasecurity/cloudsploit) - AWS security auditing tool that scans cloud accounts for misconfigurations and risky settings. Used by cloud security engineers; a practical open-source scanner for AWS posture. (GPL-3.0)
- [CycloneDX](https://cyclonedx.org/) - OWASP-led SBOM standard lightweight and suited for expressing component relationships and vulnerabilities. Used by developers and security teams; a widely adopted free standard.
- [GardWatch](https://pypi.org/project/gardwatch/) - CLI tool analyzing dependencies for trust, security risks, and quality signals across PyPI, npm, Go, Cargo, Maven, and NuGet. Used by developers; a free dependency risk checker.
- [in-toto](https://in-toto.io/) - CNCF-graduated framework protecting software supply chain integrity by verifying each task in the chain. Used by build and release teams; a mature free attestation framework.
- [Istio](https://istio.io/) - Open-source service mesh for Kubernetes providing traffic control, security, monitoring, and policy enforcement via sidecar proxies. Used by platform engineers; a leading free mesh.
- [Jacked](https://github.com/carbonetes/jacked) - Vulnerability scanner for Docker images, code repositories, and tarballs using CycloneDX SBOM internally. Used by DevSecOps teams; a free container scanning utility. (Apache-2.0)
- [Kimera](https://pypi.org/project/kimera/) - Kubernetes security validation platform from Dynatrace testing whether controls actually block attacks. Used by Kubernetes defenders; a free attack-validation tool.
- [Kube-hunter](https://github.com/aquasecurity/kube-hunter) - Penetration testing tool that hunts for weaknesses in Kubernetes clusters by discovering and exploiting misconfigurations. Used by cloud pentesters; a practical free cluster assessment tool. (Apache-2.0)
- [NodeAgent](https://github.com/kubescape/node-agent) - Kubescape runtime security agent using eBPF for process monitoring, file integrity, and network tracing. Used by Kubernetes defenders; a free runtime detection component. (Apache-2.0)
- [Open Policy Agent (OPA)](https://www.openpolicyagent.org/) - Policy engine applying unified rules across microservices, Kubernetes, CI/CD, and API gateways. Used by platform and security teams; a widely adopted free policy framework.
- [OpenSSF Scorecard](https://scorecard.dev/) - Automated tool assessing repositories against security heuristics and assigning scores. Used by open-source maintainers and consumers; a free supply chain risk signal.
- [Safety](https://github.com/pyupio/safety) - Python dependency security scanner that checks installed packages against known vulnerability databases. Used by developers and DevSecOps teams; a practical tool for catching vulnerable dependencies in CI. (MIT)
- [Sigstore](https://www.sigstore.dev/) - Open-source project for signing and verifying software artifacts to improve supply chain security. Used by developers and release engineers; a foundational free signing ecosystem.
- [SLSA (Supply-chain Levels for Software Artifacts)](https://slsa.dev/) - OpenSSF security framework providing standards and controls to prevent supply chain tampering. Used by software producers and consumers; a widely adopted free framework.
- [SPDX](https://spdx.dev/) - Linux Foundation SBOM standard emphasizing license compliance expression. Used by compliance and security teams; a popular free regulatory-friendly standard.
- [Tetragon](https://github.com/cilium/tetragon) - eBPF-based runtime security tool enforcing policy in-kernel with near-zero overhead for Kubernetes workloads. Used by platform and security engineers; a powerful free runtime defense. (Apache-2.0)
- [verifyfetch](https://github.com/hamzaydia/verifyfetch) - Browser-side integrity verification and resumable downloads for large files using SRI hashes, defending against CDN compromise and supply-chain attacks. Used by web developers; a focused supply-chain defense utility. (Apache-2.0)
- [AFL++](https://github.com/AFLplusplus/AFLplusplus) - coverage-guided fuzzer, the community fork that won. (AGPL-3.0)
- [cloudfox](https://github.com/BishopFox/cloudfox) - cloud attack-surface auditor (AWS/Azure/GCP). (MIT)
- [CodeQL](https://github.com/github/codeql) - semantic code analysis, free for research. (MIT)
- [GCP Scanner](https://github.com/google/gcp_scanner) - GCP security scanner from google. (Apache-2.0)
- [pip-audit](https://github.com/pypa/pip-audit) - Python dependency vulnerability scanning (PyPA). (Apache-2.0)
- [Slither](https://github.com/crytic/slither) - Solidity static analysis, 90+ detectors. (AGPL-3.0)
- [Tracee](https://github.com/aquasecurity/tracee) - eBPF container runtime security tracing. (Apache-2.0)
- [xeol](https://github.com/xeol-io/xeol) - end-of-life dependency detection. (Apache-2.0)

## 🔵 The Sentinel Ops — Blue Team, SOC & DFIR Tools
Intro: Detect, hunt, forensicate — the defensive operations toolkit.

- [Wazuh](https://github.com/wazuh/wazuh) - Open-source XDR/SIEM: FIM, log analysis, vulnerability detection and compliance reporting in one free platform. Used by SOC teams running open XDR and SIEM; the free alternative to Splunk, QRadar and commercial EDR stacks. (GPL-2.0)
- [Velociraptor](https://github.com/Velocidex/velociraptor) - Endpoint visibility and DFIR at scale: hunt across thousands of machines with one query language. Used by DFIR teams hunting across thousands of endpoints; the open-source alternative to commercial endpoint-forensics platforms. (AGPL-3.0)
- [Suricata](https://github.com/OISF/suricata) - High-performance IDS/IPS/NSM engine with full protocol logging and Eve JSON output. Used by network defenders running IDS/IPS at line rate; the open-source engine behind countless commercial and gov deployments. (GPL-2.0)
- [Zeek](https://github.com/zeek/zeek) - The network analysis framework that turns raw traffic into structured, queryable intelligence. Used by NSM teams producing rich network telemetry; the framework most SOC pipelines are built around. (BSD-3)
- [YARA](https://github.com/VirusTotal/yara) - The pattern-matching language for malware identification — if you write detections, you write YARA. Used by malware analysts everywhere to classify samples; the pattern-matching standard commercial AV also uses. (BSD-3)
- [Sigma](https://github.com/SigmaHQ/sigma) - Vendor-neutral SIEM detection rules — "Snort for logs", convertible to Splunk/Sentinel/Elastic. Used by detection engineers writing portable detection rules; the vendor-neutral alternative to proprietary SIEM rule languages. (DRL 1.1 rules)
- [osquery](https://github.com/osquery/osquery) - Operate endpoints as a SQL database: ask security questions, get rows back. Used by IT-security teams querying endpoints with SQL; the open-source foundation Facebook built for fleet visibility.
- [Security Onion](https://github.com/Security-Onion-Solutions/securityonion) - The free SOC-in-a-box distro: Suricata, Zeek, Elastic, dashboards and detection pipelines. Used by SOC and DFIR teams as a complete network-security-monitoring distro; the free alternative to commercial NSM suites. (Elastic License 2.0)
- [Atomic Red Team](https://github.com/redcanaryco/atomic-red-team) - Library of executable detection tests mapped to MITRE ATT&CK; prove your defenses fire. Used by purple teams testing detections per ATT&CK technique; the open-source alternative to commercial breach-attack-simulation platforms.
- [Timesketch](https://github.com/google/timesketch) - Google's collaborative forensic timeline analysis for multi-investigator cases. Used by DFIR analysts building collaborative forensic timelines; the open-source alternative to commercial timeline-analysis tools.
- [Volatility 3](https://github.com/volatilityfoundation/volatility3) - Memory forensics framework — RAM malware has nowhere to hide. Used by DFIR analysts extracting artifacts from memory images; the framework that defined open memory forensics.
- [CAPEv2](https://github.com/kevoreilly/CAPEv2) - The maintained Cuckoo sandbox: automated malware detonation, config extraction and reporting. Used by malware analysts running automated sandbox detonations; the open-source alternative to commercial sandboxes like JoeSandbox. (GPL-3.0)
- [Arkime](https://github.com/arkime/arkime) - Full-packet capture plus indexed search at scale (formerly Moloch); PCAP you can actually query. Used by NSM teams capturing and searching full packets at scale; the open-source alternative to commercial full-packet-capture appliances.
- [RITA](https://github.com/activecm/rita) - Detects C2 beaconing in Zeek logs — traffic analysis that finds the quiet intruders. Used by threat hunters finding C2 beaconing in DNS and flows; the free open-source hunter for network telemetry. (GPL-3.0)

- [capa](https://github.com/mandiant/capa) - Identifies what malware actually does: maps capabilities and ATT&CK techniques straight from binaries. Used by malware analysts determining what binaries can do; the open-source capability engine from Mandiant research.

- [sysmon-modular](https://github.com/olafhartong/sysmon-modular) - Swiss-army Sysmon configuration: per-ATT&CK-technique logging modules you compose yourself. Used by detection engineers tailoring Sysmon per event type; the community config alternative to fixed baselines.
- [ElastAlert2](https://github.com/jertel/elastalert2) - The maintained successor to ElastAlert: flexible alerting on Elasticsearch data with dozens of rule types. Used by SOC teams alerting from Elasticsearch data; the open-source alerting layer for ELK stacks.
- [pspy](https://github.com/DominicBreuker/pspy) - Watch process and file events on any Linux box without root — catch cron-based persistence in the act. Used by pentesters and CTF players watching process activity without root; the standard privesc enumeration tool. (GPL-3.0)
- [ThreatMapper](https://github.com/deepfence/ThreatMapper) - Deepfence's open runtime threat scanner: finds and ranks exploitable vulnerabilities in cloud and container workloads. Used by cloud security teams mapping runtime risks; Deepfence's open-source alternative to commercial CNAPPs.
- [Adalanche](https://github.com/lkarlslund/Adalanche) - Active Directory attack-graph visualizer and explorer: ingest AD objects and see the real paths to Domain Admin. Used by blue teams auditing ACL misconfigurations and red teams planning paths; the go-to open-source AD attack-path analyzer. (AGPL-3.0)
- [Snort 3](https://github.com/snort3/snort3) - The next generation of the classic open-source IDS/IPS, rewritten in C++ with multi-threading. Used by network defenders running the classic open IDS; the engine that defined open-source intrusion detection. (GPL-2.0)
- [Kismet](https://github.com/kismetwireless/kismet) - Passive wireless detection layer: discovers Wi-Fi and BLE devices, rogue access points and wardrives. Used by wireless security auditors monitoring WiFi and Bluetooth; the standard open wireless detection platform. (GPL-2.0)
- [ntopng](https://github.com/ntop/ntopng) - High-speed web-based traffic monitoring: flows, top talkers and protocols with long historical views. Used by network engineers observing traffic in real time; the open-source traffic-analysis alternative to commercial NPM tools. (GPL-3.0)
- [Nagios Core](https://github.com/NagiosEnterprises/nagioscore) - The classic infrastructure monitor: availability checks that surface outages and anomalies early. Used by ops teams monitoring infrastructure for decades; the classic open-source monitoring platform. (GPL-2.0)
- [The Sleuth Kit](https://github.com/sleuthkit/sleuthkit) - The forensic library behind half the DFIR tools you know: disks, filesystems and timelines. Used by DFIR analysts examining disk images and file systems; the open-source foundation under commercial forensic suites. (CPL-1.0)
- [HijackThis](https://github.com/dragokas/hijackthis) - The veteran Windows hijack analyzer: scans browser and OS settings for malicious tampering. Used by technicians diagnosing Windows malware persistence; the veteran free scanner still trusted for autostart analysis. (GPL-2.0)
- [NoScript](https://github.com/hackademix/noscript) - The browser extension that blocks JavaScript and plugin content by default — client-side hardening's origin story. Used by privacy-focused users blocking scripts by default; the long-standing open-source browser-hardening extension. (GPL-3.0)
- [Sysinternals Suite](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/sysinternals/) - Microsoft's free Windows admin and DFIR toolkit: Process Explorer, Autoruns, Procmon and dozens more. Used by every Windows admin and IR analyst; Microsoft's free toolkit commercial IR teams also rely on. (free, proprietary)
- [Fibratus](https://github.com/rabbitstack/fibratus) - Windows kernel-event tracing with YARA filtering; a Swiss army knife for endpoint visibility. Used by defenders tracing Windows kernel activity for threats; the open-source kernel-observability tool. (Apache-2.0)
- [Threat Hunter Playbook](https://github.com/OTRF/ThreatHunter-Playbook) - Open hunting playbooks as code: hypotheses mapped to data sources and detections. Used by threat hunters learning detection strategy; the open knowledge base for hunting methodology. (MIT)
- [HEARTH](https://github.com/THORCollective/HEARTH) - Community-driven threat-hunting repository: 300+ searchable hunting hypotheses and methodologies with detection-coverage heatmaps, built for hunters to share and collaborate. Used by threat hunters looking for their next hypothesis; the open alternative to closed hunt-playbook libraries.. (MIT per README/package.json)
- [awesome-honeypots](https://github.com/paralax/awesome-honeypots) - The curated honeypot directory: deception tech from low to high interaction. Used by deception researchers picking deployable honeypots. (curated list)
- [Conpot](https://github.com/mushorg/conpot) - ICS/SCADA honeypot: emulates the protocols that run factories, safely. Used by researchers deploying ICS/SCADA honeypots; the open-source deception tool for industrial protocols. (GPL-2.0)
- [FIR](https://github.com/certsocietegenerale/FIR) - Fast Incident Response from Société Générale: ticketing built for IR workflows. Used by incident-response teams managing cases; the open-source IR platform for teams without commercial case tools. (GPL-3.0)
- [Awesome Incident Response](https://github.com/meirwah/awesome-incident-response) - The curated IR toolkit index used across the industry. Used by DFIR professionals assembling their toolkit. (Apache-2.0, curated list)
- [Awesome YARA](https://github.com/InQuest/awesome-yara) - Curated YARA rules, tooling and research for malware hunting. Used by malware analysts collecting detection rules. (CC-BY-4.0, curated list)
- [Krawl](https://github.com/BlessedRebuS/Krawl) - Deception-driven defense: a honeypot server framework for research. Used by defenders discovering and inventorying web assets; the open-source exposure-mapping crawler. (MIT)
- [Shuffle](https://github.com/Shuffle/Shuffle) - Self-hosted SOAR automation: connect your TIP, SIEM and responders in drag-and-drop workflows. Used by SOC teams automating playbooks; the open-source SOAR alternative to Splunk SOAR and XSOAR. (AGPL-3.0)
- [Sublime Platform](https://github.com/sublime-security/sublime-platform) - Open email threat detection: rules-as-code against phishing and BEC. Used by email-security teams defending mail flows; the open-source email-defense platform alternative to commercial email gateways. (MIT)
- [Sublime Rules](https://github.com/sublime-security/sublime-rules) - The community rule corpus for the Sublime platform. Used by email defenders detecting phishing patterns; the open detection ruleset for the Sublime platform. (MIT)
- [go-audit](https://github.com/slackhq/go-audit) - Auditd in JSON for Linux: structured events that pipelines can actually parse. Used by defenders replacing auditd for reliable Linux auditing; the open-source audit daemon for scale. (MIT)
- [Netcap](https://github.com/dreadl0ck/netcap) - Security audit framework over network traffic: sensors, audit and collection from live or captured streams. Used by researchers analyzing network captures as structured data; the open-source framework for network-audit work. (GPL-3.0)
- [Sysmon for Linux](https://github.com/Sysinternals/SysmonForLinux) - The Windows-trusted system monitor ported to Linux. Used by defenders unifying endpoint telemetry across OSes; Microsoft's cross-platform extension of Sysmon. (MIT)
- [msticpy](https://github.com/microsoft/msticpy) - SecOps analysis library from Microsoft: pivots, timelines and hunting helpers for Jupyter. Used by defenders hunting in Microsoft 365 and Sentinel data; Microsoft's open hunting library. (MIT)
- [Elastic detection-rules](https://github.com/elastic/detection-rules) - The detection corpus for Elastic Security: hundreds of maintained rules mapped to MITRE. Used by SOC teams deploying detection content; the open-source ruleset behind Elastic Security. (Elastic-2.0)
- [logarc](https://github.com/FRTX045/logarc) - Offline access-log forensics that reconstructs attacker activity graphs. Used by incident responders analyzing web logs without cloud tooling; the offline log-forensics analyzer. (MIT)
- [TRACE Forensic Toolkit](https://github.com/Gadzhovski/TRACE-Forensic-Toolkit) - All-in-one local DFIR desktop app: read-only analysis of E01/AFF4/raw images across a dozen filesystems, BitLocker/FileVault/LUKS unlock, 70-type carving, timelines and hash-chained custody. Used by forensic examiners and students working cases without commercial licenses; the open DFIR suite for evidence-grade analysis. (MIT)
- [BlueTeam-Tools](https://github.com/A-poc/BlueTeam-Tools) - Community-fed directory of 70+ blue-team tools and operational resources across 13 categories plus practical defensive tradecraft tips. Used by SOC builders assembling their defensive stack; the blue-team counterpart to red-team toolkit lists. (curated list)
- [AIDE (Advanced Intrusion Detection Environment)](https://aide.github.io/) - File integrity monitoring tool creating cryptographic checksums of system files and detecting unauthorized modifications. Used by blue teams; a classic host-based integrity baseline. (GPL-2.0)
- [Any.run](https://any.run/) - Interactive online sandbox with real-time analysis letting analysts observe and interact with malware execution. Used by malware analysts and researchers; a popular cloud sandbox with a genuine free tier. (free tier)
- [Autopsy](https://github.com/sleuthkit/autopsy) - Open-source disk forensics platform with a GUI for analyzing E01, raw, and VHD images plus carving and keyword modules. Used by investigators; a widely adopted free forensic suite.
- [AVML (Acquire Volatile Memory for Linux)](https://github.com/microsoft/avml) - Microsoft's static binary for acquiring volatile memory on Linux systems during incident response. Used by DFIR practitioners; a dependable free acquisition utility. (MIT)
- [Bulk Extractor](https://github.com/simsong/bulk_extractor) - Extracts email addresses, URLs, and other artifacts from raw data and disk images. Used by forensic investigators; a fast free bulk artifact scanner.
- [CAINE](https://www.caine-live.net/) - Digital forensics live Linux distribution. Used by forensic investigators; a ready-to-run environment for evidence analysis.
- [Cortex](https://github.com/TheHive-Project/Cortex) - Observable analysis and active response engine pairing with TheHive for automated enrichment of IPs, domains, and hashes. Used by SOC and IR teams; a powerful open-source analysis backend. (AGPL-3.0)
- [DeepBlueCLI](https://github.com/sans-blue-team/DeepBlueCLI) - PowerShell module for hunt teaming via Windows Event logs, enabling rapid triage of security events from PowerShell. Used by incident responders; a fast free event log triage tool. (GPL-3.0)
- [Digital Forensics Framework (DFF)](https://github.com/arxsys/dff) - Open-source platform for digital forensics with a modular analysis framework. Used by forensic examiners; a free extensible investigation environment. (GPL-2.0)
- [DoD Blue Team Network Lab](https://github.com/arisharazakhan-cpu/dod-blue-team-network-lab) - Virtualized blue team lab simulating DoD-style enterprise defense with pfSense firewall, VLAN segmentation, and Windows Server. Used by defensive security learners; a hands-on training environment.
- [Elastic Stack (ELK)](https://www.elastic.co/) - Infrastructure for log storage, processing, and visualization rather than a complete SIEM, with Elasticsearch and Kibana relicensed. Used by log analysts; a widely adopted open log stack.
- [FakeNet-NG](https://github.com/mandiant/flare-fakenet-ng) - Dynamic network analysis tool by Mandiant simulating internet services to intercept and redirect malware C2 traffic. Used by malware analysts and sandbox operators; enables safe analysis of network behavior. (Apache-2.0)
- [Firmadyne](https://github.com/firmadyne/firmadyne) - Automated firmware emulation platform for Linux-based IoT devices enabling dynamic analysis in QEMU. Used by firmware analysts; a foundational emulation tool. (MIT)
- [FirmAE](https://github.com/pr0v3rbs/FirmAE) - Automated firmware emulation platform that boots extracted Linux-based IoT firmware in QEMU for dynamic testing. Used by vulnerability researchers; a leading emulation framework. (MIT)
- [FLARE VM](https://github.com/mandiant/flare-vm) - Windows security distribution bundling hundreds of curated tools for malware analysis, reverse engineering, and incident response. Used by analysts and DFIR teams; a comprehensive ready-to-deploy analysis workstation. (Apache-2.0)
- [FLOSS](https://github.com/mandiant/flare-floss) - Mandiant's FLARE Obfuscated String Solver that automatically extracts and deobfuscates strings from malware binaries. Used by malware analysts and reverse engineers; a leading automated string extraction tool. (Apache-2.0)
- [Foremost](https://foremost.sourceforge.net/) - File carving tool that recovers data from disk images based on file headers. Used by forensic investigators; a classic free carving utility.
- [Graylog](https://graylog.org/) - Centralizes logs and provides alerting and dashboards through a polished interface. Used by log analysts; a capable open-source log management platform. (SSPL)
- [GRR Rapid Response](https://github.com/google/grr) - Incident response framework focused on remote live forensics with a Python agent on assets and server infrastructure. Used by IR teams; a scalable open-source forensic collection platform. (Apache-2.0)
- [Hashdeep](https://github.com/jessek/hashdeep) - File hashing tool with recursive hashing and audit mode for verifying evidence integrity. Used by forensic examiners; a dependable free hashing utility.
- [Hayabusa Lens](https://pypi.org/project/hayabusa-lens/) - Desktop application wrapping Hayabusa and Chainsaw with a dashboard, Sigma rule viewer, and AI investigation helpers. Used by DFIR analysts; a convenient Windows event triage frontend.
- [HELK (Hunting ELK)](https://github.com/Cyb3rWard0g/HELK) - All-in-one free threat hunting stack built on Elasticsearch, Logstash, Kafka, and Kibana with Jupyter Notebook integrations. Used by threat hunters; a comprehensive open hunting platform. (GPL-3.0)
- [Hybrid Analysis](https://www.hybrid-analysis.com/) - Public sandbox powered by Falcon Sandbox providing automated malware analysis with detailed behavioral reports. Used by analysts and researchers; a widely used free cloud analysis service. (free tier)
- [Malcolm](https://github.com/cisagov/Malcolm) - Network traffic analysis tool suite for full packet capture artifacts, Zeek logs, and Suricata alerts, published by CISA. Used by blue teams and incident responders; a deployable platform for deep traffic visibility. (Apache-2.0)
- [Maltrail](https://github.com/stamparm/maltrail) - Malicious traffic detection system using publicly available blocklists and custom blacklists. Used by network defenders and SOC analysts; a simple sensor for spotting known-bad traffic. (MIT)
- [malware-jail](https://github.com/HynekPetrak/malware-jail) - Sandbox for semi-automatic JavaScript malware analysis, deobfuscation and payload extraction. Used by malware analysts and DFIR teams; a practical JS analysis environment. (MIT)
- [masscanned](https://github.com/ivre/masscanned) - Low-interaction honeypot focused on network scanners and bots, integrating with IVRE for self-hosted threat intelligence. Used by blue teams and threat researchers; a lightweight sensor for capturing scanning activity. (GPL-3.0)
- [Nessus](https://www.tenable.com/products/nessus) - Vulnerability assessment and security scanning platform from Tenable. Used by defenders and sysadmins; Nessus Essentials is the long-standing free tier for home scanning. (free tier)
- [oletools](https://github.com/decalage2/oletools) - Python tools for analyzing Microsoft OLE2 files including Office documents and MSI files, targeting malicious macros and embedded objects. Used by malware analysts and document forensicators; an essential Office analysis toolkit. (NOASSERTION)
- [OpenSearch](https://opensearch.org/) - Fork of Elasticsearch and Kibana led by AWS, including OpenSearch and OpenSearch Dashboards for visualization. Used by log analysts; a truly open search and analytics stack. (Apache-2.0)
- [Pdf-parser](https://blog.didierstevens.com/programs/pdf-tools/) - Didier Stevens' PDF analysis tools for extracting and examining content from suspicious PDF files. Used by malware analysts; a trusted free PDF forensics toolkit.
- [PEStudio](https://www.winitor.com/) - Static analysis tool for Windows executables examining PE headers, imports, exports, strings, and indicators without execution. Used by malware analysts and triagers; a fast first-pass PE inspection utility. (free, proprietary)
- [PEView](https://www.aldeid.com/wiki/PEview) - Portable executable file viewer for inspecting PE headers and structure during malware analysis. Used by reverse engineers; a simple free static analysis viewer.
- [PhotoRec](https://www.cgsecurity.org/wiki/PhotoRec) - File recovery software that carves deleted files from disks and memory cards. Used by forensics examiners and recovery specialists; a free companion to TestDisk.
- [Plaso / log2timeline](https://github.com/log2timeline/plaso) - Super-timeline engine that extracts timestamps from dozens of forensic sources into a unified chronology. Used by forensic analysts; the standard open-source timeline builder. (Apache-2.0)
- [Process Hacker](https://processhacker.sourceforge.io/) - Advanced Windows process viewer and memory editor offering detailed inspection of processes, threads, memory regions, and handles. Used by malware analysts and reverse engineers; a powerful live-system inspection tool. (free, proprietary)
- [Process Monitor (Procmon)](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/sysinternals/downloads/procmon) - Sysinternals tool monitoring real-time file system, registry, and process/thread activity for dynamic analysis. Used by malware analysts and incident responders; a core Windows behavioral observation utility. (free, proprietary)
- [RedSentinel](https://pypi.org/project/redsentinel/) - AI-assisted defensive security assessment framework orchestrating Nmap, Nikto, and WhatWeb, normalizing output and enriching findings. Used by blue teamers; a defensive scanning orchestrator.
- [RegRipper](https://github.com/keydet89/RegRipper3.0) - Windows registry analysis tool for parsing and extracting forensic artifacts from hive files. Used by incident responders; a widely relied-upon free registry parser.
- [REMnux](https://remnux.org/) - Linux toolkit for reverse-engineering and analyzing malicious software, providing a curated collection of free community tools in a preconfigured environment. Used by malware analysts and incident responders; a standard Linux analysis distro. (free, proprietary)
- [S.I.D.E. (SCADA Intrusion Detection Environment)](https://github.com/UmbertoDellaMonica/S.I.D.E.) - Intrusion detection system designed for ICS/SCADA networks, monitoring devices, detecting anomalies, and providing real-time alerts. Used by OT defenders; a focused blue-team monitor for industrial traffic. (MIT)
- [SecurityOnion](https://securityonion.net/) - Functions as both a SIEM and intrusion detection system, integrating Snort, Suricata, and Wazuh for defense. Used by blue teams; a comprehensive open-source security platform.
- [SELKS](https://www.stamus-networks.com/open-source) - Suricata-based IDS/IPS platform for network detection. Used by defenders and SOC analysts; an open-source intrusion detection stack.
- [SentinelOps](https://github.com/USH3R/BLUETEAM.-SentinelOps.-Defense-Detection-System-Dashboard) - Lightweight defensive platform ingesting system and network logs to detect brute-force attempts, anomalous logins, and misuse patterns. Used by SOC analysts; a simple detection dashboard.
- [Snort](https://www.snort.org/) - Classic signature-based intrusion detection system widely used for real-time traffic analysis and packet logging. Used by network defenders and SOC teams; a long-standing staple of open-source IDS deployments. (GPL-2.0)
- [TestDisk](https://www.cgsecurity.org/wiki/TestDisk) - Disk recovery tool that restores lost partitions and repairs filesystem structures. Used by forensics and recovery technicians; a reliable free utility.
- [The DFIR Report](https://thedfirreport.com/) - Real-world incident response case studies with detailed timelines, IOCs, and attacker TTPs. Used by blue teamers and threat hunters; an essential reference for detection and response.
- [Volatility](https://www.volatilityfoundation.org/) - Memory forensics framework extracting artifacts from RAM dumps, including hidden processes, injected code, and network connections. Used by DFIR analysts and forensic investigators; the leading memory analysis framework. (free, proprietary)
- [Hayabusa](https://github.com/Yamato-Security/hayabusa) - Windows EVTX analysis + Sigma detection. (AGPL-3.0)

## 🤖 The Oracle's Domain — AI Security & LLM Security Tools
Intro: Secure the new surface — guardrails, model-file safety and signing for AI-powered applications.

- [NeMo Guardrails](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NeMo-Guardrails) - Programmable guardrail layer for LLM apps: input/output rails, dialog safety, topic control. Used by AI engineers adding safety rails to LLM apps; NVIDIA's open-source guardrail toolkit for production deployments.
- [modelscan](https://github.com/protectai/modelscan) - Scan model files (pickle, safetensors) for malicious code before you load them — supply-chain defense for ML. Used by AI security teams scanning model files for pickle and format exploits; the open-source scanner for ML supply chains.
- [OWASP Top 10 for LLM Applications](https://genai.owasp.org) - The standard risk taxonomy for LLM apps; the shared language for AI threat modeling. Used by AppSec teams auditing LLM systems; the industry-standard risk list everyone maps AI security to.
- [Awesome Threat Intelligence](https://github.com/hslatman/awesome-threat-intelligence) - The curated threat-intelligence tools and platform index. Used by CTI practitioners assembling sources and tools. (Apache-2.0, curated list)
- [guardana](https://github.com/guardana/guardana) - AI-security verification platform: 58 deterministic rules across model artifacts, live LLM, MCP and A2A endpoints and OTel traces, mapped to OWASP LLM/Agentic/MCP, ATLAS and NIST. Used by AI security teams auditing model stacks deterministically; the pre-1.0 release-candidate platform worth tracking. (Apache-2.0)
- [model-transparency](https://github.com/sigstore/model-transparency) - Signing for ML models: verify exactly which model you are deploying. Used by AI governance teams documenting model provenance; the AI Alliance's open standard for model transparency labels. (Apache-2.0)
- [Penthera](https://github.com/danoszz/penthera) - Offline rule-based security agent that audits app code, dependencies and AI coding tooling (Claude Code, Cursor, MCP servers) with deterministic fixes applied to a local branch — zero cloud calls. Used by teams hardening AI-assisted codebases with NIS2/CRA-ready evidence; the self-hosted AI-tooling auditor with SARIF output. (MIT)
- [HackAgent](https://github.com/AISecurityLab/hackagent) - Red-team toolkit for AI models and agents: actively tests jailbreaks, prompt injection and tool misuse, then scores each reply with judge models for measured per-attack success rates. Used by AI security teams quantifying agent attack surface; the open-source AI red-teaming harness with built-in scoring. (Apache-2.0)
- [weir](https://github.com/IdoGol24/weir) - Leak tests for AI agents: deterministically finds every place an agent exposes credentials in telemetry, model calls or other services. Used by agent developers testing their own deployments before release; a focused open-source AI-agent leak auditor. (Apache-2.0)
- [CL4R1T4S](https://github.com/elder-plinius/CL4R1T4S) - The community archive of leaked and reverse-engineered system prompts from ChatGPT, Claude, Gemini, Grok, Cursor and other major models — provenance-tagged and continuously updated. Used by AI governance teams auditing prompt transparency and researchers studying instruction leakage; the reference corpus for prompt-disclosure audits. (AGPL-3.0, copyleft)
- [AgentHound](https://github.com/adithyan-ak/AgentHound) - Offensive security framework for AI agent infrastructure: one collector sweep over MCP servers, A2A agents, LiteLLM gateways, Ollama/vLLM, Qdrant, MLflow and Jupyter yields secrets, services and differential proof (control vs credentialed), then a queryable attack graph. Used by red teams mapping the agentic stack; the BloodHound-style tool for AI infrastructure. (Apache-2.0)
- [EasyJailbreak](https://github.com/EasyJailbreak/EasyJailbreak) - Framework for building and testing adversarial jailbreak prompts against LLMs with modular attack components. Used by AI security researchers; a flexible jailbreak experimentation toolkit. (GPL-3.0)
- [Fickling](https://github.com/trailofbits/fickling) - Pickle file analysis and decompilation tool detecting malicious pickle payloads through allowlist-based validation. Used by ML engineers and security researchers; a strong defense against pickle-based model attacks. (LGPL-3.0)
- [FlareVM MCP Server](https://github.com/zixuantemp/flarevm-mcp) - Model Context Protocol server enabling AI agents to drive an isolated, air-gapped FlareVM through 52 unified tools. Used by AI-assisted malware analysts; a bridge between LLM agents and analysis sandboxes. (MIT)
- [Guardrails AI](https://github.com/guardrails-ai/guardrails) - Python framework adding input/output guards, validators, and structured-output controls to LLM applications. Used by AI engineers and security teams; a practical LLM safety and validation layer. (Apache-2.0)
- [HarmBench](https://github.com/centerforaisafety/HarmBench) - ICML 2024 standardized evaluation framework for automated red-teaming and robust-refusal benchmarking of LLMs. Used by AI safety researchers; a rigorous methodology for measuring model harmfulness. (MIT)
- [LangKit](https://github.com/whylabs/langkit) - LLM monitoring toolkit from WhyLabs extracting safety and security signals like jailbreak and prompt-injection similarity. Used by ML engineers and AI security teams; a practical runtime LLM guardrail library. (Apache-2.0)
- [LLAMATOR](https://github.com/LLAMATOR-Core/llamator) - Red-teaming framework for chatbots and GenAI systems providing automated adversarial testing methodologies. Used by AI security testers; a structured conversational-AI safety evaluation tool. (NOASSERTION)
- [Meta Llama Prompt Guard 2](https://huggingface.co/meta-llama/Llama-Prompt-Guard-2-86M) - Meta's prompt-injection classifier on HuggingFace, fine-tuned from mDeBERTa with adversarial-resistant tokenization. Used by LLM application developers and AI security teams; a lightweight prompt-injection detection model. (free, proprietary)
- [PurpleLlama](https://github.com/meta-llama/PurpleLlama) - Meta's open-source LLM security toolkit including Llama Guard classifiers, CodeShield, and CyberSecEval benchmarks. Used by AI developers and safety researchers; a comprehensive LLM safety evaluation suite. (NOASSERTION)
- [SmartScanner](https://www.thesmartscanner.com/) - AI-driven web vulnerability assessment tool that automates security scanning for websites, APIs, and web applications. Used by web security testers; a commercial scanner with a free tier for automated checks. (free tier)
- [ZeroTrust](https://github.com/sattyamjjain/zerotrust) - Privacy-first Chrome extension that analyzes website security locally with on-device WebGPU AI, scoring trust from HTTPS and phishing signals. Used by privacy-conscious users; a local AI security assessor. (MIT)

## 🧠 The AI Forge — AI-Dependent Security Tools

Intro: Security tools with an LLM as the engine — red-team agents, pentest copilots and scenario
generators. Every entry needs an LLM API key (yours or the target's); none are SaaS wrappers.

- [PentAGI](https://github.com/vxcontrol/pentagi) - Fully autonomous AI-agent penetration testing: multi-agent workflows, browser control, reporting. Used by red teams experimenting with autonomous pentest agents; the open-source platform for agentic security testing. (MIT)
- [Xorcise](https://github.com/xorcise-ai/xorcise) - Runs cyber-AI agents against prepared missions in isolated environments, with OpenTelemetry-based observability on every action. Used by teams evaluating agent capability and safety before deployment; the open gym for offensive AI agents. (Apache-2.0)
- [Apex](https://github.com/pensarai/apex) - Autonomous offensive-security agents that run directly in the terminal, driving tools end-to-end on scoped targets. Used by pentesters delegating recon-to-exploit chains on authorized engagements; a terminal-native AI red-team operator. (Apache-2.0)
- [strix](https://github.com/usestrix/strix) - Autonomous AI pentesting agent framework: sandboxed multi-agent teams, a 34-playbook vulnerability knowledge base, Caido MITM proxy integration, automated PoC validation and reporting. Used by security teams delegating scoped pentest workflows to supervised AI agents; the open-source agentic pentest framework with a large community. (Apache-2.0)
- [PyRIT](https://github.com/microsoft/PyRIT) - Microsoft's AI red-teaming framework: automated adversarial probing of generative AI, with scoring and reporting. Used by AI red teams automating LLM risk probing; Microsoft's open-source toolkit for generative-AI security testing. (MIT)
- [PentestGPT](https://github.com/GreyDGL/PentestGPT) - LLM-driven pentest copilot: parses recon output, plans next steps, keeps session context across test phases. Used by pentesters and learners getting guided next-step reasoning during engagements; the GPT-powered attack assistant. (MIT)
- [garak](https://github.com/NVIDIA/garak) - NVIDIA's LLM vulnerability scanner: hundreds of probes for injection, data leakage, hallucination and misuse. Used by AI security engineers probing LLMs for known vulnerability classes; NVIDIA's open-source LLM vulnerability scanner. (Apache-2.0)
- [promptfoo](https://github.com/promptfoo/promptfoo) - LLM evaluation and red-teaming framework: prompt-injection and jailbreak test suites that run in CI. Used by AI engineers evaluating and red-teaming prompts in CI; the open-source testing framework for LLM quality and safety. (MIT)
- [deepteam](https://github.com/confident-ai/deepteam) - Red-teaming library for LLMs: simulated attacks with vulnerability scoring and reporting. Used by AI teams running red-teaming simulations on their LLMs; the open-source library for adversarial testing. (Apache-2.0)
- [Nebula](https://github.com/berylliumsec/nebula) - Cybersecurity harness that pairs an LLM with common pentest tools for guided exploitation workflows. Used by security researchers exploring LLM-assisted pentesting workflows; the open-source agent for offensive research. (BSD-2-Clause)
- [hackingBuddyGPT](https://github.com/ipa-lab/hackingBuddyGPT) - LLM pentesting research harness: autonomous Linux privilege-escalation experiments in ~50 lines of code. Used by researchers studying LLM agents in ethical hacking; the research framework for autonomous pentest agents. (MIT)
- [AttackGen](https://github.com/mrwadams/attackgen) - LLM-powered, threat-informed exercise generation: realistic attack scenarios and mitigations for incident-response testing. Used by purple teams generating attack scenarios from threat intel; the open-source exercise-planning tool. (GPL-3.0)
- [rea](https://github.com/morluto/rea) - Open-source MCP coding agent that reproduces any web app's features from a live URL — the viral reference for agentic AI engineering, MCP-native and self-hosted. Used by researchers benchmarking reasoning-acting agents on security tasks; the open research agent for agentic testing. (MIT)
- [RedPilot](https://github.com/geevarghesekthomas84-sys/redpilot) - AI-orchestrated recon and scan pipeline chaining nuclei, nmap, ffuf, subfinder and httpx for authorized testing. Used by pentesters automating multi-tool recon under one AI orchestrator; the LLM-driven pipeline glue for standard recon stacks. (MIT)
- [Mitsu](https://github.com/ARCANGEL0/Mitsu) - Chibi red-team operator: connect an AI provider, set the scope, and it drives recon through exploitation via 165+ MCP-connected tools with live session intel. Used by red teamers running guided or autonomous authorized engagements; the MCP-native AI operator for red teams. (MIT)

- [Mantis](https://github.com/google/mantis) - Google's ADK-based toolkit that lets AI coding agents autonomously find, reproduce and patch vulnerabilities in source code — LLM-driven SAST with a severity-calibration rubric and anti-hallucination triage. Used by security engineers researching agentic vuln discovery; the flagship example of agent-driven security code review (needs paid LLM APIs). (Apache-2.0)
- [ARTEX](https://github.com/trhhubsd/ARTEX) - Multi-agent LLM pentest orchestrator with human approval gates and asset graphing. Used by red teams running supervised multi-agent engagements; the open orchestrator for LLM-driven pentesting. (AGPL-3.0, copyleft)
- [Cybermes](https://github.com/Zyrexnn/Cybermes) - Autonomous offensive security, bug bounty and red-teaming agent framework with MCP integration and a built-in vulnerable lab. Used by offensive teams running autonomous agents; the Apache-licensed agentic framework with curated playbooks. (Apache-2.0)
- [open-kritt](https://github.com/Kritt-ai/open-kritt) - Self-hosted AI vulnerability-research platform that orchestrates agents to find, validate, deduplicate and rank security issues in code. Used by security teams wanting AI-assisted code review without sending code to a vendor; the self-hosted alternative to commercial AI code-scanning SaaS.. (AGPL-3.0)
- [BugTraceAI](https://github.com/BugTraceAI/BugTraceAI) - Self-hosted agentic pentesting suite with evidence-first validation across an 8-repo ecosystem (CLI, WEB, API, Launcher). Used by security teams wanting self-hosted agentic scanning; the open alternative to SaaS AI pentest platforms. (Apache-2.0)
- [Moxy](https://github.com/matank001/Moxy) - Agentic MITM proxy with local-Ollama auto-testing built on mitmproxy. Used by web testers adding LLM-assisted traffic analysis; the open agentic layer for mitmproxy workflows. (Apache-2.0)
- [PHANTOM](https://github.com/OmYarewar110/PHANTOM) - AI-powered pentesting command center with MCP and reverse-engineering integration. Used by pentesters orchestrating AI-assisted engagements; the open AI pentest cockpit. (MIT)
- [WonderSuite Ai Bug Bounty](https://github.com/sfr-development/WonderSuite-Ai-Bug-Bounty) - Desktop-native AI offensive research engine with stealth browser and MCP agents. Used by bug bounty researchers automating recon-to-report workflows; the desktop AI engine for offensive research. (MIT)
- [plonix](https://github.com/SergeyMalych/plonix) - AI-native Rust MITM workbench with adaptive scope validation. Used by web security testers wanting an AI-native proxy; the Rust MITM workbench with LLM integration. (Apache-2.0)
- [bounty-operator](https://github.com/bountyoperator/bounty-operator) - AI-powered bug bounty report triage that flags evidence gaps and duplicate risk before submission. Used by bug bounty hunters polishing reports before triage; the pre-submission report validator. (MIT)
- [VISTA](https://github.com/Adw0rm-sec/VISTA) - LLM-augmented Burp triage and workflow automation assistant. Used by Burp users triaging findings with LLM assistance; the open Burp copilot for triage. (MIT)
- [Hekate](https://github.com/PhaelAwesome/Hekate) - Burp Suite passive extension with local-LLM triage and auto-validation to cut scanner false positives. Used by Burp users filtering scanner noise with local models; the open FP-reduction companion for Burp. (GPL-3.0)
- [RedAmon](https://github.com/samugit83/redamon) - Autonomous AI pentest framework chaining recon → exploit → triage → code-fix with human approval gates, a Neo4j attack-surface graph and bidirectional MCP support; its maintainers report a 97% black-box solve rate on the XBOW benchmark. Used by security teams researching supervised agentic testing; the approval-gated reference for AI-driven pentesting. (MIT)
- [PentesterFlow Agent](https://github.com/PentesterFlow/agent) - Human-in-the-loop agentic pentest CLI: LLM-driven recon, enumeration, validation and reporting from the terminal, with evidence-gated anti-hallucination findings and full audit logging. Used by pentesters delegating scoped workflow steps to a supervised agent; the analyst-oversight-first agentic TUI. (Apache-2.0)
- [T3MP3ST](https://github.com/elder-plinius/T3MP3ST) - Multi-agent offensive-security meta-framework that repurposes AI coding agents (Claude Code, Codex, local models via Ollama) as autonomous pentest brains, with a browser War Room UI and a verify-claims integrity layer; a reported 90% pass@1 score on the XBEN challenge suite. Used by red teams researching agentic kill chains reproducibly; the research harness for agent-driven offense. (AGPL-3.0, copyleft)
- [DarkMoon](https://github.com/ASCIT31/Dark-Moon) - Autonomous AI pentest platform orchestrating 50 specialist agents across web, AD, cloud, Kubernetes and LLM endpoints (OWASP LLM Top 10), with a Privacy Gateway that tokenizes hosts and credentials locally before any LLM call. Used by teams researching full-scope agentic testing with evidence capture; dual-use — run only against authorized targets. (GPL-3.0)
- [Suijin](https://github.com/0xwi11iam/Suijin) - Fully autonomous red-team/pentest framework built on LangGraph that plans, executes and documents attacks with a POC catalog and severity-honest reporting. Used by researchers exploring agent-driven pentesting with human oversight; the open autonomous-pentest lab. (AGPL-3.0)
- [Frida Game Hacking MCP](https://pypi.org/project/iflow-mcp_0xhackerfren-frida-game-hacking-mcp/) - Model Context Protocol server that turns AI agents into game hacking assistants using Frida for memory operations, function hooking, and code patching. Used by AI-assisted reverse engineers; an experimental bridge between LLM agents and live process manipulation.
- [mobile-security-mcp](https://pypi.org/project/mobile-security-mcp/) - MCP server that gives AI agents automated access to Android reverse-engineering tools like apktool and jadx. Used by mobile security researchers; a novel AI-driven analysis bridge.
- [ReClass.NET-MCP](https://github.com/NateWeav/ReClass.NET-MCP) - MCP integration for ReClass.NET that exposes memory read/write, class/node management, and process inspection to AI coding assistants. Used by reverse engineers; an experimental bridge between LLM tooling and live memory analysis.

## 🆓 The Free Bazaar — Free Cybersecurity APIs, Feeds & Data
Intro: Free (or free-tier) data sources every security program should be using — no budget required.

- [Shodan](https://www.shodan.io) - The search engine for internet-exposed devices; free-tier lookups plus Monitor alerts for your own assets. Used by CTI analysts and bug hunters mapping exposed assets; the original internet-exposure search engine. (free tier)
- [VirusTotal](https://www.virustotal.com) - The multi-engine file/URL reputation service with a free public API. Used by SOC analysts everywhere for file, URL and domain reputation; the multi-engine standard, free for basic use.
- [HaveIBeenPwned](https://haveibeenpwned.com) - Breach database lookups for emails and whole domains, with a free API. Used by everyone from helpdesks to auditors checking breach exposure; the free breach-notification standard.
- [Censys](https://search.censys.io) - Internet asset search with certificate-aware pivots; free academic/community tier. Used by CTI teams hunting certificates and services across the internet; the free-tier search companion to Shodan. (free tier)
- [Trickest Cloud](https://github.com/trickest/cloud) - Weekly-refreshed SSL-certificate dataset covering all AWS EC2 and GCP public IPv4 ranges; captures self-signed, ephemeral and never-CT-submitted certificates that CT logs miss. Used by attack-surface teams enriching cloud IP inventories with certificate data; the free weekly cloud-cert dataset. (MIT, dataset)
- [GreyNoise](https://www.greynoise.io) - Classifies internet-wide scanner noise so your team can ignore it; free community API. Used by SOC teams separating internet background noise from real targeting; the community API is free.
- [AbuseIPDB](https://www.abuseipdb.com) - Community-driven IP-abuse reports with a free API (1,000 checks/day). Used by firewall admins checking and reporting malicious IPs; the free crowd-sourced reputation feed.
- [urlscan.io](https://urlscan.io) - URL scanning with sandbox screenshots and full resource capture; free tier. Used by analysts detonating and inspecting URLs safely; the free-tier scanning and screenshot service. (free tier)
- [AlienVault OTX](https://otx.alienvault.com) - Open threat exchange: community-contributed threat pulses with a free API. Used by SOC teams exchanging threat indicators; the free open threat-exchange with millions of pulses.
- [PhishTank](https://phishtank.org) - The community phishing-URL database with a free API. Used by mail defenders validating phishing URLs; the free community phishing database.
- [abuse.ch](https://abuse.ch) - The free malware-intel feed suite: URLhaus, ThreatFox, MalwareBazaar and Feodo Tracker. Used by defenders consuming URLhaus, MalwareBazaar and ThreatFox feeds; the free threat feeds many commercial products ingest.
- [CISA KEV Catalog](https://www.cisa.gov/known-exploited-vulnerabilities-catalog) - The Known Exploited Vulnerabilities list; patch-first prioritization, straight from the source. Used by patch-priority teams worldwide; the free authoritative list of actively exploited vulnerabilities.
- [NVD](https://nvd.nist.gov) - The National Vulnerability Database feeds and API (free API key) — the backbone of CVE enrichment. Used by every security team for CVE enrichment; the free NIST vulnerability database the industry keys on.
- [OSV.dev](https://osv.dev) - Google's open-source vulnerability database with a clean free API and per-package queries. Used by developers checking OSS vulnerabilities; Google's free vulnerability database for supply chains.
- [FIRST EPSS](https://www.first.org/epss) - Exploit-prediction scores for every CVE — probability-based patch prioritization, free data. Used by risk teams scoring exploitation probability for patch prioritization; the free open data model replacing gut-feel CVSS-only triage.
- [Exploit-DB](https://www.exploit-db.com) - The classic public exploit archive and Google Hacking Database; still where PoCs surface first. Used by pentesters finding public exploit references; the veteran free exploit archive.
- [Trickest CVE](https://github.com/trickest/cve) - Repository gathering and updating all available and newest CVEs with their PoCs. Used by researchers tracking fresh exploit code for newly published CVEs; a comprehensive public CVE+PoC index. (MIT)
- [PoC Index](https://github.com/0xMarcio/pocindex) - Search public CVE PoCs across GitHub, Nuclei, ExploitDB, Metasploit and Vulhub, with CVSS, EPSS and CISA KEV context. Used by defenders prioritizing what actually has exploit code; the searchable index of public PoC sources. (MIT)
- [vulnerability-poc](https://github.com/fankh/vulnerability-poc) - CVE proof-of-concept labs, exploit scripts and matching detection and prevention rules (Nginx, Apache, Snort, YARA) for high-severity CVEs, with a machine-readable catalog. Used by detection engineers and researchers reproducing and detecting real CVEs; the free PoC-plus-detection corpus.. (Apache-2.0)
- [SecLists](https://github.com/danielmiessler/SecLists) - The security tester's wordlist collection: usernames, passwords, URLs, fuzz payloads — the one corpus everyone uses. Used by every pentester and bug hunter for wordlists; the standard free collection no commercial tool improves on.
- [Splunk security_content](https://github.com/splunk/security_content) - The maintained detection-content corpus: thousands of searches and analytic stories mapped to ATT&CK. Used by Splunk SOC teams deploying detections; the free ES content library from the research team.
- [MDN HTTP Observatory](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/observatory/) - Mozilla's free no-key scanner that grades your site's security headers and best practices. Used by web developers scoring site security headers; the free Mozilla scanner for quick posture checks.
- [Security Datasets](https://github.com/OTRF/Security-Datasets) - OTRF's library of pre-recorded adversary-emulation event logs (Mordor's successor home) for detection testing; frozen library by design. Used by detection engineers testing content against realistic telemetry; the free datasets for purple-team validation.
- [EVTX-ATTACK-SAMPLES](https://github.com/sbousseaden/EVTX-ATTACK-SAMPLES) - A large collection of real Windows event logs capturing ATT&CK techniques — test detections without a lab; frozen dataset by design. Used by detection engineers replaying attack artifacts in Windows event logs; the free sample corpus for rule testing. (GPL-3.0)
- [MITRE Cyber Analytics Repository](https://github.com/mitre-attack/car) - CAR: curated, versioned detection analytics with full logic and ATT&CK mappings. Used by detection engineers implementing analytics; the free repository of detection logic mapped to ATT&CK.
- [Boss of the SOC (BOTS)](https://github.com/splunk/botsv3) - Splunk's public blue-team CTF dataset: full realistic telemetry for investigations, CC0 data; frozen CTF dataset by design. Used by training teams running SIEM CTFs; the free Splunk datasets behind the famous range.
- [Packet Storm](https://packetstormsecurity.com/) - The advisory and exploit-db archive; a quarter-century of security disclosures. Used by researchers tracking advisories and tool releases; the veteran free security archive. (free service)
- [Awesome Pentest](https://github.com/enaqx/awesome-pentest) - The most-starred pentest resource collection; the community canon. Used by practitioners navigating the pentest tool landscape. (CC-BY-4.0, curated list)
- [Awesome Web Hacking](https://github.com/infoslack/awesome-web-hacking) - Curated web-security research: talks, papers and tooling. Used by web hunters collecting methodology resources. (MIT, curated list)
- [Bug Bounties](https://github.com/lissy93/bug-bounties) - Community-maintained directory, MCP and API of 3,000+ active bug bounty programs and VDPs. Used by hunters picking targets and checking scope before engaging; the open dataset for program discovery. (MIT)
- [Splunk attack_data](https://github.com/splunk/attack_data) - Curated attack datasets for detection validation, mapped to MITRE techniques. Used by detection engineers replaying attacks in Splunk; the free datasets for content validation. (Apache-2.0)
- [onion-lookup](https://github.com/ail-project/onion-lookup) - A free API for .onion existence checks; part of the AIL stack. Used by researchers checking dark-web onion services safely; the free lookup for onion intelligence. (AGPL-3.0)
- [Chaos](https://chaos.projectdiscovery.io) - The internet-wide subdomain dataset from ProjectDiscovery; free for community use. Used by bug bounty hunters discovering subdomains via ProjectDiscovery's free API; the free attack-surface dataset. (free API)
- [SearchWebCode](https://searchwebcode.com) - Search engine over the HTML/JS/CSS of 127M websites; source-level search without crawling. Used by hunters searching public code for exposures; the free code-search angle for recon. (free service)
- [OpenSanctions](https://opensanctions.org) - Open data on sanctioned entities and PEPs; the compliance watchlist as a dataset. Used by compliance investigators screening entities against watchlists; the free open-data alternative to commercial screening services. (open data)
- [Destroylist](https://github.com/phishdestroy/destroylist) - Real-time phishing, scam and crypto-drainer domain blocklist: 205k+ manually curated plus 1M+ community-aggregated domains, DNS-verified and shipped as keyless hosts/AdGuard/RPZ/JSON feeds for any DNS firewall. Used by defenders blocking live phishing infrastructure at Pi-hole/AdGuard scale; the community blocklist for next-gen DNS filtering. (MIT)
- [HProxy Free Proxy List](https://github.com/hproxy-com/free-proxy-list) - Continuously verified keyless proxy feed: 100+ public sources re-tested daily for protocol, anonymity grade, geolocation, latency and uptime, served as txt/JSON/CSV plus a free API and MCP server. Used by security teams auditing whether their own IP space appears on public proxy feeds — detection intel, never egress infrastructure. (MIT)
- [nuclei-wordfence-cve](https://github.com/topscoder/nuclei-wordfence-cve) - Daily-regenerated corpus of 87k+ Nuclei templates covering WordPress core, plugin and theme vulnerabilities, built from Wordfence's production threat-intel feeds with recalculated severities. Used by WordPress security teams extending nuclei scans beyond the default template packs; the keyless machine-curated WP vulnerability feed. (MIT)
- [APIFreaks](https://apifreaks.com/) - API hub delivering over a hundred real-time endpoints across IP, domain, and email intelligence categories. Used by developers and analysts; a broad free-tier feed for enrichment lookups. (free tier)
- [cyber-threat-intel-rss-feeds](https://github.com/iNadeemDev/cyber-threat-intel-rss-feeds) - Curated list of cybersecurity threat intelligence blogs and RSS feeds for SOC analysts and researchers. Used by defenders building awareness; a convenient feed starter set.
- [cyber_rss](https://github.com/mr-r3b00t/cyber_rss) - Organized list of security feeds covering CERT advisories, vulnerabilities, vendor advisories, malware, and exploit research. Used by SOC analysts and researchers; a broad feed aggregation resource.
- [Emailrep.io](https://emailrep.io/) - Email reputation and breach API. Used by defenders and developers; a feed for assessing address risk. (free tier)
- [infosec-opml](https://github.com/phretor/infosec-opml) - Curated collection of 366 information security RSS and Atom feeds organized by reading action and urgency. Used by analysts and researchers; a ready-made feed bundle for any RSS reader. (MIT)
- [IP Geolocation](https://ipgeolocation.io/) - IP geolocation and threat intelligence API covering location, company, ASN, and VPN or proxy and Tor usage. Used by developers and analysts; a free-tier feed for enriching IP data. (free tier)
- [IP2Location.io](https://www.ip2location.io/) - IP geolocation and intelligence API returning location, network, and related details for addresses. Used by developers and analysts; a free-tier feed for enriching IP data. (free tier)
- [ipapi.is](https://ipapi.is/) - IP intelligence and geolocation service identifying locations, ISPs, hosting providers, and VPN or proxy connections. Used by developers and fraud teams; a free-tier feed for IP enrichment. (free tier)
- [ProxyDetect.live](https://proxydetect.live/) - Proxy detection service that checks IP addresses for proxies, VPNs, and other anonymization services. Used by defenders and fraud analysts; a free lookup for flagging anonymized traffic. (free, proprietary)
- [Quttera Website Security API](https://quttera.com/quttera-anti-malware-api-help) - API for malware detection, reputation checks, and website security analysis. Used by developers and SOC teams; a practical feed for wiring scanning into their own pipelines. (free tier)
- [SatNOGS DB](https://db.satnogs.org/) - Open database of satellite frequencies, modulations, and operational status maintained by the SatNOGS community for planning satellite signal reception. Used by ground-station operators and satellite researchers; a community-curated frequency reference.
- [urlcap](https://urlcap.com/?utm_source=chatgpt.com) - HTTP capture and analysis API for inspecting requests and responses, extracting structured data, and detecting bots. Used by developers and defenders; a free-tier feed for web traffic analysis. (free tier)
- [WhoisFreaks](https://whoisfreaks.com/) - Domain WHOIS, historical WHOIS, and DNS intelligence API for tracking ownership and registration timelines. Used by investigators and developers; a free-tier feed for domain attribution. (free tier)
- [Netlas](https://netlas.io/) - internet-registry search, free research tier. (free tier)

## 🎓 The Academy — Cybersecurity Learning Resources & Career Paths
Intro: Learn the craft — free academies, knowledge bases, walkthroughs and career roadmaps.

- [PortSwigger Web Security Academy](https://portswigger.net/web-security) - Free, best-in-class web-hacking labs from the Burp Suite team; the standard web-security curriculum. Used by aspiring bug bounty hunters and web pentesters; the free training platform from the Burp Suite makers that rivals paid courses.
- [HackTricks](https://github.com/HackTricks-wiki/hacktricks) - The pentester's playbook: technique-by-technique attack references from recon to post-exploitation. Used by pentesters as the encyclopedic reference during engagements; the donation-supported playbook the community keeps current. (free; custom content license)
- [PayloadsAllTheThings](https://github.com/swisskyrepo/PayloadsAllTheThings) - The curated payload and methodology repo — copy, paste, understand, learn. Used by pentesters grabbing working payloads fast; the community cookbook for every attack class.
- [GTFOBins](https://github.com/GTFOBins/GTFOBins.github.io) - The living Linux binary-abuse matrix; the privilege-escalation bible. Used by pentesters finding Unix binary-abuse paths for privesc; the standard free lookup red teams quote. (GPL-3.0)
- [LOLBAS](https://github.com/LOLBAS-Project/LOLBAS) - The Windows counterpart to GTFOBins: living-off-the-land binaries and scripts. Used by red teamers planning fileless attacks and defenders writing detections for them. (GPL-3.0)
- [OWASP WSTG](https://owasp.org/www-project-web-security-testing-guide/) - The Web Security Testing Guide — the methodology standard for thorough assessments. Used by auditors and pentesters as the canonical assessment methodology; the free OWASP guide commercial audits map to.
- [OWASP API Security Project](https://owasp.org/projects/api-security-project) - The API Top 10 and testing methodology: the standard reference for assessing API authentication, authorization and abuse cases. Used by API auditors mapping coverage against the canonical API risk list; the free OWASP guide every API assessment references. (CC BY-SA)
- [OWASP Cheat Sheet Series](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/) - Concise, developer-friendly defensive guidance per topic; the fix side of every finding. Used by developers building defenses correctly; the free OWASP guidance engineers link in code reviews.
- [Professor Messer](https://www.professormesser.com) - Free complete video courses for Security+, Network+ and CySA+; the default free cert-prep resource. Used by certification students as the default free alternative to paid bootcamps.
- [pwn.college](https://pwn.college) - ASU's free hands-on curriculum from Linux basics to kernel exploitation, with dojo challenges. Used by students mastering binary exploitation; Arizona State's free curriculum from basics to kernel pwn.
- [IppSec](https://ippsec.rocks) - The legendary HTB walkthrough archive; watch a master work through every box, searchable by technique. Used by HTB learners studying full walkthroughs; the free video walkthroughs that taught a generation of pentesters.
- [LiveOverflow](https://www.youtube.com/@LiveOverflow) - Deep-dive security videos that explain *why* things break — the channel every hacker watched. Used by learners building deep intuition for exploitation; the free video series on hacking fundamentals.
- [Hacker101](https://www.hacker101.com/) - HackerOne's free web-security course: video lessons plus an always-on CTF for bug-bounty practice. Used by aspiring bounty hunters starting on HackerOne; the free training with bounties attached.
