- [Hacksplaining](https://www.hacksplaining.com/) - Interactive security lessons for developers: exploit each vulnerability class, then patch it yourself. Used by developers learning web vulns interactively; the free lesson series engineering teams assign.
- [Roppers Academy](https://www.hoppersroppers.org/) - Free structured courses from computing fundamentals to security — the patient beginner's path. Used by beginners building computing and security fundamentals; the free structured coursework.
- [SEED Labs](https://seedsecuritylabs.org/) - The university-grade lab curriculum: hundreds of hands-on exercises for every security course topic. Used by university courses teaching hands-on security; the free open labs adopted by hundreds of classes.
- [Infosec Reference](https://github.com/rmusser01/Infosec_Reference) - The giant information-security reference: tools, papers and guides in one place. Used by learners and practitioners organizing knowledge; vendor-marketing-free. (curated list)
- [CTF Field Guide](https://trailofbits.github.io/ctf/) - Trail of Bits' field guide to capture-the-flag: from fundamentals to finals. Used by CTF newcomers learning the game; the free guide from the CTF community. (CC-BY-4.0)
- [Network Segmentation Cheat Sheet](https://github.com/sergiomarotco/Network-segmentation-cheat-sheet) - Practical segmentation architecture: VLANs, zones and rules that survive audits. Used by network architects designing defensible segmentation; the free practical reference. (Apache-2.0)
- [OWASP MSTG](https://github.com/OWASP/owasp-mstg) - The Mobile Security Testing Guide: the canonical methodology for mobile app assessments. Used by mobile security testers following the standard methodology; the OWASP mobile testing guide commercial mobile audits follow. (CC-BY-SA-4.0)
- [Android Security Awesome](https://github.com/ashishb/android-security-awesome) - Curated Android security research: tools, papers and training. Used by mobile security researchers assembling toolkits. (Apache-2.0, curated list)
- [Bug Bounty Hunter](https://www.bugbountyhunter.com/) - Free bug-bounty training platform: real methodology and lab targets. Used by aspiring hunters learning methodology; the free resource for bounty skill-building. (free service)
- [SecuriBench Micro](https://github.com/too4words/securibench-micro/) - The Stanford benchmark suite for security-analysis tools. Used by academic researchers benchmarking static analysis; Stanford's free test suite for tool evaluation. (Apache-2.0)
- [Cyber Security Base](https://cybersecuritybase.github.io/) - Free course series by Aalto University and F-Secure; from beginner to blue team. Used by students taking university-level security courses; the free MOOC series from F-Secure and Helsinki. (free course)
- [$36k Google App Engine RCE](https://sites.google.com/site/testsitehacking/-36k-google-app-engine-rce) - Ezequiel Pereira's account of a $36k Google App Engine remote code execution bounty. Used by bug bounty hunters; a high-profile rewarded disclosure narrative.
- [$7.5k Google services mix-up](https://sites.google.com/site/testsitehacking/-7-5k-Google-services-mix-up) - Write-up of a $7.5k Google services mix-up bug. Used by bug bounty hunters; a concrete example of chained logic flaws.
- [.NET Roulette: Exploiting Insecure Deserialization in Telerik UI](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=--6PiuvBGAU) - A conference talk demonstrating exploitation of insecure deserialization in Telerik UI for .NET applications. Used by red teamers and .NET developers; a focused video walkthrough of the attack.
- [0Day Labs](https://blog.0daylabs.com/) - Blog with bug bounty writeups and security challenges. Used by aspiring bug bounty hunters; a practical learning write-up resource.
- [102 Deep Dive in the Dark Web OSINT Style Kirby Plessas](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fzd3zkAI_o4) - A recorded talk on deep dive dark web OSINT by Kirby Plessas. Used by OSINT investigators; a video learning resource on dark web research.
- [@cure53berlin](https://twitter.com/cure53berlin) - Twitter account of Cure53, a German cybersecurity research firm. Used by web security researchers; a source of expert security commentary.
- [@filedescriptor](https://twitter.com/filedescriptor) - Twitter account of an active penetration tester who shares useful articles. Used by web security practitioners; a source of practical security insights.
- [@garethheyes](https://twitter.com/garethheyes) - Twitter account of an English web security researcher sharing exploitation insights. Used by bug bounty hunters; a source of web security commentary.
- [@h3xstream](https://twitter.com/h3xstream/) - Twitter account of a security researcher focused on web security, crypto, and static analysis. Used by security engineers; a source of technical security commentary.
- [@HackwithGitHub](https://twitter.com/HackwithGithub) - Twitter initiative showcasing open-source hacking tools for hackers and pentesters. Used by pentesters; a discovery feed for offensive tooling.
- [@hasegawayosuke](https://twitter.com/hasegawayosuke) - Twitter account of a Japanese JavaScript security researcher. Used by web security researchers; a source of browser and JS security insights.
- [@kinugawamasato](https://twitter.com/kinugawamasato) - Twitter account of a Japanese web penetration tester sharing research. Used by web security practitioners; a source of offensive web security commentary.

- [XSS without parentheses and semi-colons](https://portswigger.net/blog/xss-without-parentheses-and-semi-colons) - A PortSwigger research post demonstrating XSS payloads without parentheses or semicolons. Used by bug-bounty hunters; a classic payload-crafting reference.
- [XSS-Auditor — the protector of unprotected and the deceiver of protected.](https://medium.com/bugbountywriteup/xss-auditor-the-protector-of-unprotected-f900a5e15b7b) - An analysis of the XSS-Auditor browser filter, showing how it protects some pages and deceives others. Used by web security learners; a deep technical write-up.
- [XSS.png](https://github.com/LucaBongiorni/XSS.png) - A visual XSS cheat sheet presented as an image for quick reference. Used by testers; a compact at-a-glance payload reference.
- [XXE](https://phonexicum.github.io/infosec/xxe.html) - A short write-up explaining XML external entity injection. Used by learners; a concise introduction to XXE attacks.
- [XXE in WeChat Pay Sdk ( WeChat leave a backdoor on merchant websites)](https://seclists.org/fulldisclosure/2018/Jul/3) - A full-disclosure post on an XXE in the WeChat Pay SDK. Used by security researchers; a concrete real-world disclosure.
- [XXE OOB extracting via HTTP+FTP using single opened port](https://skavans.ru/en/2017/12/02/xxe-oob-extracting-via-httpftp-using-single-opened-port/) - A write-up on XXE out-of-band extraction via HTTP and FTP using a single opened port. Used by penetration testers; a practical real-world technique.
- [Zen Rails Security Checklist](https://github.com/brunofacca/zen-rails-security-checklist) - A checklist of Rails security best practices maintained as an open-source repository. Used by Rails developers and reviewers; a practical hardening checklist. (MIT)
- [Особенности Safari в client-side атаках](https://bo0om.ru/safari-client-side) - A write-up on Safari-specific behaviors in client-side attacks. Used by web security researchers; a deep technical reference.

## 🎯 The Proving Grounds — CTF Practice, Labs & Hacking Training
Intro: Hack legally — wargames, CTF platforms and deliberately-vulnerable apps to sharpen your skills.

- [Hack The Box](https://www.hackthebox.com) - The platform for ranked machines, pro labs and active-dir ranges; the industry's proving ground. Used by aspiring OSCP candidates and professional pentesters keeping sharp; the leading freemium training range. (free tier)
- [TryHackMe](https://tryhackme.com) - Guided rooms from your first command to advanced red/blue paths — the friendliest on-ramp. Used by newcomers learning security step by step; the guided-labs platform that made entry-level training free. (free tier)
- [picoCTF](https://picoctf.org) - CMU's beginner-friendly CTF — the best first flag anyone can capture. Used by high-school and university students in CMU's free beginner CTF; the on-ramp for countless security careers.
- [OverTheWire](https://overthewire.org) - Bandit and friends: learn Linux, networking and protocols through SSH wargames. Used by learners mastering Linux and security fundamentals; the classic free wargames every starter list recommends.
- [VulnHub](https://www.vulnhub.com) - Downloadable vulnerable VMs; build and break your own home lab offline. Used by students building offline home labs; the free vulnerable-VM library that replaced expensive lab hardware.
- [CTFtime](https://ctftime.org) - The global CTF calendar, team rankings and writeup index — the hub of the sport. Used by CTF teams tracking events and rankings worldwide; the free calendar of every serious competition.
- [OWASP Juice Shop](https://github.com/juice-shop/juice-shop) - The modern deliberately-vulnerable app with gamified challenges covering the whole OWASP spectrum. Used in corporate trainings and university courses; the de facto standard vulnerable app for web security practice.
- [DVWA](https://github.com/digininja/DVWA) - The classic Damn Vulnerable Web Application for safely learning injection basics. Used by instructors teaching web vulnerabilities for years; the classic PHP vulnerable app in every classroom lab. (GPL-3.0)
- [WebGoat](https://github.com/WebGoat/WebGoat) - OWASP's teaching app: guided lessons with a solution walkthrough for each vulnerability class. Used by Java teams and instructors learning web exploitation; the OWASP teaching app with lessons built in. (GPL-2.0)
- [Metasploitable 3](https://github.com/rapid7/metasploitable3) - Rapid7's purpose-built vulnerable target VM; the canonical Metasploit practice range. Used by pentest trainees practicing Metasploit workflows; the Rapid7-blessed free practice target.
- [CTFd](https://github.com/CTFd/CTFd) - The CTF platform that powers thousands of events; self-host your own competition. Used by CTF organizers self-hosting competitions; the open-source platform behind thousands of events.
- [pwntools](https://github.com/Gallopsled/pwntools) - The CTF exploit-development library: from sockets to ROP chains, pwn everything. Used by CTF players and exploit developers writing exploits fast; the standard Python library for pwn challenges.
- [Cryptopals](https://cryptopals.com) - The legendary crypto challenges; finish them and AES is no longer magic. Used by engineers learning real-world cryptography; the free challenge set that trained a generation of crypto implementers.
- [Gandalf](https://gandalf.lakera.ai) - Lakera's free prompt-injection game; learn AI-security attacks hands-on, level by level. Used by teams learning prompt injection hands-on; Lakera's free AI-security game.
- [bWAPP](http://www.itsecgames.com/) - The "buggy web application" with 100+ vulnerability flavors — an old-school playground that still teaches. Used by instructors demonstrating 100+ web bug classes; the free buggy app for classroom labs.
- [OWASP Mutillidae II](https://github.com/webpwnized/mutillidae) - Deliberately vulnerable app that re-creates every OWASP Top 10 issue, resettable per install. Used by teachers demonstrating nearly every OWASP vulnerability; the deliberately vulnerable training app. (GPL-3.0)
- [Root Me](https://www.root-me.org/) - Hundreds of real-world hacking challenges across dozens of categories, from web to stego. Used by French-speaking and international learners; the free platform with hundreds of hands-on challenges. (free tier)
- [Google Gruyere](http://google-gruyere.appspot.com/) - Google's cheese-themed vulnerable web app: exploit XSS, XSRF and information disclosure in the cloud. Used by beginners hunting their first web bugs; Google's free vulnerable-app tutorial.
- [GOAD](https://github.com/Orange-Cyberdefense/GOAD) - Game of Active Directory: a three-forest lab purpose-built for safely practicing AD attack techniques. Used by AD red-team trainees practicing real attack paths; the free Game of Active Directory lab commercial courses emulate. (GPL-3.0)
- [CloudGoat](https://github.com/RhinoSecurityLabs/cloudgoat) - Rhino Security's "vulnerable by design" AWS scenarios — deploy, attack, tear down. Used by cloud security learners practicing AWS attacks safely; Rhino Security's free vulnerable-by-design deployment.
- [CryptoHack](https://cryptohack.org/) - Learn modern cryptography by breaking it: challenges from classical ciphers to ECC. Used by learners leveling up applied cryptography; the free interactive challenge platform.
- [Hackxor](https://hackxor.net/) - A realistic web-app hacking game with a story arc — WebGoat with sharper teeth. Used by aspiring web hackers playing a realistic first-person hacking game; the free browser-based range.
- [WackoPicko](https://github.com/adamdoupe/WackoPicko) - The vulnerable app built for testing vulnerability scanners — a frozen-by-design calibration target for your tooling. Used by researchers and instructors demonstrating classic web flaws; the free academic vulnerable social network.
- [CyberDefenders](https://cyberdefenders.org/) - Blue-team CTF platform: investigate realistic incidents with the tools a SOC actually uses. Used by aspiring DFIR analysts practicing blue-team cases; the free DFIR CTF platform. (free tier)
- [LetsDefend](https://letsdefend.io/) - Browser-based SOC simulator: alert queues, playbooks and IR challenges mapped to MITRE ATT&CK. Used by aspiring SOC analysts simulating alert triage; the freemium blue-team range with realistic cases. (free tier)
- [pwnable.kr](https://pwnable.kr/) - The classic system-hacking wargame: exploit your way from userland to kernel. Used by exploit developers practicing binary pwn; the classic free challenge server.
- [Hack This Site](https://www.hackthissite.org/) - The community wargames veteran: missions, articles and forums since the early 2000s. Used by a generation of learners starting out; the veteran free training community.
- [OWASP NodeGoat](https://github.com/OWASP/NodeGoat) - The Node.js vulnerable-by-design app: the Top 10 in a controllable lab. Used by Node.js teams learning the OWASP Top 10 in their stack; the OWASP training app for JavaScript. (Apache-2.0)
- [Vulhub](https://github.com/vulhub/vulhub) - Dockerized vulnerable environments: spin up a CVE, test, tear down. Used by learners spinning up vulnerable environments with Docker; the free compose-file library of real CVE setups. (MIT)
- [Pentest-Ground](https://pentest-ground.com/) - Hosted practice playground of intentionally vulnerable apps for security testing. Used by testers practicing against free lab environments; the training range from the Netsparker lineage. (free service)
- [testasp.vulnweb.com](http://testasp.vulnweb.com/) - The ASP demo target for scanner testing. Used by scanner developers and QA validating detection; Acunetix's free intentionally-vulnerable classic ASP test site. (free service)
- [testaspnet.vulnweb.com](http://testaspnet.vulnweb.com/) - The ASP.NET demo target for scanner testing. Used by scanner developers and QA validating detection; Acunetix's free intentionally-vulnerable ASP.NET test site. (free service)
- [Zero Bank](http://zero.webappsecurity.com/) - Demo banking site for web security exercises. Used by learners practicing web vulns on a simulated banking app; the free demo target behind Invicti training material. (free service)
- [Altoro Mutual](http://demo.testfire.net/) - The classic demo bank: legal target for appsec training and tool demos. Used by security teams demoing scanners on a safe target; the classic IBM testfire-style demo banking site. (free service)
- [Firing Range](https://public-firing-range.appspot.com/) - Google's XSS testbed: the original scanner benchmarking target. Used by XSS researchers testing payloads systematically; Google's free testbed for cross-site scripting classes. (Apache-2.0)
- [XSS Game](https://xss-game.appspot.com/) - Google's XSS tutorial game: six levels from context basics to DOM tricks. Used by beginners learning XSS exploitation; Google's free reverse-engineering-free XSS CTF. (free service)
- [lfi-labs](https://github.com/paralax/lfi-labs) - LFI/RFI/commix practice pages: one flaw family, many variants. Used by learners practicing local file inclusion safely; the free PHP lab for LFI classes. (MIT)
- [Arizona Cyber Warfare Range](http://azcwr.org/az-cyber-warfare-ranges) - Free live-fire ranges for defenders and researchers. Used by learners attacking live-fire lab environments; the free volunteer-run range. (free service)
- [0l4bs](https://github.com/tegal1337/0l4bs) - XSS practice labs with progressive difficulty. Used by students practicing SQLi, XSS and command injection individually; the free isolated labs per bug class. (GPL-3.0)
- [DSVW](https://github.com/stamparm/DSVW) - Damn Small Vulnerable Web: the Top 10 in ~100 lines of Python. Used by trainers demonstrating web flaws with a single Python file; the deliberately small vulnerable app. (public domain)
- [DVHMA](https://github.com/logicalhacking/DVHMA) - The Damn Vulnerable Hybrid Mobile App: Android+iOS appsec in one codebase. Used by mobile security students practicing hybrid-app flaws; the vulnerable hybrid mobile app lab. (Apache-2.0)
- [DVIA-v2](https://github.com/prateek147/DVIA-v2) - Damn Vulnerable iOS App v2: the mobile testing curriculum as an app. Used by iOS security learners practicing on Swift; the free deliberately vulnerable iOS app. (MIT)
- [DVWS (Web Sockets)](https://github.com/interference-security/DVWS) - The vulnerable WebSocket app from Interference Security. Used by students practicing WebSocket-specific attacks; the vulnerable websockets lab. (MIT)
- [damnvulnerable.me](https://github.com/skepticfx/damnvulnerable.me) - A modern vulnerable webapp for the OWASP Top 10 era. Used by mobile security students learning both Android and iOS flaws; the free vulnerable mobile apps. (MIT)
- [Java Vulnerable Lab](https://github.com/CSPF-Founder/JavaVulnerableLab) - The Java vulnerable web application from the CSPF crew. Used by Java developers learning exploitable patterns; the free vulnerable Java application. (GPL-2.0)
- [SentinelTestbed](https://github.com/dobin/SentinelTestbed) - A testbed app for evaluating security instrumentation. Used by engineers testing detection content against a deliberately vulnerable host; the free detection-lab target. (GPL-3.0)
- [SocketToMe](https://digi.ninja/projects/sockettome.php) - Robin Wood's WebSocket testing challenge app. Used by learners practicing network socket exploitation; the free deliberately vulnerable service. (free service)
- [techlogia Lab](https://techlogia.de/en/lab) - Free browser-based hacking lab for security training. Used by students practicing end-to-end web exploitation; the free vulnerable lab environment. (CC-BY-4.0)
- [Vulnerable Node](https://github.com/cr0hn/vulnerable-node) - The Node.js vulnerable application for testing analysis tools. Used by Node.js teams auditing a realistic vulnerable app; the free Express-based training target. (MIT-style)
- [CryptOMG](https://github.com/SpiderLabs/CryptOMG) - Cryptographic flaw testbed from SpiderLabs. Used by crypto learners attacking flawed implementations; the free crypto-focused challenge set. (GPL-3.0)
- [AzureGoat](https://github.com/ine-labs/AzureGoat) - The vulnerable-by-design Azure cloud lab. Used by cloud security learners practicing Azure attacks; the free vulnerable-by-design Azure lab. (MIT)
- [Crackmes.one](https://crackmes.one/) - The reverse-engineering challenge community: thousands of crackmes, all legal. Used by reverse-engineering learners practicing on thousands of binaries; the free community crackme archive. (free service)
- [HEVD](https://github.com/hacksysteam/HackSysExtremeVulnerableDriver) - The Windows kernel-driver training target for exploit development. Used by kernel exploit developers learning Windows driver exploitation; the free HackSys training driver with documented vulns. (GPL-3.0)
- [Microcorruption](https://microcorruption.com/) - Embedded-security CTF: reverse the lock firmware, level by level. Used by embedded security learners solving lock-firmware puzzles; the free embedded CTF from Matasano lineage. (free service)
- [MASTG Crackmes](https://github.com/OWASP/mastg/tree/master/Crackmes) - The mobile RE challenges companion to the OWASP MASTG. Used by mobile security learners practicing the OWASP MASTG on real targets; the free crackme suite. (CC-BY-SA-4.0)
- [Reversing.kr](http://reversing.kr) - The classic Korean reverse-engineering challenge site. Used by RE learners practicing Korean-style binary challenges; the classic free challenge site. (free service)
- [Vulnserver](http://www.thegreycorner.com/2010/12/introducing-vulnserver.html) - The TCP vulnerable server for exploit-dev practice. Used by exploit developers practicing buffer overflows and ROP; the standard free TCP exploitation trainer. (free service)
- [CTFlearn](https://ctflearn.com/) - The beginner-friendly CTF platform with a permanent challenge set. Used by absolute beginners solving first flags; the free beginner-friendly CTF platform. (free service)
- [Hackertest.net](http://www.hackertest.net/) - The 20-level hacking simulation for web basics. Used by beginners playing a level-by-level web puzzle; the veteran free challenge series. (free service)
- [HackThis](https://www.hackthis.co.uk/) - 50+ levels of web security challenges, from basics to advanced. Used by beginners practicing across 50+ mission levels; the free UK challenge community. (free service)
- [MysteryTwister C3](https://www.mysterytwisterc3.org/en/) - The international cryptography challenge: from Caesar to quantum. Used by crypto enthusiasts solving graded cipher challenges; the free crypto CTF with legendary pedigree. (free service)
- [RingZer0 CTF](https://ringzer0ctf.com/) - Hundreds of challenges across web, crypto, forensics and reversing. Used by learners solving web, crypto and coding challenges; the free online CTF platform. (free service)
- [SmashTheStack](http://smashthestack.org/) - The wargames network: shellcoding, exploitation and logic puzzles. Used by learners playing classic wargames like Amateria and Blowfish; the veteran free wargame community. (free service)
- [W3Challs](https://w3challs.com) - Pentest training challenges with a ranking system. Used by learners practicing web, crypto and stego; the free challenge platform. (free service)
- [WeChall](http://www.wechall.net/) - The challenge-site aggregator: one profile across all major wargames. Used by CTF players tracking ranks across challenge sites; the free aggregator for wargame rankings. (free service)
- [DIVA Android](https://github.com/payatu/diva-android) - Damn Insecure and Vulnerable App: the Android training classic. Used by mobile security students learning Android flaw classes; the classic deliberately vulnerable Android app. (GPL-3.0)
- [ExploitMe Mobile](http://securitycompass.github.io/AndroidLabs/index.html) - The SecurityCompass mobile labs. Used by mobile security learners practicing iPhone and Android labs; the free mobile range. (free service)
- [OWASP iGoat](https://wiki.owasp.org/index.php/OWASP_iGoat_Tool_Project) - The iOS learning app from the OWASP mobile family. Used by iOS security learners practicing on Objective-C; the OWASP deliberately vulnerable iOS app. (wiki project)
- [APTSimulator](https://github.com/NextronSystems/APTSimulator) - Makes a Windows host look compromised so you can test detections safely. Used by blue teams generating APT-style artifacts for detection testing; the free Windows simulator for purple exercises. (MIT)

- [Splunk Attack Range](https://github.com/splunk/attack_range) - Builds instrumented detection labs: attack, collect and test in one workflow. Used by detection engineers building full attack/defend labs in the cloud; the free Splunk range that emulates commercial cyber ranges. (Apache-2.0)
- [pwn-scenarios](https://github.com/AlaBYahya/pwn-scenarios) - 36,000+ structured pentest/bug-bounty playbooks (condition, numbered steps with expected observations, impact, remediation) grounded in disclosed reports from HackerOne, GHSA, Exploit-DB, HTB/THM/CTF writeups, auto-collected daily. Used by bounty hunters studying how real reports were structured; the largest public report-pattern dataset. (MIT code, CC BY 4.0 data)
- [AEVisionLab](https://github.com/yeoant/AEVisionLab) - Automotive Ethernet test platform using off-the-shelf ECUs for sniffing, interception, injection, and fuzzing. Used by vehicle security researchers; an open testbed.
- [alert(1) to win](https://alf.nu/alert1) - Series of browser-based XSS challenges with escalating difficulty. Used by web security learners; a classic hands-on XSS practice site. (free, proprietary)
- [Cooja Simulator](https://github.com/contiki-ng/cooja) - Contiki OS network simulator for emulating resource-constrained IoT devices and implementing attack scenarios. Used by IoT researchers; a standard simulation testbed. (BSD-3-Clause)
- [FLAWS](http://flaws.cloud/) - Amazon AWS CTF challenge covering cloud misconfigurations and privilege escalation. Used by cloud security learners; a well-known hands-on AWS practice range. (free, proprietary)
- [GRFICSv3](https://github.com/Fortiphyd/GRFICSv3) - Free and open-source OT security lab with realistic networking and 3D process simulation, featuring PLCs, HMIs, engineering workstations, and Kali. Used by ICS defenders and students; a safe, realistic practice range for industrial attacks. (GPL-3.0)
- [H5SC](https://github.com/cure53/H5SC) - A collection of HTML5-based XSS test vectors and cheat sheets. Used by security testers; a practical payload reference for XSS testing. (MPL-2.0)
- [Hacker101 CTF](https://ctf.hacker101.com/) - HackerOne's free CTF platform with real-world web security challenges; used by aspiring bug hunters to practice vulnerability hunting. (free tier)
- [HackSimulator](https://chatgpt.com/g/g-jnT7HlNeK-hacksimulator) - A ChatGPT-based GPT acting as a hacking CTF that quizzes users on their experience. Used by learners and hobbyists; an interactive capture-the-flag style challenge. (free tier)
- [HTB SATCOM Security Essentials](https://ctf.hackthebox.com/pack/satcom-security-essentials) - Hands-on CTF pack covering orbital mechanics, RF signal analysis, CCSDS protocols, DVB-S2 demodulation, and satellite firmware reverse engineering. Used by space security learners; a themed challenge set for SATCOM skills. (free tier)
- [ICSim (Instrument Cluster Simulator)](https://github.com/zombieCraig/ICSim) - CAN bus simulator emulating a vehicle instrument cluster for safe, legal testing of CAN injection, replay, and reverse engineering techniques. Used by automotive security researchers; a practical lab for learning vehicle bus attacks. (GPL-3.0)
- [IDS-Defender](https://github.com/salhabheba-cyber/IDS-Defender) - Educational IDS/IPS deployment project using Snort 3 and Suricata on Kali Linux with Hydra attack simulation. Used by learners; a hands-on lab for practicing detection deployment. (MIT)
- [Invoke-AtomicRedTeam](https://github.com/redcanaryco/invoke-atomicredteam) - PowerShell module for executing Atomic Red Team tests, automating reproducible execution of 1,000+ ATT&CK techniques. Used by purple teams and detection engineers; the standard ATT&CK test runner. (MIT)
- [Kali Autopilot](https://www.kali.org/tools/kali-autopilot/) - Kali Linux tool for developing automatic attack scripts for red and purple teaming. Used by red and purple teamers; a scripted attack-automation utility in the Kali toolkit.
- [LearnPwn](https://github.com/Adamkadaban/LearnPwn) - Sample problems and solutions for learning binary exploitation with progressive difficulty. Used by security students and CTF players; a hands-on practice resource.
- [ModbusPal](https://esf.eurotech.com/v5.2.0/docs/modbus-application-in-kura-wires) - Modbus slave simulator for creating virtual Modbus devices for security testing, protocol learning, and attack validation. Used by ICS testers and students; a convenient way to stand up realistic Modbus targets.
- [OopsSec Store](https://github.com/kOaDT/oss-oopssec-store) - Intentionally vulnerable e-commerce application built with Next.js for practicing web security. Used by security learners; a deliberately insecure training target. (MIT)
- [Open Redirect](https://github.com/yogsec/Open-Redirect) - An educational web application that demonstrates open redirect vulnerabilities in a safe lab setting. Used by students and trainers; a focused practice target for redirect flaws. (MIT)
- [Open5GS](https://open5gs.org/) - Open-source project for emulating 4G and 5G core network functions, widely used as a test environment for security research and fuzzing. Used by telecom researchers; a standard lab core.
- [PureEdgeSim](https://github.com/CharafeddineMechalikh/PureEdgeSim) - Simulation tool for edge and IoT computing environments used to evaluate security models and attack scenarios. Used by academic researchers; a flexible modeling framework. (GPL-3.0)
- [RangeForce Community](https://www.rangeforce.com/) - Free cloud-based community SOC simulation labs using Splunk, Wireshark, and Suricata; used by defenders to practice hands-on skills. (free tier)
- [SamuraiWTF](https://github.com/SamuraiWTF/samuraiwtf) - Web application penetration testing environment for practice. Used by pentesters and learners; a hands-on lab for web app testing. (LGPL-3.0)
- [SELinux Game](https://selinuxgame.org/) - Interactive game for learning SELinux by solving puzzles and demonstrating skill. Used by Linux administrators; a hands-on SELinux practice platform.
- [SentinelX](https://pypi.org/project/sentinelx/) - Modular CLI framework for authorized security testing, defensive validation, and purple team simulations on Kali Linux. Used by purple teamers and defenders; a scriptable simulation and validation toolkit.
- [SimRun](https://github.com/IBM/simrun) - IBM's attack simulation platform for detection engineering that detonates attacks and verifies expected SIEM alerts fire. Used by detection engineers and purple teams; a practical detection-validation framework. (Apache-2.0)
- [Stratus Red Team](https://stratus-red-team.cloud/) - Datadog's cloud-focused attack simulation tool enabling granular detonation of offensive techniques in AWS and other clouds. Used by cloud security and purple teams; an open cloud attack emulation tool. (free tier)
- [TTPForge](https://github.com/facebookincubator/TTPForge) - Meta's cyber attack simulation platform built by red, blue, and purple teams to automate attacker TTPs. Used by purple teams and detection engineers; an open-source adversary emulation framework. (MIT)
- [UERANSIM](https://github.com/aligungr/UERANSIM) - Open-source 5G UE and RAN simulator commonly used for fuzzing NAS and RRC protocols in telecom security research. Used by telecom researchers; a standard testbed component. (AGPL-3.0)
- [Vulnerable Login](https://github.com/yogsec/Vulnerable-Login) - A deliberately vulnerable login form built for security education and hands-on training. Used by learners and instructors; a simple practice target for authentication flaws. (MIT)
- [Vulnerable Shopping Cart](https://github.com/yogsec/Vulnerable-shopping-Cart) - Deliberately insecure e-commerce application for security practice. Used by learners and testers; a safe target for web exploitation exercises. (MIT)
- [VWGen](https://github.com/qazbnm456/VWGen) - Generator of vulnerable web applications for practice. Used by security learners and trainers; a handy lab-building utility.
- [WebVerse Labs](https://webverselabs-pro.com/) - Hands-on hacking labs, challenges, learning paths, and web ranges. Used by learners and practitioners; a practice platform for offensive skills.
- [XSS Challenges](http://xss-quiz.int21h.jp/) - Series of XSS challenges hosted by yamagata21. Used by web security learners; a long-standing hands-on XSS practice site. (free, proprietary)
- [XSS Labs](https://github.com/yogsec/xss-labs) - XSS lab exercises for learning web application security. Used by learners; a hands-on environment for practicing cross-site scripting. (NOASSERTION)

## 🧰 The Workshop — Utilities & Swiss-Army Tools
Intro: The everyday carry — decoding, proxying, dissecting and transforming anything the job throws at you.

- [CyberChef](https://github.com/gchq/CyberChef) - GCHQ's web Swiss-army knife: 400+ operations for decoding, transforming and analyzing data, all client-side. Used by analysts daily for decoding, crypto and data wrangling; GCHQ's free web tool that replaces a shelf of utilities.
- [Wireshark](https://www.wireshark.org) - The packet analyzer everyone knows; deep protocol visibility for troubleshooting and forensics. Used by network engineers and DFIR analysts everywhere; the standard free packet analyzer commercial tools still get compared to. (GPL-2.0)
- [Ghidra](https://github.com/NationalSecurityAgency/ghidra) - The NSA's open-source reverse-engineering suite; disassembly, decompilation and scripting for free. Used by reverse engineers analyzing binaries of any architecture; the NSA's free suite that stands in for IDA Pro and Binary Ninja.
- [jadx](https://github.com/skylot/jadx) - The standard Java/Android decompiler: dex-to-java source recovery with both GUI and CLI — the first stop for APK and JAR analysis. Used by Android reverse engineers decompiling DEX to readable Java; the free decompiler every APK analysis starts with. (Apache-2.0)
- [Apktool](https://github.com/iBotPeaches/Apktool) - Disassemble and rebuild Android APKs: decode resources to near-original form, patch smali, repackage and sign — the Android RE classic. Used by mobile pentesters decoding Android resources and smali; the standard free APK disassembler. (Apache-2.0)
- [Cutter](https://github.com/rizinorg/cutter) - Free and open reverse-engineering platform powered by rizin: graph disassembly, decompiler views and debugging in a polished desktop UI. Used by RE learners driving Ghidra through a friendlier GUI; the free reverse-engineering IDE. (GPL-3.0)
- [tcpdump](https://www.tcpdump.org) - The command-line packet capture classic; quick looks and long captures where Wireshark won't fit. Used by admins capturing packets on servers without GUIs; the classic free CLI sniffer preinstalled everywhere.
- [mitmproxy](https://github.com/mitmproxy/mitmproxy) - Interactive HTTPS interception for debugging and testing your own apps; scriptable as a library. Used by developers and pentesters intercepting HTTPS traffic programmatically; the free alternative to Charles Proxy and Fiddler.
- [Frida](https://github.com/frida/frida) - Inject JavaScript into running processes; dynamic instrumentation for mobile, desktop and IoT. Used by mobile pentesters and researchers injecting logic into running processes; the dynamic-instrumentation standard. (GPL-2.0 / wxWindows dual)
- [Interactsh](https://github.com/projectdiscovery/interactsh) - Out-of-band interaction server for detecting blind vulnerabilities (SSRF, blind RCE, blind XXE). Used by bug hunters detecting out-of-band interactions blind spots miss; ProjectDiscovery's free alternative to Burp Collaborator.
- [Ciphey](https://github.com/Ciphey/Ciphey) - Automated decoder: paste ciphertext, get plaintext plus the method used — evidence triage in seconds. Used by CTF players and analysts auto-decoding ciphertext; the free automated decoder.
- [pyWhat](https://github.com/bee-san/pyWhat) - Identify anything: hashes, keys, URLs, PII — the "what is this string?" answer. Used by analysts identifying arbitrary blobs and strings; the free 'what is this' identifier.
- [ExifTool](https://github.com/exiftool/exiftool) - The metadata reader/writer for files and images; the last word in metadata triage. Used by investigators reading and stripping file metadata; the standard free metadata tool used across forensics. (Perl Artistic/GPL)
- [Scapy](https://github.com/secdev/scapy) - Packet crafting without limits: forge, sniff, dissect and send anything — the Python network Swiss-army knife. Used by network researchers crafting packets in Python; the standard library protocol testing builds on. (GPL-2.0)
- [boofuzz](https://github.com/jtpereyda/boofuzz) - Network protocol fuzzing framework descended from Sulley — script your protocol and hardware bug hunts. Used by protocol fuzzers finding crashes in network services; the open-source fuzzing framework from the Sulley lineage. (GPL-2.0)
- [Dangerzone](https://github.com/freedomofpress/dangerzone) - Takes hostile documents and re-renders them as safe PDFs inside an isolated sandbox. Used by newsrooms converting untrusted documents into safe PDFs; the free sanitization tool for adversarial files. (AGPL-3.0)
- [Faraday](https://github.com/infobyte/faraday) - The pentest collaboration platform: import tool output, deduplicate findings and manage the whole engagement. Used by pentest teams collaborating on findings; the community edition of the commercial collaboration platform. (GPL-3.0)
- [Dradis CE](https://github.com/dradis/dradis-ce) - Collaborative pentest reporting: team notes, evidence and findings assembled into one professional report. Used by pentest teams assembling reports from tool output; the free edition of the commercial reporting platform. (GPL-2.0)
- [DefectDojo](https://github.com/DefectDojo/django-DefectDojo) - Open-source vulnerability management and AppSec platform: import findings from your whole scanner fleet, deduplicate, track remediation and map to compliance from one self-hosted app. Used by security teams running continuous vulnerability management across scanners; the free alternative to commercial ASPM platforms. (BSD-3-Clause)
- [reconmap](https://github.com/reconmap/reconmap) - Collaboration-first security-operations platform: plan engagements, run and parse recon commands, AI-assist the summaries and export client-ready reports — built for infosec teams and MSSPs. Used by pentest teams managing engagements end to end; the self-hosted alternative to commercial pentest-management SaaS.. (MIT)
- [pwndoc](https://github.com/pwndoc/pwndoc) - Offline pentest report generator: reusable finding databases, CVSS scoring and multi-language docx templates that turn engagement notes into client-ready reports. Used by pentesters producing polished client reports without cloud tooling; the free offline reporting suite for engagements. (MIT)
- [Vulnrepo](https://github.com/kac89/vulnrepo) - Free end-to-end-encrypted vulnerability report generator and repository: issue templates with CWE/CVE/MITRE mapping, scanner imports, DOCX output and Jira export. Used by pentesters assembling reports fast after engagements; the free alternative to commercial reporting suites. (Apache-2.0)
- [ATT&CK Navigator](https://github.com/mitre-attack/attack-navigator) - MITRE's matrix explorer: layer, annotate and score ATT&CK techniques for detection-coverage planning. Used by purple teams planning and visualizing coverage; the free MITRE tool for layer-based ATT&CK exploration.
- [GnuPG](https://github.com/gpg/gnupg) - The free implementation of OpenPGP: encrypt, sign and verify files and mail, everywhere. Used by privacy-conscious users for email and file encryption; the free open implementation of OpenPGP. (GPL-3.0)
- [OpenSSL](https://github.com/openssl/openssl) - The TLS library the internet runs on — and the CLI for keys, certs, CSRs and everything crypto. Used by literally everyone through the TLS it provides; the open library the commercial internet runs on.

---
- [socat](http://www.dest-unreach.org/socat/) - The relay for bidirectional data transfer between addresses: TCP, UDP, sockets, pipes. Used by sysadmins and pentesters relaying and shaping network connections; the netcat successor for serious plumbing. (GPL-2.0)
- [radare2](https://github.com/radareorg/radare2) - The scriptable reverse-engineering framework: disassemble, debug, patch, anywhere. Used by reverse engineers scripting binary analysis; the free framework that powers many commercial RE pipelines. (LGPL-3.0)
- [x64dbg](https://github.com/x64dbg/x64dbg) - The open Windows debugger for x32/x64, with a plugin ecosystem built for RE. Used by malware analysts debugging Windows binaries; the free Windows debugger that stands in for commercial debuggers. (GPL-3.0)
- [netcat](http://nc110.sourceforge.io/) - The original TCP/IP Swiss army knife: reads and writes across networks with one command. Used by everyone from sysadmins to CTF players for raw TCP/UDP plumbing. (freeware)
- [Kaitai Struct](https://github.com/kaitai-io/kaitai_struct) - Declare a binary format once, generate parsers for nine languages. Used by protocol researchers declaring binary formats as YAML; the free declarative parser generator for format RE. (MIT)
- [Hachoir](https://github.com/vstinner/hachoir) - Binary field inspection: split any file into its fields, even damaged ones. Used by researchers extracting metadata from damaged binaries; the free Python metadata extractor. (GPL-2.0)
- [binwalk](https://github.com/ReFirmLabs/binwalk) - Firmware analysis: scan, extract and identify embedded files and filesystems. Used by firmware researchers extracting embedded filesystems and images; the standard free firmware-analysis tool. (MIT)
- [Capstone](https://github.com/capstone-engine/capstone) - The disassembly framework behind countless security tools. Used by security developers embedding disassembly; the free engine behind countless RE and EDR tools. (BSD-3-Clause)
- [dnSpyEx](https://github.com/dnSpyEx/dnSpy) - The .NET debugger and decompiler, continued; edit assemblies live. Used by .NET researchers decompiling and debugging managed code; the maintained fork of the classic .NET debugger. (GPL-3.0)
- [UEFITool](https://github.com/LongSoft/UEFITool) - Parse, extract and replace UEFI firmware images. Used by firmware analysts parsing UEFI images; the free tool for BIOS/UEFI research. (BSD-2-Clause)
- [tcpreplay](https://github.com/appneta/tcpreplay) - Replay captured traffic at arbitrary speed for testing and validation. Used by engineers replaying captures at original speed; the free pcap replay tool for testing sensors. (GPL-3.0)
- [I2P](https://geti2p.net/) - The Invisible Internet Project: anonymous, self-organizing overlay network. Used by privacy-focused users running encrypted overlay networking; the free Invisible Internet Project with no commercial counterpart. (multiple open licenses)
- [Tails](https://tails.net/) - The amnesic incognito live system: no trace left on the machine. Used by journalists needing amnesic, compartmentalized computing; the free live system that leaves no trace. (GPL-3.0)
- [Hex Fiend](http://ridiculousfish.com/hexfiend/) - The fast, open-source hex editor for macOS. Used by macOS researchers editing binaries fast. (BSD-2-Clause)
- [friTap](https://github.com/fkie-cad/friTap) - Intercept TLS keys in real time for analysis; a Frida script for the lab. Used by traffic analysts extracting TLS keys from apps via Frida; the free tool for decrypting stubborn mobile traffic. (GPL-3.0)
- [angr](https://angr.io/) - The binary-analysis platform from UCSB's Shellphish: symbolic execution at scale. Used by researchers and CTF players symbolically executing binaries; the free platform for program analysis research. (BSD-2-Clause)
- [Detect It Easy](https://github.com/horsicq/Detect-It-Easy) - Packer/compiler/protection detection with a rule engine. Used by malware analysts identifying packers and compilers; the free packer-detection standard. (MIT)
- [Evan's Debugger](https://github.com/eteran/edb-debugger) - The C/C++ debugger with a RE focus: memory views built for analysis. Used by RE practitioners debugging Linux binaries in a GUI; the free Qt-based debugger. (GPL-2.0+)

- [pwndbg](https://github.com/pwndbg/pwndbg) - GDB with a exploit-dev UX: heap awareness, shortcuts and sane defaults. Used by CTF players and exploit developers making GDB pwn-friendly; the standard GDB plugin for exploitation work. (MIT)
- [ImHex](https://github.com/WerWolv/ImHex) - The hex editor with pattern language: parse formats while you view them. Used by reverse engineers parsing binaries with a pattern language; the free hex editor built for RE workflows. (GPL-2.0)
- [Brida](https://github.com/federicodotta/Brida) - The bridge between Burp and Frida: custom instrumentation in your proxy workflow. Used by mobile pentesters bridging Frida into Burp; the free companion for SSL-pinning bypass work. (MIT)

- [OCSF](https://github.com/ocsf/ocsf-schema) - The Open Cybersecurity Schema Framework: one schema for security events. Used by SIEM and security vendors normalizing telemetry; the open schema standard the industry is converging on. (Apache-2.0)
- [cybersec-toolkit](https://github.com/26zl/cybersec-toolkit) - Single-command installer for 670+ security tools with an authorization-gated MCP server, allowlist and flag-denylist execution policy and a prompt-injection output sanitizer. Used by security engineers building safe tool environments for AI agents; the least-privilege installer for agent-accessible security tooling. (MIT)
- [Netron](https://github.com/lutzroeder/netron) - Visualize ML models: the layers, weights and graph of what you deploy. Used by ML engineers inspecting model architectures; the free visualizer for neural-network files. (MIT)
- [XERJ](https://github.com/xerj-org/xerj) - Local-first search engine and MCP server in a single Rust binary: hybrid BM25 + vector search over code, docs, logs and PDFs with an Elasticsearch-compatible API — for agents and humans alike. Used by security teams giving AI agents searchable, air-gapped memory over case files and codebases; the self-hosted retrieval layer for agent workflows. (Apache-2.0)
- [PentestKit](https://github.com/therealilyas/pentest-toolkit) - Honest authorized-target CLI that checks headers, TLS, exposure (.git/.env/Actuator with soft-404 detection), cookies and CORS, and reports the four things that matter per finding: what, exact evidence, attacker gain, fix. Used by developers self-auditing before a pentest; the report-first checker. (MIT)
- [4ndr0tools userscripts](https://github.com/4ndr0666/userscripts) - A suite of Tampermonkey userscripts for security work — recon helpers, OSINT lookups, secret scanning and workflow automation straight in the browser. Used by researchers who live in the browser during recon; the swiss-army userscript collection. (MIT)
- [OLYMPUS Security](https://github.com/chiaraberti13/OLYMPUS-SECURITY) - Scope-gated security assessment platform (Argus recon, Athena planning, Hermes SARIF secret scanning, Minerva chain-of-custody evidence, Vulcan finding lifecycle) where every command validates against an explicit scope file before touching a target. Used by teams that need authorization-proof audit trails; the compliance-first assessment platform. (GPL-3.0)
- [7-Zip](https://7-zip.org/) - Open-source file archiver with AES-256 encryption support for creating encrypted archives on Windows and cross-platform. Used by everyday users; a reliable encrypted archiving utility. (LGPL-2.1)
- [AppSec Santa](https://appsecsanta.com) - Independent comparison of 129+ web application security tools across SAST, DAST, SCA and more. Used by security buyers; a free tool-selection reference. (free, proprietary)
- [Brave](https://brave.com/) - Chromium-based browser with built-in ad and tracker blocking plus fingerprinting protection enabled by default, including optional Tor-powered private windows. Used by mainstream privacy adopters; a drop-in private browser. (MPL-2.0)
- [can-utils](https://github.com/linux-can/can-utils) - Essential SocketCAN userspace utilities including candump, cansend, and cangen for capture, injection, and fuzzing. Used by automotive engineers; the standard CAN toolkit. (GPL-2.0)
- [Capstone](https://www.capstone-engine.org/) - Disassembly framework for binary analysis supporting many architectures. Used by reverse engineers and tool developers; a foundational free disassembly engine.
- [Charles](https://www.charlesproxy.com/) - HTTP proxy and monitor that lets developers view all HTTP and SSL/HTTPS traffic between their machine and the internet. Used by developers and testers; a long-standing debugging proxy with a limited free tier. (free tier)
- [charsetinspect](https://github.com/hack-all-the-things/charsetinspect) - Script inspecting multi-byte character sets for characters with user-defined properties. Used by security researchers; a niche encoding analysis utility.
- [Cheat Engine](https://github.com/cheat-engine/cheat-engine) - Industry-standard memory scanner and editor for game hacking, featuring value scanning, pointer scanning, code injection, and Lua scripting. Used by game hackers and reverse engineers; the de facto memory-editing workbench.
- [cheat-engine-linux](https://github.com/wleeaf/cheat-engine-linux) - Native C++/Qt6 reimplementation of Cheat Engine for Linux with faster first scans than the official native build. Used by Linux game hackers; a performance-focused memory scanning front end. (NOASSERTION)
- [Cryptii](https://cryptii.com/) - Web-based modular conversion, encoding, and encryption tool supporting classical and modern ciphers. Used by CTF players and analysts; a handy free browser utility.
- [csp evaluator](https://csper.io/evaluator) - Online evaluator for analyzing and scoring Content-Security-Policy headers. Used by web developers and security engineers; a quick CSP assessment utility. (free, proprietary)
- [CTF Tools](https://github.com/zardus/ctf-tools) - Setup scripts for security research tools commonly used in CTFs and binary exploitation. Used by CTF players and researchers; a convenient tooling installer. (BSD-3-Clause)
- [Cutter](https://cutter.re/) - GUI for Radare2 with advanced analysis features. Used by reverse engineers and malware analysts; a free graphical reverse-engineering front-end.
- [Dcode](https://www.dcode.fr/) - Online cipher and code identification/decoding tool covering classical ciphers, modern cryptographic challenges, and CTF tasks. Used by CTF players and puzzle solvers; a broad reference for decoding. (free, proprietary)
- [espi0n/Dockerfiles](https://github.com/espi0n/Dockerfiles) - Dockerfiles packaging various OSINT tools for easy deployment. Used by OSINT practitioners; a convenient containerized toolset.
- [Fluentd](https://www.fluentd.org/) - Log collector and forwarder gathering logs from many sources and routing them to other systems for processing. Used by platform engineers; a clean open-source log pipeline component. (Apache-2.0)
- [Frida](https://frida.re/) - Dynamic instrumentation toolkit for hooking and inspecting running processes. Used by reverse engineers and mobile security researchers; a leading free instrumentation framework.
- [Ghidra](https://ghidra-sre.org/) - Open-source software reverse engineering framework for disassembly and decompilation. Used by reverse engineers and malware analysts; a leading free RE suite.
- [GNU Radio](https://www.gnuradio.org/) - Free and open-source signal processing framework providing blocks for modulation, demodulation, filtering, and spectrum analysis with graphical flowgraphs. Used by RF engineers and SDR developers; the foundational toolkit for software radio work. (GPL-3.0)
- [Gpredict](http://gpredict.oz9aec.net/) - Real-time satellite tracking and orbit prediction software for identifying satellite passes over ground stations and accounting for Doppler shift. Used by satellite operators and SDR hobbyists; a reliable pass-planning tool. (GPL-3.0)
- [GQRX](https://gqrx.dk/) - Software defined radio receiver built on GNU Radio and Qt supporting HackRF, RTL-SDR, Airspy, and USRP hardware for AM/FM/SSB reception. Used by RF hobbyists and security researchers; a popular general-purpose SDR frontend. (GPL-3.0)
- [Inspectrum](https://github.com/miek/inspectrum) - Offline spectrum analysis tool with spectrogram visualization, zoom and pan for examining captured RF signals including Iridium bursts. Used by RF analysts and satellite researchers; a focused tool for inspecting recorded IQ data. (GPL-3.0)
- [IPObfuscator](https://github.com/OsandaMalith/IPObfuscator) - Simple tool converting an IP address to its DWORD representation. Used by pentesters; a small obfuscation helper.
- [LaTeX](https://www.latex-project.org/) - High-quality typesetting system often used to produce professional security reports. Used by technical writers and pentesters; a standard for polished document output.
- [LibreWolf](https://librewolf.net/) - Privacy-hardened Firefox fork with no telemetry, uBlock Origin pre-installed, DuckDuckGo default search, and built-in DNS-over-HTTPS. Used by privacy-conscious desktop users; a hardened daily browser. (MPL-2.0)
- [LIEF](https://lief.quarkslab.com/) - Library for parsing and modifying executable formats. Used by reverse engineers and security tool developers; a free cross-platform binary analysis library.
- [Lychee](https://github.com/lycheeverse/lychee) - Fast broken link checker that can be used to spot dead or suspicious links across sites and repositories. Used by developers and security testers; a utility with light security-adjacent value. (Apache-2.0)
- [Markdown](https://www.markdownguide.org/) - Lightweight markup language for clean, portable report writing. Used by security professionals and developers; a simple way to author readable documentation.
- [MetaDefender](https://metadefender.opswat.com/) - Multi-engine file and URL malware scanning service. Used by analysts and defenders; a convenient reputation and malware check. (free tier)
- [mosquitto_sub](https://mosquitto.org/) - MQTT client for subscribing to IoT device topics, used to test broker authentication and topic ACL enforcement. Used by IoT testers; a standard broker utility. (EPL-2.0)
- [Mullvad Browser](https://mullvad.net/en/browser) - Collaboration between Tor Project and Mullvad providing Tor Browser's anti-fingerprinting protections without requiring the Tor network. Used by privacy advocates; a strong anti-fingerprinting browser. (free, proprietary)
- [OllyDbg](http://www.ollydbg.de/) - 32-bit assembler-level debugger for Windows binaries. Used by reverse engineers and malware analysts; a classic free debugging tool. (free, proprietary)
- [OnionShare](https://onionshare.org/) - Open-source tool for securely and anonymously sharing files, hosting websites, and chatting over the Tor network via temporary onion services. Used by privacy advocates; a simple Tor-based sharing tool. (GPL-3.0)
- [PEiD](https://www.softpedia.com/get/Programming/File-Editors/PEiD.shtml) - Detects packers, cryptors, and compilers in PE executables. Used by malware analysts and reverse engineers; a classic free identification utility. (free, proprietary)
- [Privacy Badger](https://privacybadger.org/) - Automatic tracker blocker from the Electronic Frontier Foundation that learns which domains track you and blocks them without configuration. Used by privacy-conscious browsers; an EFF-backed defense. (GPL-3.0)
- [Proton Mail](https://proton.me/mail) - End-to-end encrypted email service based in Switzerland requiring no personal information to sign up. Used by privacy-conscious individuals; a leading encrypted mail provider with a free tier. (free tier)
- [ps5dbg-scanner](https://pypi.org/project/ps5dbg-scanner/) - Cross-platform Cheat Engine-style tool for PS5 built on the ps5dbg library, supporting value scanning, freeze/edit operations, and server-resident tuning. Used by console game hackers; a specialized memory scanner for PlayStation 5 research.
- [PyModbus](https://github.com/pymodbus/pymodbus) - Python Modbus library used for simulating industrial environments and crafting custom protocol packets for ICS security testing and fuzzing. Used by ICS developers and testers; a widely adopted building block for Modbus tooling.
- [QEMU](https://www.qemu.org/) - Emulation platform used with qemu-mips and qemu-mipsel to run extracted embedded binaries in controlled environments. Used by firmware analysts; the standard emulation engine. (GPL-2.0)
- [ReClass.NET](https://github.com/ReClassNET/ReClass.NET) - Memory scanner and structure dissect tool for the .NET platform with a plugin ecosystem for visualizing process memory. Used by game hackers and reverse engineers; a modern, extensible alternative to classic class-rebuilding tools. (MIT)
- [RetDec](https://retdec.com/) - Open-source decompiler for machine code across multiple architectures. Used by reverse engineers and malware analysts; a free retargetable decompiler.
- [Rizin](https://rizin.re/) - Fork of Radare2 focused on usability for binary analysis. Used by reverse engineers and malware analysts; a free reverse-engineering framework.
- [RTL-SDR](https://www.rtl-sdr.com/) - Budget-friendly SDR receiver based on the RTL2832U chipset, widely used for satellite signal reception including NOAA weather satellites and amateur radio. Used by hobbyists and RF researchers; the entry-level hardware for software-defined radio.
- [SatDump](https://github.com/SatDump/SatDump) - Satellite image decoding software supporting a wide range of weather and Earth observation satellites, processing received RF signals into imagery. Used by satellite hobbyists and researchers; a comprehensive decoder for many downlink formats. (GPL-3.0)
- [scanmem](https://github.com/scanmem/scanmem) - Linux memory scanner used for Cheat Engine-style value scanning, allowing game state manipulation via interactive commands. Used by Linux game hackers and reverse engineers; a lightweight, scriptable memory search utility. (GPL-3.0)
- [SearXNG](https://searxng.org/) - Self-hosted privacy metasearch engine aggregating results from multiple sources without profiling users. Used by privacy-conscious self-hosters; a community-maintained search front-end. (AGPL-3.0)
- [SecuriTool](https://securitool.js.org/) - Free online collection of 29 client-side web security tools including a web auditor, JWT attacker, CVE search and CSP evaluator. Used by web developers and testers; a handy browser-based toolkit. (free, proprietary)
- [Session](https://getsession.org/) - Decentralized messenger requiring no phone number or email, using onion routing for metadata protection on the Oxen network. Used by privacy-focused users; a metadata-resistant messaging option. (GPL-3.0)
- [Signal](https://signal.org/) - End-to-end encrypted private messaging with open-source clients and minimal metadata collection. Used by journalists and privacy advocates; the gold standard for private messaging. (GPL-3.0)
- [SimpleLogin](https://simplelogin.io/) - Email aliasing service creating anonymous addresses to protect your real inbox from spam and data breaches. Used by privacy-minded users; an open-source aliasing solution with a free tier. (MIT)
- [snort2lua](https://www.kali.org/tools/snort/) - Conversion utility included with Snort and Suricata that converts Snort 2.x rules and configuration to Snort 3 Lua format. Used by rule maintainers; a handy migration utility.
- [Steampipe](https://steampipe.io/) - Zero-ETL SQL tool for querying cloud APIs and services across AWS, Azure, GCP, and Kubernetes. Used by cloud and security engineers; a flexible free-tier query engine.
- [Stress-ng](https://github.com/ColinIanKing/stress-ng) - Tool that stress tests CPU, RAM, I/O, and more with a wide range of configurable stressors. Used by sysadmins and testers; a comprehensive system stress-testing utility. (GPL-2.0)
- [Tutanota](https://tutanota.com/) - German encrypted email service with open-source clients, a free tier, and post-quantum encryption. Used by privacy-focused users; a future-proof encrypted mail alternative. (GPL-3.0)
- [uBlock Origin](https://ublockorigin.com/) - Efficient wide-spectrum content blocker blocking ads, trackers, and malware domains with minimal resource usage across browsers. Used by millions of users; the most recommended content blocker. (GPL-3.0)
- [Unfurl](https://github.com/tomnomnom/unfurl) - Extracts and parses URLs and their components from text streams for recon and analysis. Used by bug bounty hunters and recon analysts; a handy pipeline utility for URL wrangling. (MIT)
- [Web Application Exploitation @ Rawsec Inventory](https://inventory.raw.pm/tools.html#title-tools-web-application-exploitation) - Rawsec inventory list of web application exploitation tools. Used by pentesters; a broad catalog of web testing tools.
- [WXtoImg](https://wxtoimgrestored.xyz/) - NOAA weather satellite image decoding software that processes APT signals received via SDR to generate weather imagery with map overlays. Used by weather satellite hobbyists; a classic tool for receiving NOAA APT broadcasts.
- [x64dbg](https://x64dbg.com/) - Open-source Windows debugger for x64 and x86 binaries. Used by reverse engineers and malware analysts; a popular free debugging tool.
- [androguard](https://github.com/androguard/androguard) - Android RE framework: DEX parsing, decompile, diffing. (Apache-2.0)
- [chisel](https://github.com/jpillora/chisel) - TCP tunnel over HTTP. (MIT)
- [GoReplay](https://github.com/probelabs/goreplay) - capture and replay real HTTP traffic at scale. (LGPL-3.0, open-core)
- [hurl](https://github.com/Orange-OpenSource/hurl) - plain-text HTTP requests with assertions. (Apache-2.0)
- [TestDisk](https://github.com/cgsecurity/testdisk) - partition-table and file recovery. (GPL-2.0)

## Related Lists

- [Awesome Security](https://github.com/sbilly/awesome-security) - Software, libraries, documents and resources spanning all of security.
- [Awesome CTF](https://github.com/apsdehal/awesome-ctf) - CTF frameworks, libraries, resources, software and tutorials.
- [Awesome OSINT](https://github.com/tracelabs/awesome-osint) - A curated collection of OSINT tools and resources maintained by Trace Labs.
- [Awesome Reverse Engineering](https://github.com/morluto/awesome-reverse-engineering) - Reverse-engineering tools and learning resources for binaries and firmware.
- [Awesome Cybersecurity Blue Team](https://github.com/fabacab/awesome-cybersecurity-blueteam) - Resources, tooling and use cases for cybersecurity blue teams.

## 🧭 Editorial Standards

Entries must be **free (or free-tier)**, **useful to professionals**, and **not dormant upstream** — tools with no upstream commit in 18+ months move to the [Legacy Vault](legacy.md) with their last-commit date, so the main list only carries tools you can rely on. Deliberately frozen practice targets (DVWA, WebGoat, Metasploitable, HEVD and friends) and published datasets stay in the main list — static artifacts are the point. Copyleft, freemium and proprietary licenses are marked inline. Archived and non-redistributable projects are excluded from the main list — the few archived classics are kept separately in legacy.md. One entry is the curator's own project, marked `(maintainer's project)`.

✔ Flagship entries' license & activity verified via GitHub API on 2026-11-01 — re-verified quarterly.

## Contributing

Found a tool that belongs in Heaven? PRs are welcome — see [CONTRIBUTING.md](CONTRIBUTING.md) for
the entry criteria (free/free-tier + maintained within the last 12 months + a one-line
"when to use it" case + license disclosed if non-permissive).

## Footnotes

⭐ **Found Heaven useful?** Starring is the fastest way to help other defenders and bug hunters find it —
and it tells us the quarterly re-verification grind is worth it.

💙 This curation — license checks, API-verified activity, dead-link sweeps and quarterly
re-verification — is supporter-funded. If this map saved you hours of tool-hunting, you can
[support it by card or PayPal](https://2mny.com/support). Every entry stays
free: no ads, no sponsored placements, no data selling — ever.
